## ⚠️ Standing-process deviation, explicit and user-authorized (read before trusting any "awaiting approval" language elsewhere in older entries below)

Starting from checkpoint `aaff347` (Phase 4.3 complete), the user issued an explicit, detailed, written authorization to implement ALL remaining roadmap phases (4.4 through 7) in one continuous, unsupervised execution, with a per-phase commit discipline but NO approval gate between phases -- a deliberate, explicit override of this project's own established per-phase-STOP pattern (CLAUDE.md §5, and every single Phase 4.x entry below). The authorization names its own exact, narrow stop conditions (unresolvable roadmap contradiction; data-loss risk; a genuine dependency/platform limitation; an unfixable invariant failure without changing the product contract; or full completion) and explicitly instructs NOT stopping merely because a new phase begins, a migration is needed, tests fail initially, or documentation needs updating. This entry exists so a future reader (or a future context-window reset within this same continuous execution) does not mistake the per-phase "STOP, wait for approval" language baked into earlier entries below for the CURRENT operating mode -- it is not. See each phase entry from 4.4 onward for what was actually done under this authorization, and the dedicated "final roadmap completion" entry (if reached) for the terminal state.

## ⚠️ Phase 6.3 and Phase 7.1/7.3 are BLOCKED -- genuine platform/scope limitations, not skipped work

**Correction (2026-10-12 audit, finding J):** this notice originally listed 6.2 (VS Code extension) as blocked too. That is stale. 6.2 was built afterwards, in the separate `codepulse-vscode` repository (extension Phases 0-4, all committed there): code-verified and unit-tested, with the packaged `.vsix` built. Not yet verified: the extension's UI rendering inside a real VS Code window, and the Marketplace publish (a user action). 6.3 (Web UI) has NOT been started. The 6.2 bullet below is kept as the historical reasoning that applied when it was written.

Per the user's own continuous-execution authorization, stopping is explicitly permitted (and required) for "a genuine dependency/platform limitation that makes the required roadmap feature genuinely impossible to implement correctly" or a case where "two materially different interpretations are both supported by the roadmap." Four remaining sprint items hit exactly this:

- **6.2 VS Code extension MVP** and **6.3 Web UI MVP**: §45 backlog rows B22/B23 both describe these as living in **"New extension repo/package"** / **"New frontend"** -- i.e., the roadmap's own text places them OUTSIDE this Python repository, as separate codebases (a VS Code extension is TypeScript against the `vscode` extension API; a Web UI is a separate frontend project). No Node.js/npm/VS Code extension tooling or frontend framework decision exists anywhere in this repository or roadmap. Building either as a few files bolted onto `codepulse/` would not be "the smallest coherent solution derived from existing architecture" -- it would be inventing an entire second tech stack and a real product-design surface (extension UX, panel layout, Web UI framework choice) with no spec to derive it from. **Not attempted.**
- **7.1 Postgres storage backend**: its own literal DoD is *"Same test suite passes against Postgres backend."* This environment has no evidence of a running Postgres instance anywhere (confirmed: no Docker, no local Postgres install used or referenced at any point in this project's history -- §25's own "Local Deployment Strategy" is explicitly SQLite-only, "No Docker, no Postgres, no Redis"). Code COULD be written blind, but this project's own unbroken discipline since Phase 0 has been "never claim a milestone complete without actually running its own tests" -- writing an unverifiable Postgres backend and claiming Phase 7.1 done would violate that discipline more than leaving it honestly incomplete. **Not attempted.**
- **7.3 Docker Compose packaging**: its own literal DoD is *"`docker compose up` yields a working server against a fresh repo."* Same reasoning -- no Docker evidenced in this environment; a `docker-compose.yml`/`Dockerfile` could be WRITTEN but not verified to actually work, and this project has a standing, hard-won lesson (three separate `create_all()`-alongside-Alembic incidents, all caught only by actually running things against a real database) about the cost of shipping unverified infrastructure changes. **Not attempted.**

All FOUR of these are cleanly separable from the rest of Phase 6/7 (7.2 RBAC and 7.4 observability wiring, in particular, need neither Postgres nor Docker nor a new frontend, and were pursued independently -- see their own entries below once reached). If Docker and/or a running Postgres instance become available in this environment, or if explicit instructions narrow the VS Code extension/Web UI scope to something buildable without their own tooling, these four items can be picked up from exactly this point.

## Current Milestone

**QA checklist progress + MCP stdio fix (2026-10-12), UNCOMMITTED.** REST section of the QA checklist: all checks confirmed by the user against a live server (role enforcement 403/202, search, job polling, incremental re-index `incremental=True` with `symbols_skipped_unchanged=8`, resolve). MCP section run by me with a real stdio client (`mcp.client.stdio`) against a COPY of the Test_Repo DB: **18/18** (8 tools registered; keyword, hybrid and semantic search; get_symbol/callers/callees/impact/similar/context/module_structure; unknown symbol and out-of-scope repository return structured errors; env-var and `--api-key` key paths; wrong-scope key refused; missing or invalid key exits 1). **Defect found and fixed**: the first semantic/hybrid `search_code` over stdio hung (>240 s, vs 19 s when run directly). A thread dump showed the tool-call worker thread stuck importing `sentence_transformers` -> `transformers` -> `sklearn` -> `scipy.special` (a native DLL load); importing on the main thread first made it return. Root cause is a Windows hang on a first-time import from the stdio server's worker thread; the in-process MCP unit tests could not see it. Fix: `CodeEmbedder.preload()` (new) is called by `serve --mcp` on the main thread before the stdio loop starts; if the model cannot load the server still starts, says so on stderr, and keyword search keeps working (no pseudo-embedding fallback). Also moved `serve --mcp`'s status and error lines from stdout to stderr, since stdout is the protocol channel. Files: `codepulse/embeddings/embedder.py`, `codepulse/cli.py`, `tests/test_cli.py` (+3 tests, red first). Verified: `test_cli.py` + `test_mcp_server.py` 40 passed; embedding tests 29 passed; live stdio check 18/18. Full suite NOT re-run after this change (last full run: 607 passed, 1 skipped, before it). One check in my own harness first failed because of a bad assumption (a nonsense query containing the words "not" and "present" legitimately matched Test_Repo comments); not a product bug. **CLI sections run by me (same day)** against a scratch copy of Test_Repo with its own fresh DB (the real Test_Repo DB was not touched): setup-1..3 (index 10 files / 55 symbols / 110 chunks / 59 edges, `embedding_status=success`; doctor all OK; parse works and creates no database file), cli-idx-1 (non-git folder: `incremental=false`, reason "not a git repository", identical on two runs), cli-idx-1b (git repo: `incremental=false` on every CLI run, reason "no in-memory call graph cached"), and cli-q-1..11 -- **all match the checklist's stated expectations** (e.g. `refs Shape` -> only `Polygon`; `callers add` -> 4 callers with 2 high / 2 low confidence; `impact add` depth 1 -> 4 direct / 0 indirect, depth 3 -> 4 / 4; `similar sum_two_values` -> `add` first at 0.88; `--threshold 0.99` -> empty; `modules` -> 6 clusters; `ShoppingCart` and `WidgetRegistry` resolve). Observations, not defects: `search` and `similar` list one symbol several times (per-chunk scoring, already tracked as FUTURE_CHANGES item 1), and a nonsense query still returns ~0.24-scored noise (same item). The checklist's `cli-idx-1b` text says the first CLI `update` shows "no prior successful git-backed snapshot"; that only holds when no `index` ran before it -- after one, every run shows "no in-memory call graph". The user's real Test_Repo has two extra commits (`0fac2a5`, `ac65b09`) from earlier checklist edits. **Publication audit (2026-10-15): a P0 data-loss bug found and fixed; the rest recorded as FUTURE_CHANGES items 20-30.** Method: everything measured from the repositories and real runs, not from earlier summaries. Clean: no secrets in any tracked file or in all 104 commits of history; no tracked databases, archives or binaries; one author; 3.4 MB history; `pip check` clean; server binds 127.0.0.1 and CORS is deny-all by default with wildcard+credentials rejected; only `/health/*` and `/metrics` are unauthenticated; API keys are stored as SHA-256 hashes. Size: 12,504 lines in 46 package files, 18,551 lines in 64 test files, 1,841 lines of extension TypeScript. Coverage 91% (4,297 statements, 368 missed). **The P0 (item 19):** benchmarking a 46-file index showed `Database save failed: database is locked` in the log while the run reported success; the database held a repository row and ZERO files/symbols/embeddings. Root cause 1 (a regression from the module-edges work): `_module_edges_table_exists` inspected through a separate connection while the save transaction was open, which SQLite refuses once the transaction spills past its page cache, i.e. on any real-sized repository. Root cause 2 (older): `_persist_to_db` swallowed every exception and `index_directory` recorded a snapshot and reported success anyway. It escaped every test because they index tiny repositories and the relevance test only reads in-memory indexes. Fix: the check goes through the session's own connection; a failed save raises `IndexingPersistenceError`, records no snapshot, and `_realign_memory_with_database` restores the in-memory indexes from the database so a running server never serves content a restart would lose; REST jobs fail with the real message; the CLI prints a clean error and exits 1. Tests: `tests/test_index_persistence_integrity.py` (6: a 750-function/1,500-chunk repo saved completely and restored after a restart, a failed save raises / saves nothing / leaves memory matching the database / fails the REST job; restoring the old separate-connection check makes it fail with the original lock error). After the fix the real benchmark index persisted 46 files, 357 symbols, 1,210 embeddings, 1,905 call edges, 128 module edges, 1 snapshot. Measured (idle machine, torch 2.3.1+cpu): model load 15-20 s; index 46 files/357 symbols/1,210 chunks in 75 s (about 13 embeddings/s, inherent CPU cost); 110 files in 125-184 s; an incremental update after a one-file change 0.55-1.5 s (120-225x faster); search latency p50/p95 keyword 4.5/7 ms, semantic 31/37 ms, hybrid 37/43 ms over 141 queries. Search quality on the 141-query self-benchmark (Recall@10 / MRR): keyword 0.922/0.816, semantic 0.837/0.620, hybrid 0.872/0.682, rrf 0.936/0.642; but on the 41 hand-written paraphrases (the realistic subset) keyword 0.732/0.458, semantic 0.732/0.568, hybrid 0.659/0.476; about 46% of the semantic/hybrid top-10 are repeats of one symbol (FUTURE_CHANGES item 20). Not verified: anything on Linux or on GitHub Actions (no WSL/Docker here), vulnerability exposure beyond reading each advisory against the code. 

**`codepulse migrate`, tracked databases and packaged migrations (2026-10-14), FUTURE_CHANGES items 8, 10 (finding K) and 17, user-approved ("go ahead with the migrate command"); UNCOMMITTED at time of writing.** Problem: bringing a database up to date meant running `alembic upgrade head` by hand from the repository root; nothing told a user their database was behind; a database CodePulse created itself was never tracked by Alembic, so it could not be upgraded later; and a built wheel shipped NO migrations (the scripts and `alembic.ini` sat outside the package), so none of it could work from an installed copy. Packaging (K): the scripts moved with `git mv` (history kept) from the top-level `alembic/` to `codepulse/storage/schema_migrations/`; `get_alembic_config` now builds the Alembic `Config` in code (no `alembic.ini`, no dependence on the checkout or the current directory); `alembic.ini` stays for developers and points at the new folder; `pyproject.toml` lists the non-.py files as package-data. A side effect, also fixed: a migration run no longer applies the ini's logging section, which used to disable every existing logger and reset the root handlers (the cause of an earlier test quirk). The command (`codepulse/storage/migrations.py`, `cmd_migrate` in `cli.py`): `get_schema_status` classifies a database as fresh / current / behind / unstamped (tables, never tracked, schema matches the models) / diverged (unstamped and the schema differs: structural diff of tables and columns via Alembic's `compare_metadata`) / unknown (stamped with a revision this CodePulse does not have). `codepulse migrate` creates a fresh database at head; upgrades a behind one after a consistent backup of the SQLite file (`<file>.pre-<revision>-<time>.bak`, via the sqlite3 backup API, safe with a writer present); stamps an unstamped one ONLY when its schema provably matches (after a backup); and REFUSES a diverged or unknown one, listing what differs and changing nothing. It verifies the result (revision and `PRAGMA integrity_check`). `--check` only reports (exit 0 up to date, 1 needs migrating, 2 cannot be migrated safely), `--no-backup` skips the copy, `--json` is supported. Looking at a database never creates or changes it. Also: `StorageManager` stamps a database it creates from SCRATCH at head (item 8; a legacy database that already has tables but no tracking is deliberately left alone, since only `migrate` checks its schema before stamping); every CLI command prints a stderr warning when the database is behind/untracked/diverged/from a newer version (stdout stays clean for `--json`); `codepulse doctor` has a `database_schema` check; the module-edges warning now says `codepulse migrate`. Tests: `tests/test_migrate_command.py` (28) covering every state, every migrate outcome (including refusing and leaving the file byte-identical), backups, idempotence, `StorageManager` stamping, the CLI (exit codes, JSON, warnings on stderr, doctor), the packaged config, the logging side effect and a build-config guard; two old assertions changed deliberately, each with the reason in the test: `test_alembic_config_loads` (asserted the folder was called `alembic`) and `test_storage_manager_still_creates_tables_on_a_genuinely_fresh_database` (asserted a database `StorageManager` creates from scratch was NOT tracked "by design", which is exactly the defect of item 8; it now asserts the database is recorded at head). Full suite: the run that included the new code was 714 passed, 2 skipped, 1 failed (that second test, which was the only failure; both migration test files then 43 passed), so a final full run after the fix is still to do. Verified for real: a wheel built from this tree contains all 13 migrations plus `env.py`, the template and the README and no top-level `alembic/`; installed into an empty folder and run from a directory with no `alembic.ini`, `migrate` created a database at the head revision with the INSTALLED copy of the code in use (checked by path). Honest limits: only SQLite files are backed up (other databases are migrated without a copy; none is supported yet); a legacy unstamped database whose data lives in a table a later migration renames (for example the old `dependencies`) is reported as diverged and cannot be fixed by stamping, so it needs a rebuild by re-indexing or a by-hand migration; `migrate` is explicit, nothing migrates automatically on start-up. Demo on COPIES of your three real databases (originals' hashes unchanged): the real `codepulse.db` was `behind` (b9014c049e33, 2 revisions) and `migrate` backed it up and upgraded it (integrity ok, rows identical: call_edges 528, files 18, symbols 128); `Test_Repo/codepulse.db` was unstamped with a matching schema and was backed up and stamped (11 tables' row counts identical); `Test_Repo_NoGit/codepulse.db` was unstamped and missing only the `module_edges` table (made before it existed), which the first version of the rule REFUSED as diverged; refusing over one absent empty table was too strict, so the rule was refined the same day: an unstamped database whose ONLY difference is tables it does not have yet gets those tables created (empty; it cannot conflict with data, and it is what the old code did on every open) and is then stamped, while any missing or extra COLUMN, or an extra table (e.g. a legacy table a later migration renames), still refuses and changes nothing (`SchemaStatus.missing_tables`; 6 new/changed tests; the three refusal tests now drop a column, not a table). A second `migrate --check` on each copy then reported up-to-date. For the real `codepulse.db` (at `b9014c049e33`): run `codepulse migrate --check`, then `codepulse migrate` (it backs the file up first), then re-index `local_repo`; NOT touched here.

**One repository, one identity, however its path is spelled (2026-10-14), FUTURE_CHANGES item 2, user-approved ("fix item 2"); UNCOMMITTED at time of writing.** Problem: a repository was identified by the exact string it was first indexed under, so the same directory spelled with backslashes vs forward slashes, a trailing separator, `..` segments or (found by the real-VS-Code tests) a lowercase vs uppercase Windows drive letter (`c:\\x` vs `C:\\x`) became two repositories; re-indexing created a duplicate with a new id and, since a differently spelled path looked brand-new to the "re-indexing needs indexer" check, a viewer-only key could bypass it. Change: new module `codepulse/repository_identity.py`. `canonical_repository_url` makes a LOCAL PATH absolute with symlinks and `..` resolved (`os.path.realpath`, which on Windows also returns the true case of every component and normalizes slashes), an uppercase drive letter on Windows (also for a path that no longer exists) and no trailing separator; anything that is not a local path (git URLs, `https://`, the synthetic `file:///...` ids) is returned exactly as given. `find_repository` is the one lookup: canonical form first, then an exact row, then any row stored under another spelling (a database not yet migrated, or a duplicate that predates the fix), lowest id first; it never merges or deletes. Used by `index_directory` (stores and indexes under the canonical form), `_resolve_repo_id`, `_persist_to_db`, `hydrate_repository`, `create_repository_owned_by`, the `POST /v1/repositories/index` endpoint (the existing-repository role check now applies to every spelling; the response `url` is the canonical form) and the CLI `status` command. Migration `a41f0d2c6b8e` (head moved from `7c3e5a91d2f4`): DATA-ONLY and non-destructive. It rewrites a local-path `repositories.url` to its canonical form only when no other row already holds that form; a duplicate that predates the fix is left unchanged and logged (the two rows each own their own files, symbols, embeddings, jobs and key grants, so merging is a decision for a person). The canonicalization is written out inside the migration so it keeps doing what it did that day. Downgrade is a no-op (the original spellings were not recorded). Tests: `tests/test_repository_identity.py` (18 passed, 1 skipped: the symlink test, because this Windows account cannot create symlinks; disabling canonicalization made 11 fail) covering the canonical form (every spelling, relative paths, Windows drive/case, non-paths untouched, a missing path), the engine (one repository for six spellings; stored url canonical; hydration), `find_repository` (an old spelling, preferring the canonical row), the REST endpoint (a viewer is refused for every spelling, an indexer re-indexes the same id, no duplicate row, response url canonical), the owner-creation helper and CLI `status`; plus a migration test in `test_migrations.py` (rewrites spellings, leaves non-paths and a pre-existing duplicate untouched, never deletes, idempotent; a no-op migration makes it fail) and the head assertions bumped. No existing test depended on urls being stored as typed. Verification: full backend suite **687 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failures** (10m21s); all migrations applied to a COPY of the real `codepulse.db` (b9014c049e33 -> 7c3e5a91d2f4 -> a41f0d2c6b8e; its one repository was already canonical, nothing rewritten, integrity ok, row counts unchanged, original file's hash unchanged); live against a real server: one directory indexed under six spellings (backslashes, trailing separator, forward slashes, lowercase drive, all-uppercase with forward slashes, `..` segment) gave repository id 1 every time, the same canonical `url`, and the repository count did not change. Limits (also in the module docstring): identity follows the filesystem, so a mapped network drive resolves to its UNC path while the mapping exists, and a path whose true case cannot be recovered (a deleted directory; macOS) can still be spelled more than one way. For the real `codepulse.db`: apply with `alembic upgrade head` (it is at `b9014c049e33`; back it up first; NOT touched here). The extension's workaround (send the stored URL) is now redundant but harmless; it is left in place.

**Who may create a repository, and what the creator gets (2026-10-14), FUTURE_CHANGES item 18, option (a) user-approved ("go with option 1"); UNCOMMITTED at time of writing.** Problem (found by the real-VS-Code tests): `POST /v1/repositories/index` let ANY valid key create a brand-new repository (the role check only ran for an existing one), but `GET /v1/jobs/{id}` needs a grant on the job's repository, which the creating key did not have; the extension's Index Workspace on a new folder ended with "lost track of indexing job ... not scoped to repository N" and the key could never see what it had created. Even a viewer-only key could create repositories. Change (owner semantics): (1) creating a NEW repository requires the `indexer` role (or higher) on at least one EXISTING repository (a viewer-only key or a key with no grants gets 403 and nothing is created); (2) the repository row and the creator's `admin` grant are written in ONE transaction by `codepulse.auth.create_repository_owned_by`, and only the call that actually created the row gets the grant (a second key indexing the same new URL, or one that loses a creation race on the unique url, gets the normal existing-repository role check and no grant); (3) authorization is now decided BEFORE the path is validated, so a key that may not index cannot use 400-vs-403 to probe which paths exist on the host; (4) indexing an EXISTING repository is unchanged. REST only (the CLI has no keys). Tests: `tests/test_repository_creation_authorization.py` (12; two mutation checks, disabling the grant and disabling the permission rule, made 4 and 3 of them fail). 21 existing tests relied on any key creating a repository (most used an unscoped key purely as a convenient way to create one); they now use a new conftest helper `make_creator_headers` (an `indexer` key on a seeded anchor repository), and the two that STATED the old rule were rewritten to the new contract with the reason noted in each: `test_api_authorization.py::test_index_repository_succeeds_with_an_unscoped_key` (now `..._succeeds_with_a_key_that_may_create_repositories`, plus a new `..._is_forbidden_for_an_unscoped_key`) and `test_rbac.py::test_any_authenticated_key_can_still_create_a_brand_new_repository` (now `test_a_key_with_no_grants_can_no_longer_create_a_brand_new_repository`). The ten affected test files: 150 passed, 1 skipped. Extension side (`codepulse-vscode`, uncommitted): Index Workspace now re-resolves the workspace when the job finishes so the status bar stops saying "not indexed"; the e2e suite now rebuilds its fixtures before every run (it mutates them: Index Workspace creates a repository), clears git's read-only files when doing so, waits for the command palette to settle on a command before pressing Enter (a fixed delay raced the filter and made the MCP check flaky), and no longer lists a known gap. Operational note: a real database whose repositories were created by keys under the old rule may hold repositories that no key owns (created without a grant); they can still be indexed by a key with `indexer` or higher granted on them, as before. 

**VS Code extension tests completed (2026-10-12), `codepulse-vscode` repo, UNCOMMITTED at time of writing.** The previously unexercised paths were run in a REAL VS Code window (1.141.0, isolated profile, driven over the Chrome DevTools protocol): Start Server, Index Workspace and the MCP registration. They found and fixed three extension bugs and one backend gap. Fixed in the extension: (1) "Start Server" always launched `codepulse serve` on port 8000 regardless of `codepulse.serverUrl`, so with any other port the extension never connected (seen failing first: listener on 8000 not 8201); now `--host/--port` are derived from serverUrl (`src/serverArgs.ts`), and a non-local or non-http URL gets a clear message. (2) Index Workspace sent the raw VS Code folder path (`c:\\...`), which the server treats as a different repository from the stored `C:\\...`, creating a duplicate repository instead of re-indexing; it now sends the stored URL when the folder matches. (3) Finding D: the repository lookup (about a third of all requests; 26 of 73 measured) is cached for 30 s, shared by concurrent callers and invalidated on key change or index completion, and a 429 is retried up to twice honouring Retry-After (capped at 5 s). (Earlier the same day: `health()` used `/health/ready`, commit `450feb9`.) Tests added to the extension repo: `npm test` runs 23 fast unit tests (`test/unit`, plain Node with fetch stubbed and time injected: server-launch arguments, the repository cache, 429 retry, health endpoint); `npm run test:e2e` runs the real-window suite (`test/e2e`: `setup-fixtures.py` builds disposable fixtures from the sample projects with the backend's Python; `run-all.mjs` starts/stops its own server and runs four scenarios). Result of a from-scratch run (fresh fixtures, fresh profile): **36 passed, 0 failed, 1 known gap**; unit tests 23/23. `.vscodeignore` now excludes `test/**` and `.e2e/**` (a package listing showed the screenshots folder would have shipped); `CHANGELOG.md` updated. MCP in VS Code: the extension's server is listed by `MCP: List Servers`, starts, reaches Running after the model preload (the `serve --mcp` fix from earlier) and its tools are discovered. Backend findings recorded, NOT fixed: FUTURE_CHANGES item 2 now has a concrete user-visible effect (the duplicate repository above; server-side canonicalization is still the real fix) and new item 18 (indexing a never-indexed folder from the extension cannot complete with a scoped key: a key may create a repository but cannot poll its job; real-window check E3, reported as a known gap; options listed, an RBAC decision for the user). Also: the full backend suite run for the stale-rows fix (`1b00cae`) finished 654 passed, 1 skipped, 1 failed; the failure was `test_module_edges_persistence.py::test_the_restored_graph_has_exactly_the_parsed_files_not_stale_files_rows`, written around the stale-row bug, now repaired (both affected files 26 passed). A final full backend run after that repair is still to do.

**A full re-index now purges rows of files that are no longer indexable (2026-10-12), FUTURE_CHANGES item 16; commit `1b00cae`.** Problem: `_persist_to_db` upserted current files but never removed the `files`/`symbols`/`code_embeddings` rows of files deleted, ignored or made unindexable since; only the incremental path purged. A full run is what non-git repositories, first runs and every post-restart run get, so deleted files lingered (their symbols returned in keyword search and in the restored symbol map after a restart; `file_count`/`symbol_count` were inflated; cached parse results of deleted files could be merged back into a later incremental run). Fix: after a full run's parse loop, every stored file not parsed this run is purged through the existing `_purge_stale_entries` (rows, embeddings, keyword and vector indexes, symbol maps, parse cache together). Exceptions, each tested: a file whose parse raised this run keeps its rows; a run that parsed NO files purges nothing (an empty or unmounted directory must not wipe an index; logged). `_purge_stale_entries` now looks paths up in batches of 500 (a single `IN (...)` overflows SQLite's variable limit for a large stale set; tested with 40,000 paths). Incremental runs unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_full_reindex_purges_stale_rows.py` (11; 7 failed before the fix). One existing test, `test_module_edges_persistence.py::test_the_restored_graph_has_exactly_the_parsed_files_not_stale_files_rows`, was written around the stale-row bug and failed once it was fixed (found by the full suite, which I had not seen finish when `1b00cae` was committed, so HEAD was briefly red); it now plants a stale `files` row directly, keeping its purpose (the restored node set comes from the stored snapshot, not from `files` rows). Verification: full suite 654 passed, 1 skipped, 1 failed (the test above, since repaired; both affected files then 26 passed); a final full-suite run after the repair is still to do.

**Module structure survives a restart (2026-10-12), user-approved ("go with persisting the import edges"); commit `0a01b0b`.** Problem: module structure (REST `/modules`, CLI `modules`, MCP `module_structure`, VS Code Module Structure) was built from the in-memory `parse_results`, so it was empty (0 clusters) after every restart; imports were stored nowhere. This reverses Phase 4.3's original "no persistence" scope (noted in the code). Change: new table `module_edges` (Alembic revision `7c3e5a91d2f4`, head moved from `b9014c049e33`; purely additive) holding one row per RESOLVED file-to-file import edge plus a NULL-target row for every file with no resolved imports, so the node set is stored explicitly; unique per (repo_id, source_file, target_file); `repo_id` NOT NULL, cascades on repository delete. The indexer replaces a repository's rows wholesale on every run from the complete merged parse set (idempotent; correct for incremental runs). `ModuleGraphBuilder` gained `snapshot()`, `edges`, `from_edges()` and a shared edge-resolution helper, so a fresh and a restored graph cannot disagree. `hydrate_repository` restores the graph; `analyze_module_structure` prefers this process's parse cache and falls back to the restored graph. Design decisions worth knowing: (a) a repository with NO rows stays cold (empty) rather than showing an all-singletons graph, so a migrated-but-not-re-indexed database behaves exactly as before; (b) nodes come from the stored snapshot, not from `files` rows, which can include stale rows (item 16); (c) a database without the table (Alembic-tracked, not yet migrated) keeps indexing and logs one warning, since `StorageManager` never creates tables there. Tests: `tests/test_module_edges_persistence.py` (15), a new migration test in `test_migrations.py` (head assertions bumped to `7c3e5a91d2f4`), and `test_module_structure_endpoint.py`'s old cold-cache test changed deliberately (it asserted an empty graph after a restart; it now asserts the same structure, and a separate never-indexed case keeps the empty-graph assertion). A mutation check (persistence disabled) made 8 of the then-12 tests fail. Verification: full suite **644 passed, 1 skipped** (8m44s); the migration applied to a COPY of the real `codepulse.db` (integrity ok, row counts unchanged, only `module_edges` added, original file's hash unchanged, and the call graph restored from the real 528 edges); live on a scratch server: 0 clusters before a re-index, 12 rows after one (6 edges + 6 node rows for 10 files), 6 clusters over 10 files after a restart with no re-index; the real VS Code window against that restarted server passed **20 of 20**, including Module Structure. **For the real `codepulse.db`:** it is Alembic-tracked at `b9014c049e33`, so run `alembic upgrade head` from the repository root (back it up first; it was NOT touched here), then re-index `local_repo` once; until then module structure behaves as before. See README "Upgrading an existing database". New open items: FUTURE_CHANGES 16 (a full re-index never deletes rows of deleted files) and 17 (no migrate/stamp command).

**Restart restore (2026-10-12), user-approved ("go with the first option"). Call graph committed as `97f81d8`; symbol map UNCOMMITTED at time of writing (full suite green, awaiting commit).** Problem: after any server restart `impact-analysis`, `references`, the extension's CodeLens/References, `GET /v1/autocomplete` and the graph part (20%) of hybrid ranking were silently empty/absent until a re-index, because `hydrate_all()` restored embeddings and the keyword index but neither `call_graphs` nor `symbols_by_id`. Fix: `CallGraphBuilder.from_edges` + `IndexingEngine._restore_call_graph` rebuild each repository's graph (calls and inheritance, with confidence) from the durable `call_edges` rows; `hydrate_repository` also restores `symbols_by_id`/`symbol_repo_map` from the same symbol rows it already reads for the keyword index; the unscoped-search alias `call_graph` is set to the first restored graph when none exists. Safety: a restored graph has no parse cache, so `_plan_incremental_run` still forces a full run after a restart (an incremental merge onto it would silently drop every unchanged file's edges), and the full run replaces it and re-enables incremental; a FULL re-index now also wipes that repository's entries from the symbol map before re-adding (otherwise a deleted file's symbols would linger; previously a latent same-process bug). `CallGraphEdge.confidence` may be `None` for legacy pre-3.4 rows rather than being faked. Tests: `tests/test_call_graph_restart_restore.py` (7) and `tests/test_symbol_map_restart_restore.py` (10, including identical hybrid/keyword/semantic ranking before and after a restart, autocomplete after a restart, no resurrection of deleted symbols, repository scoping). Three existing assertions that encoded the old limitation were changed deliberately, each with the reason in place: `test_restart_idempotency.py` (`call_graphs == {}` and `symbols_by_id == {}` after a restart) and `test_impact_analysis.py` (`call_graphs == {}` precondition). Verification: full suite after the call-graph change **617 passed, 1 skipped** (9m38s); after the symbol-map change the full suite is **627 passed, 1 skipped** (11m12s; 617 + the 10 new tests, 0 failures). Live: a fresh server on the scratch DB with NO re-index returned `add` = 4 direct / 4 indirect callers with the correct high/low confidences and working autocomplete; the real VS Code window against that cold server passed 19 of 20 (CodeLens `4 callers · 0 callees`, grouped impact list, References `Subclasses (1)`, Search, Similar Code). **Remaining gap at the time (FUTURE_CHANGES item 15, since RESOLVED -- see the entry above):** module structure was still empty after a restart because imports were not persisted. The first real-window run's vsc-9 failure (which I put down to my harness) was this same cold state.

**VS Code section run by me in a REAL VS Code window (1.141.0), 2026-10-12.** Method: the installed VS Code launched with an isolated throwaway profile (own user-data/extensions dirs; the user's VS Code untouched) loading only the extension from `codepulse-vscode/out`, driven over the Chrome DevTools protocol (real key presses, real mouse moves/clicks, DOM reads, screenshots) against a test server on port 8200 serving a scratch copy of Test_Repo. The API key was entered through the extension's real masked input box. Result: **vsc-1 to vsc-14 all pass** (hover shows `function calculate_distance` + docstring + `complexity: 1`; blank-line hover empty; CodeLens `4 callers · 0 callees` on `add` and `0 callers · 0 callees` on `unused_helper`; clicking a lens opens the grouped impact quick-pick with confidence; Search -> picks `calculate_distance` and the cursor lands on Ln 19; References shows `Subclasses (1)` = Polygon only; Similar Code lists `add` first at 88%; Module Structure shows 6 clusters; status bar `55 symbols`; server stopped -> warning with Start Server / Check Connection; unindexed folder -> `isn't indexed yet` + Index Workspace (a different message); multi-root -> folder picker listing both folders, and the status bar follows the active editor 55 -> 1 -> 55 symbols). Not covered by the real-window run: the `Start Server` button actually launching `codepulse serve`, `Index Workspace` completing, MCP registration inside VS Code's chat, and the Marketplace publish. The throwaway harness lives in the session scratchpad, not in either repo. **Defects found:** (1) FIXED in the extension repo (uncommitted there): `CodePulseClient.health()` called `/health/ready`, which a freshly started server answers with 503 until its lazily loaded embedding model loads, so the extension showed a healthy new server as disconnected, Search said "no server is reachable", and auto-launch waited on a check that could never pass; it now uses `/health/live`. Seen as a failing smoke run first, then passing. (2) OPEN, backend, significant: the in-memory call graph is not restored on server start (documented earlier as a known limitation), so after ANY server restart `impact-analysis`, `references`, and therefore the extension's CodeLens and References sidebar silently report 0 callers/subclasses until the repository is re-indexed; reproduced directly over REST (`add`: 0 callers after restart, 4 after re-index) and in the window. The responses give no hint that the graph is cold. The durable `call_edges` table exists but is not read on startup. Needs a decision before any fix (CLAUDE.md §12). (3) Data for finding D: of 73 requests the extension made in the final server run, 26 were uncached `GET /v1/repositories`; no 429 occurred in this run. Still to do: decide (2); the extension's finding-D fix; commit the extension change.

Backend hardening driven by manual QA testing (not roadmap sprint items), three commits made after the VS Code extension Phase 4 entries below and previously unrecorded here: `e9e360c` (pin `numpy<2` -- torch 2.3.x's CPU wheel cannot use NumPy 2.x, which silently degraded indexing to lexical-only), `04d7c17` (remove `from __future__ import annotations` from `mcp_server.py` -- it made every one of the 8 MCP tools fail to register with the `mcp` SDK), and `6a7e968` (every CLI command now prints which database file it is using and loudly warns when it is about to create a NEW one). Verified: full suite 590 passed, 1 skipped -- COMPLETE

## Current Implementation State

### Audit 2026-10-12 -- second audit + pre-publication fixes

**Verified healthy (re-checked from the repository, not from earlier reports)**: git clean in all three repos at the start; **590 passed, 1 skipped** before the batch; `pip check` clean; all 43 roadmap items map to valid commits; each permanent invariant has dedicated tests; no outbound network use beyond the SSRF DNS lookup and the Hugging Face model download; real `codepulse.db` integrity ok (only `local_repo`: 18 files / 128 symbols); extension manifest matches its code and `npm audit` reports 0 vulnerabilities.

**Fixed in this batch (each with tests that were red first)**

| | Fix | Where | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| E | `[project.scripts] codepulse = "codepulse.cli:main"`; `license = { text = "MIT" }` | `pyproject.toml` | `tests/test_packaging.py` (3) |
| A | A nonexistent path or a file is rejected: REST `400` ("not an existing directory") with no repository row created; CLI exits 1 with a message (`--json` gives `{"error":"path_not_found"}`) and does not create the database. The allow-list check runs first, so paths outside the allowed roots cannot be probed for existence. `parse`, `init`, `doctor`, `serve` are exempt. | `ingestion_safety.validate_local_path`, `cli.main` | +4 in `test_ingestion_safety.py`, +1 in `test_ingestion_safety_endpoint.py`, +6 in `test_cli.py` |
| F | Unknown route and wrong method now return the §21 envelope (`not_found` / `method_not_allowed`, `Allow` header preserved). The handler is registered on `starlette.exceptions.HTTPException`, the base class Starlette's router raises. Router-level errors share one fixed metric label, `endpoint="unmatched"`, so random URLs cannot create unbounded `api_errors_total` series. | `api/errors.py` | +2 in `test_error_format.py`, +1 in `test_observability.py` |
| B | README rewritten to the real architecture (SQLite + sqlite-vec + Tantivy, no Docker/Postgres/Redis) with install, CLI, REST (including first-key bootstrap), MCP and config sections. The quick-start commands (`init`, `index`, `search`, `callers`, `doctor`, key bootstrap) were run against a throwaway directory. | `README.md` | n/a (docs) |
| J | The stale "6.2 BLOCKED" statements were corrected in the standing notice, "Current Phase", "Roadmap work remaining", the Context Handoff status line and the deferred-items list. Older per-milestone history entries still describe 6.2 as blocked at the time they were written. | this file | n/a |
| M | `LICENSE` (MIT, matching the extension repo) and `.gitattributes` (`text=auto`; `*.png`, `*.zip`, `*.db` binary). The git index was already 100% LF. | repo root | `tests/test_packaging.py` |

**New findings (recorded, NOT fixed -- each needs its own go-ahead per CLAUDE.md §12)**
- **K (Medium, packaging)**: a built wheel lacks `alembic.ini` and the migrations, because `migrations.get_alembic_config` resolves them relative to the source tree. An editable install (the documented path) works. A non-editable `pip install` would break any migration call.
- **L (Low, hygiene)**: `ruff` and `mypy` are declared in the dev extras but never run in CI. Today: ruff 124 findings and mypy 118 errors, mostly noise (FastAPI `Depends()` default arguments, SQLAlchemy typing). Real minors: ~16 unused imports, ~16 blind `except Exception`, 7 `datetime.utcnow()` uses. Five subpackages lack `__init__.py` and rely on implicit namespace packages.
- **N (Low)**: `api_errors_total` is labelled by the raw request path for MATCHED routes, so ids in the path (`/v1/symbols/{id}`) give one series per id. Fix F only collapses unmatched paths. Fix: label by route template (`request.scope["route"].path`).

**Lint note**: ruff on the files touched by this batch reports 16 findings. All but one are older and untouched by it (unused imports, blind excepts in `cli.py`; an unused import in `test_observability.py`). The one this batch introduced (import order in `errors.py`) was fixed.

### Audit 2026-10-02 -- post-hiatus resumption audit + live REST verification (audit only; no fixes made)

**Why**: work resumed after a gap, so per CLAUDE.md §10/§13 the repository state was reconstructed from git, files, tests, databases and migrations rather than from this document or earlier reports.

**Verified healthy**
- Git: backend `master` clean except the new untracked `CODEPULSE_FUTURE_CHANGES.md`; extension repo clean (4 commits, `ffda71a`..`e68ec21`); no stray processes or listening ports.
- Tests: **590 passed, 1 skipped** (`pytest -q`, 9m57s, at `6a7e968`); 174 of those are REST-facing (`test_api*`, `test_rbac`, `test_error_format`, `test_cors`, `test_rate_limit_middleware`, `test_job_endpoints`, ingestion-safety, listing/resolve, isolation, observability).
- Dependencies: `pip check` clean; numpy 1.26.4 / torch 2.3.1 / mcp 1.10.1 / pydantic 2.7.4 as pinned.
- Migrations: Alembic head `b9014c049e33` == the real `codepulse.db` revision; `PRAGMA integrity_check` ok.
- Code scan of `codepulse/`: one `subprocess.run` (git, no `shell=True`), no `eval`/`exec`/`pickle`/`os.system`, no TODO/FIXME. Extension: `tsc` clean, `npm audit` 0 vulnerabilities, 0 runtime dependencies.
- **REST, live (real uvicorn, real embedder, real HTTP):** all 10 `rest-*` checks from the QA checklist re-run verbatim from the published HTML -- pass. Plus a 69-check live suite (every route family; cross-repo isolation on 13 endpoints + autocomplete + "search all repos"; validation + §21 envelope; SSRF/`file://` rejection; full key lifecycle + privilege-escalation attempts; no raw key at rest, `key_hash` is 64-hex SHA-256; DB integrity after the run): **69/69 pass**. Search results are deterministic across repeated identical queries (permanent invariant). `/metrics` counters reflect real traffic.

**Findings (all reproduced; none fixed)**

| | Sev | Finding | Root cause / where |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | Medium | **RESOLVED 2026-10-12.** A nonexistent local path is accepted: CLI `index`/`callers`/`symbol`/... and `POST /v1/repositories/index` (202) create an empty phantom repository; CLI exits 0 with "0 found". The real `codepulse.db` already has one (`C:\path\to\your\repo`, from the manual's placeholder command; removed from the real DB 2026-10-02, the bug itself is still open). | `ingestion_safety.validate_local_path` (line ~96) resolves and checks containment but never checks existence / is-a-directory |
| B | Medium (docs) | **RESOLVED 2026-10-12 (README rewritten and its commands run).** `README.md` claims PostgreSQL `pgvector`, Redis Streams, Docker prerequisites; none exist in code or deps (actual: sqlite-vec, in-process, no Docker). README has no usage section (no CLI, `serve`, key bootstrap). | stale/aspirational text |
| C | Low-Med (latent) | Every fresh DB (first CLI/`serve` run) is created by `create_all()` and **never stamped** with the Alembic head; no CLI command stamps/upgrades (`migrations.stamp_database` exists, uncalled). Any future migration needs a manual stamp. | `StorageManager.create_tables()` by design (docstring), but no stamping step exists for end users |
| D | Low-Med | Default limiter is 5 req/s, burst 20 **per API key**; the extension shares one key across hover/CodeLens/sidebars. Measured with the real compiled client: opening a 9-lens file + 3 hovers = 17 requests (ok); 12 cursor settles in 5.5s = 48 requests, **3 x 429 (6%)**. Extension has no 429 retry, and `listRepositories()` (uncached) is ~25% of its traffic. | `config.rate_limit_*` defaults; `workspaceRepo.resolveWorkspaceRepository` |
| E | Medium (packaging) | **RESOLVED 2026-10-12 (`codepulse = "codepulse.cli:main"`).** No `[project.scripts]` entry, so there is no `codepulse` executable; the extension's default `codepulse.serverCommand` = `codepulse` cannot work out of the box (real invocation is `python -m codepulse.cli`). | `pyproject.toml` |
| F | Low | **RESOLVED 2026-10-12.** Unmatched route (404) and wrong method (405) return `{"detail": ...}`, not the §21 `{"error": {...}}` envelope; application-raised errors are correctly shaped. | `api/errors.py:129` registers on `fastapi.HTTPException`; Starlette's routing raises its base `starlette.exceptions.HTTPException` (fastapi's is a subclass, so the handler does not match) |
| G | Low (latent) | `validate_git_url` rejects NAT64-synthesized addresses (`64:ff9b::/96`, `is_reserved`) -- on a DNS64/IPv6-only network *every* public git host is rejected as an SSRF risk (here github.com -> `64:ff9b::14cf:4952`, embedded public IPv4 20.207.73.82). No impact today: git ingestion is not implemented (501 regardless). | `ingestion_safety.py:151-155` does not extract the embedded IPv4 |
| H | Info | 403-vs-404 on a symbol id is an existence oracle for foreign keys (ids are sequential). Common tradeoff; recorded, not necessarily a defect. | route-level `authorize_repository` after lookup |
| I | Process (mine) | During QA I deleted two stray `repositories` rows with raw SQL on a connection without `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON`; child rows (files/symbols/embeddings) survived as orphans and SQLite reused id 3, so a later phantom repo adopted them (surfaced as `file_count = 11`). Repaired by deleting through `StorageManager` (FK-enforcing, cascades): `foreign_key_check` empty, `integrity_check` ok, repos 1/2 counts unchanged; pre-repair backup kept in the session scratchpad. Lesson: never delete via a raw connection in this project. | my cleanup |

**Also corrected during the audit**: the QA checklist's `rest-1` promised `200` from `/health/ready`, but a cold server correctly returns `503` (`embedding_model_loaded:false`) until the first index/semantic search; the checklist now tests `/health/live` + explains `/health/ready`, and warns about the limiter.

**Not verifiable from this environment (unchanged)**: VS Code's own UI rendering/MCP host behavior (no interactive window), CI on real GitHub Actions (no remote), Postgres/Docker (blocked 7.1/7.3).

**Resolved 2026-10-02 (CLAUDE.md §7 -- done only on explicit instruction)**: the real `codepulse.db` held two rows that originated in this QA session -- repo 2 `Test_Repo` (real data, harmless, but not original) and repo 3 `C:\path\to\your\repo` (a phantom, finding A). The user explicitly instructed their removal; see "Database State" for the backup and verification.

### VS Code extension Phase 4 — polish and ship (separate `codepulse-vscode` repo, not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: `CODEPULSE_CLIENT_STRATEGY.md`'s own named Phase 4 -- "Marketplace packaging (icon, README, publisher registration), multi-root workspace support (multiple indexed repos open at once, using Phase 0's list endpoint to disambiguate), basic telemetry opt-in if wanted, error states for 'server unreachable' / 'not yet indexed'." Entirely built in the separate `codepulse-vscode` repository (commit `e68ec21`); no backend code in THIS repo changed.

- **Multi-root workspace support (`src/workspaceRepo.ts`)**: `pickWorkspaceFolder()` -- no prompt when exactly one folder is open (unchanged UX for the common case), VS Code's own built-in workspace-folder quick-pick when there's more than one. Wired into `src/search.ts`, `src/indexWorkspace.ts`, `src/moduleStructure.ts`, each of which previously hardcoded `workspaceFolders?.[0]` and silently ignored any other open folder. `activeOrFirstWorkspaceFolder()` -- the status bar now tracks the ACTIVE editor's own folder (`extension.ts` re-runs `refreshConnectionState` on `onDidChangeActiveTextEditor`), so it reflects whichever of two open repositories the user is actually looking at, not always the first one.
- **A real correctness fix found while doing this, not just new capability**: `toRepoRelativePath` previously delegated to `vscode.workspace.asRelativePath`'s own GLOBAL folder-detection rather than actually scoping to the `folder` argument it was given -- harmless in every case exercised so far (every caller's `folder` always happened to be the URI's own containing folder), but not an enforced invariant, and a real latent bug for a genuinely divergent call in a multi-root workspace. Rewritten to compute the relative path directly against the given folder's own `fsPath`, case-insensitively on Windows.
- **Error states (`src/workspaceRepo.ts`)**: `resolveWorkspaceRepositoryDetailed()` returns one of `{status: "found"|"not-indexed"|"unreachable"}` -- distinguishing "the server itself isn't reachable" from "the server is up but this folder isn't indexed," which `search.ts`/`moduleStructure.ts` previously conflated into one blanket "not indexed yet" message that was actively misleading when the real problem was connectivity. `explainUnresolvedWorkspace()` shows the right message with an actionable button (`Start Server`/`Check Connection` vs. `Index Workspace`) wired to the existing commands, rather than a dead-end warning.
- **Marketplace packaging**: new `media/icon.png` (128x128; generated with a small, dependency-free pure-Node PNG encoder script since no image library/tool was available in this environment -- the script itself was NOT committed, only its output, matching how a design asset produced by any other external tool wouldn't be either; visually matches `media/icon.svg`'s own pulse-line motif but with real, defined colors, since the Marketplace gallery renders it standalone, never theme-tinted the way the Activity Bar icon is). `package.json` gained `icon`, `license`, `categories` (`Programming Languages`, `Other` -- deliberately NOT `Machine Learning`, which would overclaim what this tool does), `keywords`; `publisher` stays the pre-existing `"codepulse"` placeholder. New `LICENSE` (MIT) and `CHANGELOG.md`. Added `@vscode/vsce` as a devDependency and an `npm run package` script.
- **What was explicitly, deliberately NOT done, and why**:
  - **Publisher registration and actual Marketplace submission**: these require the user's own Microsoft/Azure DevOps account, a real registered publisher ID, and a Personal Access Token -- creating accounts and handling credentials on someone else's behalf is out of bounds regardless of how routine the action is. Documented instead, as concrete next steps, in the extension's own new README "Publishing" section.
  - **A `repository` field in `package.json`**: this repo has no real git remote (kept local per the user's own earlier explicit instruction, echoing this backend repo's own history). Fabricating a plausible-looking GitHub URL would have been actively misleading, not just incomplete -- omitted instead, with the README's own three now-fixed links (two pointed at a local sibling directory, one at a bare placeholder GitHub URL) serving as the concrete example of why that would have been a real problem once packaged (`vsce package` itself caught and refused to build past the first of these, which is what surfaced this).
  - **Telemetry**: "basic telemetry opt-in if wanted" in the plan already anticipated this might not be wanted. No telemetry-collection backend exists anywhere in this project (REST, CLI, or MCP), and building one would directly contradict `CODEPULSE_PRODUCT_AND_ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own stated "local-first... zero data leaving the machine" identity. A scope decision, not a gap.
- **Files changed** (`codepulse-vscode` repo only): `src/workspaceRepo.ts`, `src/search.ts`, `src/indexWorkspace.ts`, `src/moduleStructure.ts`, `src/extension.ts`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `README.md` (modified); `media/icon.png`, `LICENSE`, `CHANGELOG.md` (new) -- committed as `e68ec21`.
- **Exact verification commands run**: `npm run compile` (zero errors). `npx vsce package` -- first attempt failed with a clear, specific error (a relative README link that "would be broken" once published); fixed the three affected links (README-only changes, no logic affected) and re-ran -- second attempt succeeded, producing `codepulse-vscode-0.1.0.vsix` (24 files, 37.6KB, containing `LICENSE.txt`, `changelog.md`, `package.json`, `readme.md`, `media/icon.png`+`icon.svg`, and all 16 compiled `out/*.js` files) -- a real, structural proof that packaging works, not just that `npm run compile` succeeds. The `.vsix` itself was deleted after inspection (git-ignored already, trivially regenerable via `npm run package`, not meant to be a committed artifact). VS Code's own runtime behavior for every new UI path (the workspace-folder quick-pick actually appearing, the status bar actually updating on editor-switch, the error-state warning buttons actually working) could NOT be verified from this CLI-only session -- disclosed, not claimed, the same category of gap disclosed for every previous phase's UI rendering.
- **What remains**: **committing this document's own update to git** (the checkpoint commit immediately follows). The VS Code extension's own named phases (1 through 4) are now ALL complete. What's left toward a real Marketplace listing is entirely the user's own action (publisher registration, PAT, `vsce publish` or manual upload -- see the extension's own README). Beyond that, per `CODEPULSE_CLIENT_STRATEGY.md`'s own remaining scope: the entire Web UI plan beyond its already-shared Phase 0, and the genuinely-blocked roadmap items (7.1 Postgres, 7.3 Docker) noted at the top of this document.

### VS Code extension Phase 3 — MCP integration (separate `codepulse-vscode` repo, not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: `CODEPULSE_CLIENT_STRATEGY.md`'s own named Phase 3 for the VS Code extension -- "Register CodePulse's existing MCP server as a discoverable MCP provider inside VS Code... reuses `codepulse serve --mcp` exactly as it exists today -- no new backend work, just extension-side wiring." One genuine prerequisite was found mid-implementation and built in THIS repo instead (below) -- the "no new backend work" framing held for the MCP SERVER itself, but not for how its API key reaches it safely when a THIRD PARTY (VS Code, not a human typing a command) is the one launching it.

- **New CLI capability, THIS repo**: `codepulse.cli.resolve_mcp_api_key(cli_value, env=None)` (`codepulse/cli.py`). `serve --mcp` previously required `--api-key` as its ONLY input -- fine for a human running the command directly, but wrong for a process spawning it on someone else's behalf: command-line arguments are visible to any other process on the same machine (`ps aux`, Windows Task Manager, `/proc/[pid]/cmdline`), so a launcher putting a raw secret there leaks it further than necessary. `resolve_mcp_api_key` prefers `--api-key` when given (unchanged behavior, zero risk to existing callers) and falls back to a new `CODEPULSE_MCP_API_KEY` environment variable otherwise -- environment variables of a child process are NOT visible to unrelated processes the way argv is. Extracted as its own small, pure function specifically so it (unlike the rest of `cmd_serve --mcp`, undisclosed-and-untestable per `tests/test_cli.py`'s own module docstring) could be unit-tested. 4 new tests: CLI-value priority, env-var fallback, neither-set → `None`, empty-string CLI value treated as unset.
- **`src/mcpProvider.ts` (extension repo)**: `CodePulseMcpServerDefinitionProvider`, registered via `vscode.lm.registerMcpServerDefinitionProvider("codepulse.mcpServers", provider)` during activation, matching `package.json`'s new `contributes.mcpServerDefinitionProviders` entry (required by that API's own contract). Two-phase, per the API's own design: `provideMcpServerDefinitions` returns a placeholder `McpStdioServerDefinition` (command `codepulse.serverCommand`, args `["serve", "--mcp"]`, no key yet) without ANY user interaction (the API's own docs explicitly forbid that at this stage, since it's called eagerly/often); `resolveMcpServerDefinition` -- called only when the editor actually needs to start the server -- fetches the stored API key (prompting via the existing `AuthManager.setApiKeyInteractive()` if unset) and fills in `server.env = { CODEPULSE_MCP_API_KEY: apiKey }` and `server.cwd`, never touching `args`. Returns `undefined` (the API's own documented way to cancel a start) if no key ends up available.
- **`engines.vscode` bumped `^1.85.0` -> `^1.101.0`** (also `devDependencies["@types/vscode"]`, `package-lock.json` regenerated) -- `registerMcpServerDefinitionProvider` is confirmed STABLE API (present directly in `@types/vscode`'s main `index.d.ts`, not a separate `vscode.proposed.*.d.ts` file the way in-progress APIs ship), stabilized in VS Code 1.101. This narrows the extension's own minimum supported VS Code version for ALL its features, not just this one -- a real, disclosed compatibility tradeoff, not a silent one.
- **`src/extension.ts`**: wires `registerMcpProvider(context, auth)` in during activation; the existing "CodePulse: Set API Key" command handler now also calls the provider's `notifyApiKeyChanged()` after a successful save, firing `onDidChangeMcpServerDefinitions` so VS Code re-resolves (and re-prompts for tools) instead of continuing to treat a previously-missing key as still missing.
- **What was explicitly NOT done, and why**: no new MCP tools, and no change to `codepulse/mcp_server.py`'s existing 8 tools -- Phase 3 is register-and-wire only, exactly as scoped; no Marketplace packaging (Phase 4) -- a separate, later milestone.
- **Files changed**:
  - THIS repo: `codepulse/cli.py` (+28/-3 lines: `resolve_mcp_api_key` + `cmd_serve`/argparse updated to use it), `tests/test_cli.py` (+20 lines, 4 new tests + docstring note).
  - New repository (`codepulse-vscode`): `src/mcpProvider.ts` (new), `src/extension.ts`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `README.md` (modified) -- committed there as `c5134ab`.
- **Exact verification commands run**: THIS repo -- `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests/test_cli.py -q -k resolve_mcp_api_key` (4 passed), then the full `tests/test_cli.py` (17 passed), then the full suite (583 passed, 1 skipped). A real, disposable-DB end-to-end check of the CLI change itself (not just unit tests): a fresh SQLite DB + a bootstrap API key, `codepulse serve --mcp` launched with `CODEPULSE_MCP_API_KEY` set and NO `--api-key` -- printed "MCP server starting (stdio transport)..." and exited cleanly (stdin closed immediately in this non-interactive check, an expected outcome for stdio transport, not a failure); the SAME command with neither `--api-key` nor the env var set -- printed the updated error message and exited with code 1, as expected. Extension repo -- `npm run compile` (zero errors, confirmed via fresh `out/mcpProvider.js`/`out/extension.js` timestamps) and `npm install` (package-lock.json's own metadata sync only, no dependency-tree changes -- `@types/vscode` had already resolved to `1.138.0`, comfortably satisfying both the old and new declared ranges). VS Code's own MCP host behavior (actually discovering and starting the server via this provider, a real chat participant like Copilot Chat listing CodePulse's tools) could NOT be verified from this CLI-only session -- disclosed, not claimed, same category of gap as Phase 1/2's own UI-rendering disclosures.
- **What remains**: **committing this document's own update to git** (the checkpoint commit immediately follows). The extension's Phase 4 (Marketplace packaging, multi-root workspace support, telemetry opt-in, "server unreachable"/"not yet indexed" error states) has NOT been started, and lives entirely in the separate `codepulse-vscode` repository going forward -- this document will continue to record it as a cross-repo pointer, the same pattern established here.

### VS Code extension Phase 2 — core navigation (separate `codepulse-vscode` repo, not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: `CODEPULSE_CLIENT_STRATEGY.md`'s own named Phase 2 for the VS Code extension -- "Core navigation (every feature below is a thin call to an already-built, already-tested REST endpoint)." As with Phase 1, the extension's own code changes are in the separate `codepulse-vscode` repository; this entry is a cross-repo summary pointer (CLAUDE.md §10) -- that repo's own commit (`1d1406f`) carries the full implementation detail. One genuine prerequisite was found mid-implementation and built in THIS repo instead (below).

- **New backend endpoint, THIS repo**: `GET /v1/repositories/{id}/file-symbols?path=` (`codepulse/api/app.py`, committed separately as `69511ce` before the extension work). The CodeLens provider needs every symbol in an open file, by workspace-relative path, to place one lens per symbol -- neither existing lookup covered that (`resolve` answers one line at a time; `GET /v1/files/{file_id}/symbols` needs an internal `file_id` the client doesn't have). Mirrors the latter route's shape exactly, just resolved via `(repo_id, path)` like `resolve` already does, in one round trip. 4 new tests (200 shape/ordering, 404 unknown path, 403 unscoped repo, 401 unauthenticated); full suite re-run after: 583/583 passed + 1 environment-driven skip.
- **`src/client.ts` (extension repo)**: extended with 9 new methods/interfaces covering every Phase 2 endpoint -- `getFileSymbols`, `indexRepository`, `getJobStatus`, `search`, `getSymbolDetails`, `getSimilar`, `getImpactAnalysis`, `getReferences`, `getModules` -- each typed against the REST route's actual, code-verified response shape (re-read from `codepulse/api/app.py` and `codepulse/indexer/engine.py` fresh this session, not from memory).
- **`src/indexWorkspace.ts`**: "CodePulse: Index Workspace" -> `POST /v1/repositories/index`, then polls `GET /v1/jobs/{id}` inside a `vscode.window.withProgress` notification. Terminal statuses are `"succeeded"`/`"failed"` (verified directly against `codepulse/jobs.py`, not assumed -- an earlier draft incorrectly guessed `"completed"`, caught before commit).
- **`src/search.ts`**: "CodePulse: Search" -> an input box + `POST /v1/search` (hybrid by default) + a quick-pick of results; picking one jumps to `file_path:start_line` via `vscode.workspace.asRelativePath`-style joining against the workspace folder.
- **`src/hoverProvider.ts`**: a `vscode.HoverProvider` for `python`/`javascript(react)`/`typescript(react)` (the exact language set `codepulse/parser/factory.py` actually parses, not guessed) that calls only `GET /v1/repositories/{id}/resolve` -- that one response already carries name/type/docstring/complexity, so no second `GET /v1/symbols/{id}` round-trip is needed for a lightweight, every-mouse-move hover.
- **`src/codeLensProvider.ts`** + **`src/impactPanel.ts`**: a two-phase `vscode.CodeLensProvider` -- `provideCodeLenses` places one lens per symbol cheaply via the new file-symbols endpoint (one call per file), and `resolveCodeLens` -- called only for lenses actually scrolled into view -- makes the one `POST /v1/symbols/{id}/impact-analysis` call each needs, showing "N callers · M callees". Clicking opens `codepulse.showImpact`, a quick-pick of direct/transitive callers and callees that opens the containing file (no line number, since impact-analysis call-graph entries don't carry one -- an honest reflection of what the endpoint returns, not a client-side approximation).
- **`src/activeSymbolTracker.ts`**: a shared, debounced (300ms) "what symbol is the cursor in" resolver, backed by the same `resolve` endpoint, so the two sidebar TreeViews below don't each independently re-resolve on every cursor move.
- **`src/referencesView.ts`** + **`src/similarView.ts`**: two new sidebar TreeViews under a new "CodePulse" Activity Bar container (`media/icon.svg`) -- References (`GET /v1/symbols/{id}/references`: direct callers + direct subclasses, grouped) and Similar Code (`POST /v1/symbols/{id}/similar`) -- both follow `ActiveSymbolTracker`.
- **`src/moduleStructure.ts`**: "CodePulse: Module Structure" -> `GET /v1/repositories/{id}/modules`, rendered as a one-shot quick-pick grouped by cluster (no live-refresh TreeView, since clustering only changes on re-index, unlike the cursor-following views above).
- **`src/workspaceRepo.ts`**: extracted the workspace-folder <-> `CodePulseRepository` matching logic Phase 1's status bar already had inline, so six+ new features share one implementation instead of diverging; `extension.ts`'s own `refreshConnectionState` was refactored to use it too, rather than keeping two copies.
- **What was explicitly NOT done, and why**: no MCP integration (Phase 3) or Marketplace packaging (Phase 4) -- later, separate milestones; no client-side re-ranking, filtering, or symbol detection of any kind (e.g. CodeLens placement uses the new file-symbols endpoint, not a regex scan of the document) -- every feature stays a thin REST caller, per roadmap §23's own "never reimplementing retrieval logic client-side."
- **A genuine, pre-existing backend limitation confirmed (not caused) during verification**: `IndexingEngine.call_graphs` is rebuilt only by the process whose own `index_directory()` call indexed a repository -- `hydrate_repository`'s own docstring already documents this ("The call graph... remains NOT durable... still requires a fresh `index_directory()` call"). A first verification attempt that pre-indexed the test repo via a standalone script, then pointed a separately-started live server at the same database, got empty callers/subclasses/impact results for that reason -- not a Phase 2 bug, just this documented limitation surfacing for the first time in an interactive-navigation context. Corrected by indexing entirely through the live server itself for the real verification run (also a more realistic test of the actual extension usage pattern: one long-lived `codepulse serve` process). Practical implication for the extension, worth users knowing: hover/CodeLens/references/impact-analysis results reflect whichever repository state the CURRENTLY RUNNING server process last indexed -- restarting the server without re-indexing will show stale-empty call-graph data even though symbols/search still work (those two ARE durable). Not something this milestone needed to fix (no roadmap item requests call-graph persistence), but worth a future backlog entry if it proves confusing in practice.
- **Files changed**:
  - THIS repo: `codepulse/api/app.py` (+49 lines, new route), `tests/test_repository_listing_and_resolve.py` (+52 lines, 4 new tests) -- committed `69511ce`.
  - New repository (`codepulse-vscode`): `package.json`, `src/client.ts`, `src/extension.ts` (modified); `media/icon.svg`, `src/activeSymbolTracker.ts`, `src/codeLensProvider.ts`, `src/hoverProvider.ts`, `src/impactPanel.ts`, `src/indexWorkspace.ts`, `src/moduleStructure.ts`, `src/referencesView.ts`, `src/search.ts`, `src/similarView.ts`, `src/workspaceRepo.ts` (new); `README.md` status section updated -- committed there as `1d1406f`.
- **Exact verification commands run**: THIS repo -- `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests/test_repository_listing_and_resolve.py -q` (14 passed) then the full suite (583 passed, 1 skipped, ~11 min). Extension repo -- `npm run compile` (zero errors, confirmed via fresh `out/*.js` timestamps for all 9 new files). End-to-end -- a disposable SQLite DB + a live `uvicorn codepulse.api.app:app` process (disposable, ports 8020-8022 across cleanup iterations, all killed afterward; real `codepulse.db` never referenced), a rich two-file test repo (class + subclass + same-file and cross-file calls + a near-duplicate function for similarity) and a second minimal repo indexed via the live server's own async job endpoint, scoped API keys minted per repository via `codepulse.auth.create_api_key` directly against the disposable DB file, all 12 new/changed `CodePulseClient` methods driven from a throwaway Node script (`verify_phase2_full.js`, in the session scratchpad, not committed) against the compiled `out/client.js` -- every assertion passed, printed "ALL PHASE 2 CHECKS PASSED". VS Code's own UI rendering (hover popups, CodeLens display, sidebar TreeView rendering, F5 Extension Development Host) could NOT be verified from this CLI-only session -- disclosed, not claimed.
- **What remains**: **committing this document's own update to git** (the checkpoint commit immediately follows). The extension's Phase 3 (MCP integration) and Phase 4 (Marketplace packaging) have NOT been started, and live entirely in the separate `codepulse-vscode` repository going forward -- this document will continue to record each of their milestones as a cross-repo pointer, the same pattern established here.

### VS Code extension Phase 1 — foundation (separate `codepulse-vscode` repo, not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: `CODEPULSE_CLIENT_STRATEGY.md`'s own named Phase 1 for the VS Code extension -- "Foundation (new repo: `codepulse-vscode`, TypeScript)." Per the roadmap's own B22 framing ("New extension repo/package") and the client-strategy document's explicit statement that this "lives outside `codepulse/` as its own package," this milestone's actual code changes are in a NEW, SEPARATE git repository at `C:\Dhairya\ACADS\Projects\codepulse-vscode`, not in this repository. This entry is a cross-repo summary pointer (CLAUDE.md §10: "the repository is the source of truth" -- for this milestone, THAT repository, not this document, is the durable record) -- the extension repo's own commit (`ffda71a`) and its own commit message carry the full implementation detail.

- **Project scaffold**: `package.json` (name `codepulse-vscode`, `engines.vscode: ^1.85.0`, 3 commands, 3 configuration settings), `tsconfig.json` (strict mode), `.gitignore`/`.vscodeignore`, `README.md`. `devDependencies`: `typescript`, `@types/vscode`, `@types/node` only -- no HTTP client library, since the runtime's own built-in `fetch`/`URL` (available in VS Code's Electron-bundled Node since 1.83, safely under this extension's `^1.85.0` floor) covers everything Phase 1 needs.
- **`src/client.ts`**: `CodePulseClient`, a thin wrapper over exactly the REST endpoints Phase 1 needs (`health` via `GET /health/ready`, `listRepositories` via `GET /v1/repositories`, `getIndexStatus`, `resolveSymbol` via `GET /v1/repositories/{id}/resolve` -- both of the latter two calling THIS repo's own Phase 0 endpoints). Deliberately does not implement search/symbol-detail/references/impact/similar yet -- those are later phases' own thin callers, added when the UI that will actually use them exists, not speculatively ahead of it. Surfaces the server's own §21 structured-error `message` field on a non-2xx response rather than a bare HTTP status code.
- **`src/serverManager.ts`**: `ServerManager` -- checks `health()` first; if unreachable and `codepulse.autoLaunchServer` is enabled, ASKS the user (never launches silently) before spawning `<codepulse.serverCommand> serve` as a managed child process, then polls for readiness for up to 10 seconds. The launched process's stdout/stderr are streamed to a dedicated "CodePulse" output channel, and `dispose()` kills it on extension deactivation -- never leaves an orphaned server process running after VS Code closes.
- **`src/auth.ts`**: `AuthManager` -- stores the API key exclusively via VS Code's `SecretStorage`, never in `settings.json`, set through a new "CodePulse: Set API Key" command (a password-masked input box). Mirrors the CodePulse server's own "never persist the raw key anywhere it doesn't have to be" discipline on the client side.
- **`src/statusBar.ts` + `src/extension.ts`**: a status-bar item (§23's own "status-bar indexing indicator") showing connection state and, once connected, the CURRENT WORKSPACE's real, measured symbol count -- resolved by calling `listRepositories()` (Phase 0) and matching a repository's `url` (the absolute local path, for a locally-indexed repo) against the open workspace folder's own path. Shows "not indexed" (not a fabricated count) when no match is found. Three commands registered: set API key, check connection, start server.
- **What was explicitly NOT done, and why**: no search, hover provider, CodeLens, references/impact/similar-code panels, or MCP integration -- all later phases (2 and 3) in the client-strategy plan, each its own thin caller of an already-built endpoint, not built ahead of need; no Marketplace packaging/publishing (Phase 4); no automated test suite for the extension itself -- Phase 1 has very little pure logic to unit-test yet (mostly VS Code API orchestration), and the real verification available in this environment (see below) was judged more valuable than scaffolding a test runner around trivial logic this early.
- **What was verified, and what could NOT be**: strict `tsc --noEmit -p ./` compiles with zero type errors. Beyond that, a real, genuine end-to-end integration check was run (not merely "it compiles"): a disposable sample repository was indexed into a disposable SQLite database using THIS backend's own real CLI (`codepulse index`), a real API key was created against that same database (`codepulse.auth.create_api_key`), a real `codepulse serve` instance was started against it on a scratch port, and the extension's OWN COMPILED `out/client.js` was driven directly from a throwaway Node script against that live server -- `listRepositories()` correctly returned the one indexed repository with the right symbol count; `resolveSymbol()` correctly resolved a real line to the real `greet` symbol, and correctly returned `symbol: null` for a line with no symbol; an unauthenticated request correctly received a real 401 with the server's own `"Missing API key"` message. The disposable server process and database were both cleaned up afterward; the real `codepulse.db` was never touched. **What could NOT be verified from this CLI-only session**: the actual VS Code UI -- status bar rendering, the Command Palette entries, the `F5` Extension Development Host launch, or the "offer to start a server" prompt's real interactive behavior. That needs a real, interactive VS Code window, which this session doesn't have -- disclosed explicitly rather than claimed.
