### VS Code extension Phase 0 — `GET /v1/repositories` + `GET /v1/repositories/{repo_id}/resolve` (not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: `CODEPULSE_CLIENT_STRATEGY.md`'s own "Phase 0 — Backend prerequisites" for the VS Code extension, implemented in THIS repo per the user's explicit instruction to do so before starting the extension itself (a separate `codepulse-vscode` TypeScript package, not started). Two new, small, read-only REST endpoints; no schema, migration, or engine change.

- **`GET /v1/repositories`** (`codepulse/api/app.py`): lists repositories the CALLING KEY holds a grant for -- `RepositoryModel` rows filtered to `auth.repository_ids`, ordered by id. An unscoped key (`auth.repository_ids` empty) gets `{"repositories": []}` immediately, without a DB query -- deliberately mirrors Phase 1.2's own "no wildcard/superuser key" design (an unscoped key is authenticated but authorized for NO repository-scoped operation) rather than listing every repository in the system to anyone with a valid key. Response fields: `repository_id`, `name`, `url`, `branch`, `file_count`, `symbol_count`, `last_sync` (ISO timestamp or `null`) -- the same field set `get_repository_stats`/`get_index_status` already expose per-repository, just enumerated across every repo the key can see instead of requiring one already-known id.
- **`GET /v1/repositories/{repo_id}/resolve?path=&line=`** (`codepulse/api/app.py`): finds the `FileModel` for `(repo_id, path)` (404 if the file itself isn't in this repository), then every `SymbolModel` whose `[start_line, end_line]` range contains `line`, and returns the NARROWEST one (`min` by `(end_line - start_line, start_line)`) -- the most specific match, not merely the first one found, so a line inside both a method and its enclosing class resolves to the method. Returns `{"symbol": null}` (200, not an error) for a real file with no symbol at that exact line -- a genuine, valid outcome (a blank line, an import, a comment), never conflated with "file not found." This is a genuinely NEW capability, not a REST-only gap: neither the REST API nor any `IndexingEngine`/`CallGraphBuilder` method could previously answer "what symbol is at this file+line" at all -- every existing lookup needed a symbol id (`GET /v1/symbols/{id}`) or a file id (`GET /v1/files/{id}/symbols`, which returns every symbol in the file, not the one at a specific line), never a bare path+line pair. Implemented as a direct query in the route itself (not a new `IndexingEngine` method) since it's a plain, single-purpose lookup with no ranking/graph logic to share across interfaces yet -- if the CLI or MCP ever need the same capability, this logic should move into the shared core at that point rather than being duplicated, matching this project's established practice for small, newly-REST-only capabilities.
- **What was explicitly NOT done, and why**: no VS Code extension code was written (a separate TypeScript repo, per the roadmap's own B22 "new extension repo/package" framing and `CODEPULSE_CLIENT_STRATEGY.md`'s own Phase 1 -- out of scope for this repo entirely); no CLI or MCP exposure for either new capability (neither was named in the client-strategy plan's Phase 0, which scoped this to REST only, matching what the extension will actually call); no engine-level method was added for `resolve` (see above).
- **What was verified**: full suite **579 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 569 + 10 new, all in new `tests/test_repository_listing_and_resolve.py`: `GET /v1/repositories` returns only the calling key's own repos (isolation, verified with two repositories sharing nothing in common), the full response shape is correct, an unscoped key gets an empty list, authentication is required; `resolve` correctly picks the narrowest symbol when a line is inside both a class and a nested method (verified directly against the REAL persisted `start_line`/`end_line` ranges the Python parser actually produced, not assumed line numbers -- an initial test assumption about where a class's own range ends was WRONG on the first run, caught by the test itself, and corrected to query real ranges instead of guessing), correctly finds the class alone for a line only inside its range, correctly returns `symbol: null` for a location genuinely outside every symbol, 404s for an unknown path, 403s for an unauthorized repository, and 401s without a key. A targeted run of `test_api.py`/`test_api_authorization.py`/`test_module_structure_endpoint.py` also passed with zero regressions before the full suite confirmed the same. Real `codepulse.db` confirmed untouched throughout (every test uses a disposable temp database).
- **Exact files changed this session**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/api/app.py` (2 new routes), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).
  - Added: `tests/test_repository_listing_and_resolve.py` (10 new tests).
  - No other file touched -- confirmed via `git status --short`/`git diff --stat`.
- **Exact tests run**: `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests/test_repository_listing_and_resolve.py -q` → 10 passed (after fixing one incorrect test assumption about class line-range boundaries), ~22s; then `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests/test_api.py tests/test_api_authorization.py tests/test_module_structure_endpoint.py -q` → 45 passed, ~45s; then `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest -q` (full suite) → 579 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed, 58 warnings, ~584s.
- **What remains**: **committing this work to git** (the checkpoint commit immediately follows this document update). `CODEPULSE_CLIENT_STRATEGY.md`'s Phase 0 is now fully complete. The extension's own Phase 1 (foundation: a new `codepulse-vscode` TypeScript repo, activation, auth, status bar) has NOT been started -- it lives outside this repository entirely and needs its own explicit instruction to begin, in its own project.

### Client strategy document — `CODEPULSE_CLIENT_STRATEGY.md` (not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: a directly-requested planning deliverable -- explicitly NOT implementation ("suggest which will be better and the plan for implementing both... mention if any steps are remaining"). No code, test, or config file touched; the only change this session is the new document itself plus this progress-log entry.

- **The recommendation**: build the VS Code extension (Phase 6.2) before the Web UI (Phase 6.3). Reasoning, in the document itself in full: (1) product-identity fit -- CodePulse's own stated "local-first, agent-native" positioning matches an editor extension far more directly than a browser tab needing to somehow know which local repository it's talking about; (2) the REST API's own existing shape (symbol-by-id, repository-scoped, one-thing-at-a-time operations like references/impact/similar) already matches editor-style single-symbol UI (hover, CodeLens, a references panel) more than dashboard-style aggregate views; (3) smaller buildable-today scope -- no new auth-session model, no multi-tenancy, no hosting decision, just a thin TypeScript client over an already-tested REST/MCP surface; (4) the Web UI's own most-differentiated feature (the roadmap's own §24 language: the dependency-graph visualization) is better justified as a complement built AFTER Phase 7.1 (Postgres/team mode) gives a browser-based surface an actual multi-user reason to exist, rather than as a single-player wrapper around the same CLI.
- **Both plans were still written in full**, per the explicit "plan for implementing both" request -- each phased, each grounded in the roadmap's own literal MVP scope (§23's "command-palette search, go-to-symbol, find references, impact analysis panel, similar code panel, status-bar indexing indicator" for the extension; §24's "repository dashboard, search box, symbol detail view, dependency-graph visualization" for the Web UI; B22/B23's own DoD language quoted directly), not an independently-invented feature list.
- **Two new, real backend gaps found while grounding the plans in the actual current API surface** (verified via a fresh `grep` of every REST route, not assumed): neither client can currently enumerate which repositories are indexed (`GET /v1/repositories` does not exist -- every repo-scoped route needs an id the caller must already know); the VS Code extension specifically has no way to resolve "what symbol is at this file+line" (`GET /v1/symbols/{id}` needs a symbol id, `GET /v1/files/{id}/symbols` needs a file id -- neither accepts a path+line, and no engine method resolves by location either, only by name). Both flagged as small, self-contained, low-risk Phase 0 prerequisites in the relevant plan(s) -- neither has been built.
- **A consolidated "what's left to complete the project" list** was produced, combining: the three still-genuinely-BLOCKED roadmap items (6.2, 6.3, 7.1, 7.3 -- unchanged reasoning from the standing notice near the top of this document), the smaller already-disclosed gaps from `CODEPULSE_PRODUCT_AND_ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own Part 15 re-checked for current accuracy (CI never actually run on real infrastructure; key expiry/audit logging; no repository-deletion endpoint; in-memory-only rate limiter/job lock; on-disk index-segment persistence still not literal; the unresolved `SymbolType.MODULE` observation from the dead-code cleanup), and the two new gaps this document itself surfaced.
- **What was explicitly NOT done, and why**: no code was written for either client (an explicit read-only planning request, not an implementation one); no framework was chosen for the Web UI (the roadmap itself leaves this open, and its own §33 risk register explicitly says not to over-commit before usage data exists); the two newly-found backend gaps were documented, not built (a natural Phase 0 for whichever client is actually started next, not a silent scope expansion into this planning task).
- **What was verified**: every REST route referenced in either plan was freshly re-confirmed via `grep -n "^@app\.\(get\|post\|delete\)"` against the actual current `codepulse/api/app.py` (22 routes, all cross-checked) rather than recalled from earlier in this session; both new gap claims (`GET /v1/repositories`, resolve-by-location) were confirmed absent by direct, targeted `grep`, not assumed from a mental model of the API; the roadmap's own §23/§24/B22/B23 text was read and quoted directly from `CodePulse_Strategy_and_Roadmap.md` rather than paraphrased from memory. No pytest run was applicable (no code changed).

### Dead-code cleanup (not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: the last, lowest-priority item from the backend-gap recheck -- two genuinely dead-code items the user chose to clean up as a final pass.

- **`IndexRepoRequest.repository_id`** (`codepulse/api/app.py`): removed. Grep-confirmed before removal that `index_repository`'s body never read this field anywhere -- the route always resolves the real repository id itself via `_resolve_repo_id(repo_name, req.url)`, keyed on `url` (the exact same natural-key identity `IndexingEngine.index_directory` uses internally). No test anywhere sent this field in a request body, and `IndexRepoRequest` has no strict/`extra="forbid"` Pydantic config, so removing it is fully backward-compatible even for a hypothetical caller that still sends it (Pydantic silently ignores an unknown field by default).
- **`SymbolType.VARIABLE`/`INTERFACE`** (`codepulse/models.py`): removed. Grep-confirmed before removal that neither parser (`codepulse/parser/python.py`, `codepulse/parser/javascript.py`) ever produces either value, and neither is referenced anywhere else in the codebase or test suite (only `FUNCTION`/`METHOD`/`CLASS` are ever actually used; `MODULE` is also apparently unproduced by either parser but was NOT part of the original disclosed finding and was deliberately left untouched -- not silently expanded into, flagged here instead for a future, separately-scoped decision if ever wanted). The real `codepulse.db`'s `symbols` table was directly queried (read-only) before this change and confirmed to have zero rows of type `'variable'` or `'interface'` -- this removal affects no real historical data. `symbols.type` is a plain, DB-unconstrained `String(50)` (not a native SQL enum), so no migration is needed or possible for this change.
- **A genuine, previously-existing test-coverage gap found and closed while investigating, not itself the point of this milestone**: `IndexingEngine.hydrate_repository`'s defensive `try: SymbolType(sm.type) except ValueError` fallback (added during the "Restart-survivable lexical search" milestone, to skip rather than crash on a symbol row whose `type` doesn't parse as a known enum member) had never been directly exercised by any test since it was written -- confirmed absent from the entire test suite by direct search. Now regression-tested: a real symbol row's `type` column is set to `"variable"` (a value no CURRENT `SymbolType` member matches, exactly the shape this cleanup's own removal creates for any stray legacy data) via direct SQL, and a fresh engine construction against that database is confirmed to skip it cleanly (an empty `bm25_index`, not a crash) rather than raise.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: `SymbolType.MODULE` (see above -- a related-but-separate observation, not part of what was asked); no migration was written or needed (the column was never a native enum, so nothing at the schema level depended on the Python enum's exact member set); no other `IndexRepoRequest` field was touched.
- **What was verified**: full suite **569 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 568 + 1 new (the `hydrate_repository` skip-path regression test). A targeted run of every test file that constructs a `Symbol`/`SymbolType` or exercises the `/v1/repositories/index` route (parser tests, ranker/chunker tests, lexical/embedding-persistence tests, inheritance-graph tests, job-endpoint tests, ingestion-safety tests, authorization tests) was run first and passed with zero regressions, before the full suite confirmed the same. Real `codepulse.db` confirmed untouched (queried read-only exactly once, to verify zero affected rows before the code change -- never written to).

### Query-side embedding-failure degradation (not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: the third finding from the same backend-gap recheck as the entry below, tackled next per the user's explicit request.

- **`codepulse/indexer/engine.py`'s `IndexingEngine.search()`**: a new private helper, `_encode_query_or_degrade(query)`, wraps `self.embedder.encode(query)` in `try/except EmbeddingUnavailableError`, logs a `logger.warning` naming the search_type, and returns `None` on failure instead of letting the exception propagate. Used by the `"hybrid"` and `"rrf"` branches only -- each treats a `None` return as "no vector candidates this call" (`vector_res = []`) and proceeds to fuse using BM25 results alone. `fuse`/`fuse_rrf` (`codepulse/search/ranker.py`) already handle an empty `vector_results` list correctly with zero changes needed -- the exact same code path a repository with zero embeddings already exercises (verified by inspection before writing any new code, not assumed).
- **`"semantic"` deliberately excluded from this treatment** -- a pure semantic request has no other candidate source to fall back to; catching the failure and returning an empty list there would silently misrepresent "the embedding model is broken" as "your query genuinely matched nothing," which this project's own "no fabricated/misleading success" discipline (CLAUDE.md's "no silent pseudo-embedding fallback" invariant, extended here to its logical read-side counterpart) does not permit. Left raising uncaught, unchanged.
- **`POST /v1/search`** (`codepulse/api/app.py`): the one caller that could still see the uncaught `"semantic"` exception now wraps `indexing_engine.search(...)` in `try/except EmbeddingUnavailableError`, raising `HTTPException(status_code=503, detail=...)` with a clear, specific message (naming the actual `search_type` that failed and suggesting `search_type="keyword"` as a working alternative) -- reshaped into the standard `{"error": {...}}` envelope automatically by the existing, centrally-registered exception handler (Phase 1.6), with zero new error-shaping code needed. `POST /v1/ide-search` needed no equivalent change -- it always calls `search_type="hybrid"`, which no longer raises at all after the engine-level fix.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: the CLI (`codepulse search`) and MCP (`search_code` tool) both call `IndexingEngine.search()` directly and inherit the `hybrid`/`rrf` graceful-degradation fix automatically, for free, with zero interface-specific code -- exactly the benefit of fixing this once in the shared intelligence core rather than per-interface. Neither needed a semantic-specific catch added: an uncaught `EmbeddingUnavailableError` in the CLI already surfaces its own real, specific message via a normal Python traceback (not the same "opaque 500" problem REST's generic exception handler created), and MCP's `search_code` tool was left as-is since the user's request and the original recheck both centered on the REST-facing "opaque 500" symptom specifically.
- **What was verified**: full suite **568 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 560 + 8 new, all in new `tests/test_query_embedding_degradation.py`. Uses the SAME genuinely-broken-embedder trick already established in `tests/test_embedding_persistence.py` (`CodeEmbedder(model_name="definitely-not-a-real-model-xyz-789", ...)`, an unresolvable model name that really does raise `EmbeddingUnavailableError` on load -- not a mock standing in for the real failure). A real repository is indexed with a WORKING embedder first (so `bm25_index` has genuine content), then a second engine -- with the broken embedder -- is constructed against the same database, mirroring a restart; its `bm25_index` is correctly rehydrated from durable rows (the earlier "restart-survivable lexical search" fix), so only the embedder is actually broken, isolating exactly the failure mode under test. Confirmed: hybrid/rrf searches still return real, keyword-derived results with a genuinely broken embedder (both at the engine level and through a real `POST /v1/search` call); semantic search still raises `EmbeddingUnavailableError` at the engine level (explicitly locked in, not merely left untested) and returns a real 503 with a clear message through REST; keyword search is entirely unaffected either way. Real `codepulse.db` confirmed untouched throughout (every test uses a disposable temp database).

### Autocomplete + repository-stats repository-isolation fixes (not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: the first two findings from a fresh backend-gap RECHECK, run after the original 6-item audit was fully closed out. Per CLAUDE.md §13's own "reconstruct actual state from Git/files/tests, don't trust previous summaries without verification" discipline, this recheck re-read the live code directly rather than trusting this document's own prior "Known Issues" bullets -- which paid off: one bullet (`/health/live`/`ready` "doesn't exist yet") turned out to be stale documentation (those endpoints have existed since Phase 7.4), and two others (`/v1/autocomplete`, `get_repository_stats`'s `languages` breakdown) turned out to be real, live, currently-exploitable cross-repository leaks that had sat undisclosed-as-a-security-issue in this document's own Known Issues section since Phase 0.7, filed only as "not repository-scoped" data-completeness notes, never flagged with the same severity Phase 3.4/5.1's own leak fixes received.

- **`GET /v1/autocomplete`** (`codepulse/api/app.py`): previously had ZERO repository scoping -- no `auth.authorize_repository`/`authorize_search_scope` call, and its data source (`indexing_engine.symbols_by_id`) is a dict shared across every repository this engine process has ever indexed. A key scoped only to repository A could see suggestions (symbol names, types, file paths, signatures) from every other repository in memory, including ones it holds no grant for whatsoever.
- **A real design fork surfaced during investigation, presented to the user explicitly rather than silently picked**: autocomplete has been a documented, deliberately-tested "no repository concept" endpoint since Phase 1.2 (`tests/test_api_authorization.py`'s own module docstring groups it with `feedback`/ingestion as endpoints that "succeed with an unscoped key") -- roughly 15 existing test call sites across `test_api.py`, `test_api_authorization.py`, `test_cors.py`, `test_error_format.py`, and `test_rate_limit_middleware.py` call it with a completely unscoped key expecting 200. Two real options existed: (a) require an explicit `repository_id` and `authorize_search_scope`, matching `/v1/search`'s stricter pattern, which would have required updating every one of those ~15 call sites; or (b) filter results by `auth.repository_ids` ONLY when that set is non-empty, leaving an unscoped key's existing, tested "blended across every repo" behavior completely untouched. **User chose (b)** -- the additive, minimal-blast-radius fix that closes the actual harmful case (a SCOPED key leaking into repos it has no grant for) without reversing a separate, deliberate Phase 1.2 design decision about unscoped keys that nobody asked to revisit.
- **`GET /v1/repositories/{repo_id}/stats`** (`codepulse/api/app.py`): the route itself was already correctly gated (`auth.authorize_repository(repo_id)`), but its `languages` breakdown iterated `indexing_engine.parse_results.values()` unfiltered -- a caller correctly authorized for repo A, asking for repo A's own stats, got a file/symbol/language distribution blended with every OTHER repository this engine has parsed in-process. Fixed by filtering to the same `"{repo_id}::"` key-prefix scoping `IndexingEngine.analyze_module_structure` already established for this exact dict (Phase 4.3) -- no new filtering mechanism invented, reused an existing, already-proven pattern.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: `get_index_status`'s equivalent fallback-branch leak (named in the same original Phase 0.7 disclosure) was investigated but left alone -- that branch only triggers when `repo_id` has no matching `RepositoryModel` row, which is unreachable through normal authorized use (a key can only ever be granted a `repository_id` that existed at grant time, and no repository-deletion endpoint exists to make one vanish afterward); fixing genuinely dead code was judged out of scope for this pass. The query-side `EmbeddingUnavailableError` degradation gap and the stale-documentation findings from the same recheck were explicitly deferred -- the user chose "both repo-isolation leaks" specifically, not the full recheck list.
- **What was verified**: full suite **560 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 553 + 7 new, all in new `tests/test_autocomplete_and_stats_isolation.py` (a scoped key never sees a different repository's autocomplete suggestions; a scoped key sees its own repository's suggestions correctly; a key scoped to BOTH repositories sees both; an unscoped key's blended-across-all-repos behavior is explicitly confirmed unchanged; the stats `languages` breakdown never leaks a third, differently-languaged repository indexed into the same shared engine; the breakdown is independently correct for each of two repositories; the stats route still correctly 403s for an unauthorized repo_id). Every pre-existing test referencing autocomplete or stats across 5 other test files was re-run unmodified and passed, directly confirming the unscoped-key convention was genuinely preserved, not merely reasoned about. Real `codepulse.db` confirmed untouched throughout (every test uses a disposable temp/isolated database).
- **Exact files changed this session**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/api/app.py` (autocomplete's scoping filter + comment rewrite; stats' `languages` loop scoped by key prefix), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).
  - Added: `tests/test_autocomplete_and_stats_isolation.py` (7 new tests).
  - No other file touched -- confirmed via `git status --short`/`git diff --stat`.
- **Exact tests run**: `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests/test_autocomplete_and_stats_isolation.py -q` → 7 passed, ~81s; then `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests/test_api.py tests/test_api_authorization.py tests/test_cors.py tests/test_error_format.py tests/test_no_fabricated_metrics.py tests/test_rate_limit_middleware.py -q` → 73 passed, ~58s (zero regressions in every autocomplete/stats-referencing test); then `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest -q` (full suite) → 560 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed, 58 warnings, ~744s.
- **What remains**: **committing this work to git** (the checkpoint commit immediately follows this document update). From the same recheck, NOT yet acted on (deferred by explicit user choice, not forgotten): (1) query-side `EmbeddingUnavailableError` in `IndexingEngine.search()`'s semantic/rrf/hybrid branches degrades to an opaque 500 instead of falling back to keyword-only search; (2) `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md`'s own Known Issues section has several stale entries (the `/health/live`/`ready` "doesn't exist" bullet chief among them) that should be corrected; (3) low-priority dead code (`IndexRepoRequest.repository_id`, unused `SymbolType.VARIABLE`/`INTERFACE` enum values).

### Job-crash reaper (not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: the last item from the original backend-gap audit, closing out that entire list (the other five: `VectorIndex.remove_repo`, API key management, module-clustering query surface, restart-survivable lexical search, `PRAGMA foreign_keys` -- all complete, see the four entries below).

- **Root cause**: `IndexJobModel.status` moves `queued -> running -> succeeded|failed` (`codepulse/jobs.py`'s own established state machine, Phase 1.3). If the process running a job's `BackgroundTasks` coroutine crashes or is killed mid-run, nothing ever calls `mark_failed` for it -- the row stays `running` forever. Since `get_active_job` treats `queued`/`running` as "an active job already exists for this repository" (the roadmap §8 advisory lock), a permanently-stuck row would permanently refuse every future indexing attempt for that repository -- a real, previously-disclosed gap (`jobs.py`'s own module docstring has named this exact scenario as out-of-scope since Phase 1.3).
- **Deliberately a startup-time reconciliation, not a heartbeat/timeout mechanism** -- the key design decision, reasoned through explicitly rather than defaulting to the timeout-based approach the original audit sketched: this project runs indexing as in-process `BackgroundTasks` coroutines (no distributed worker pool, no persisted "which process owns this job" tracking, no heartbeat column anywhere in the schema -- matching `get_active_job`'s own existing "this project runs indexing as in-process background tasks, not multiple concurrent worker processes" framing). In that architecture, a job still `queued`/`running` at the exact moment a NEW `IndexingEngine` is constructed can ONLY be a leftover from a previous process that no longer exists -- the current process couldn't have created it (it just started), and no other process could be concurrently progressing it. A timeout heuristic would need a new column, a migration, and an arbitrary staleness threshold that either reaps a genuinely slow-but-healthy job too early or a genuinely crashed one too late; this mechanism needs neither and cannot produce a false positive for this architecture's actual failure mode.
- **`codepulse.jobs.reap_stale_jobs(storage) -> list[int]`** (new function): finds every `IndexJob` row in `ACTIVE_STATUSES` (`queued`/`running`) and transitions each through the EXISTING, already-tested `mark_failed` (not a hand-rolled column update) -- reuses that function's own `finished_at`/`error_message` handling and its existing `indexing_jobs_total{status="failed"}` metric increment, so a reaped job is counted exactly like any other real failure, never a separate, uncounted code path. Returns the reaped ids (for logging/testing, not a public API).
- **Wired into `IndexingEngine.__init__`** (`codepulse/indexer/engine.py`), immediately after the existing `hydrate_all()` call, with the identical fail-open philosophy (wrapped in `try`/`except Exception`, logged as a warning, never fatal to engine startup) -- called once per engine construction, the same timing `hydrate_all` already established for "reconcile whatever happened before this process began."
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: no new schema column or migration (this reaper needs no heartbeat/timestamp field, per the design reasoning above); no cancel-endpoint/flow (still doesn't exist, unrelated -- a reaped job's terminal state is `failed`, exactly as if `run_indexing`'s own try/except had caught a real exception, not a new `cancelled` semantic); no REST/CLI/MCP surface changes (`GET /v1/jobs/{job_id}` already correctly displays a `failed` job's `error_message` -- a reaped job is indistinguishable in shape from any other failure, only its message text discloses the reaping).
- **What was verified**: full suite **553 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 546 + 7 new (6 unit-level tests in `tests/test_index_jobs.py`: a running job is reaped, a queued job is reaped, terminal jobs are left untouched, an empty job table is a safe no-op, the advisory lock is genuinely reclaimed after reaping, multiple repositories/jobs are all reaped correctly in one call; 1 end-to-end integration test in `tests/test_restart_idempotency.py`, using that file's own established Restart-Model-B convention and shared real-embedder fixture, proving `IndexingEngine.__init__` genuinely calls this on construction, not just the standalone function in isolation). A manual sanity check (construct a `running` job, call `reap_stale_jobs`, confirm `status="failed"` with the expected message) was run before the test suite. Real `codepulse.db` confirmed untouched throughout (every test uses a disposable temp database).
- **Exact files changed this session**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/jobs.py` (`reap_stale_jobs` + module docstring update), `codepulse/indexer/engine.py` (import + wired call in `__init__`), `tests/test_index_jobs.py` (6 new tests + import), `tests/test_restart_idempotency.py` (1 new test + module docstring update), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).
  - No new file was added this milestone.
  - No other file touched -- confirmed via `git status --short`/`git diff --stat`.
- **Exact tests run**: `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests/test_index_jobs.py tests/test_restart_idempotency.py tests/test_job_endpoints.py -q` → 36 passed, ~59s; then `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest -q` (full suite) → 553 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed, 57 warnings, ~804s.
- **What remains**: **committing this work to git** (the checkpoint commit immediately follows this document update). This closes out the entire original backend-gap audit list -- every item is now COMPLETE.

### `PRAGMA foreign_keys` enforcement (not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: the final item from the original backend-gap audit list the user has been working through one at a time (the only remaining item, a job-crash reaper, was never explicitly requested -- see "Exact Next Action" below).

- **Root cause**: SQLite disables foreign-key constraint enforcement PER CONNECTION by default -- declaring `ForeignKey(..., ondelete="CASCADE")` on a `Column` does nothing on its own unless `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` is executed on every new connection. This project's schema declares `ondelete="CASCADE"` on 8 columns (`FileModel.repo_id`, `SymbolModel.file_id`, `CallEdge.repo_id`, `EmbeddingModel.symbol_id`, `APIKeyRepositoryModel.api_key_id`/`repo_id`, `IndexJobModel.repo_id`, `RepositorySnapshotModel.repo_id`) -- every one of them was silently inert until this fix.
- **Investigation before implementing, to bound the actual risk**: grepped for every `session.delete(...)` call site in production code (exactly two: `_purge_stale_entries`'s `FileModel` deletion and `_diff_and_purge_file_symbols`'s `SymbolModel` deletion, both in `codepulse/indexer/engine.py`) and checked every `relationship()` declaration's own cascade setting. Found `RepositoryModel.files`, `FileModel.symbols`, and `SymbolModel.embeddings` already had `cascade="all, delete-orphan"` at the SQLAlchemy ORM level -- this cascades correctly in Python regardless of the SQLite-level pragma, so both real deletion call sites were ALREADY safe and correct before this fix. `CallEdge`, `APIKeyRepositoryModel`, `IndexJobModel`, and `RepositorySnapshotModel` have NO equivalent ORM-level relationship providing cascade -- only the inert DB-level clause -- meaning their rows would have silently survived (orphaned) a parent's ORM-level deletion. In practice this had zero USER-VISIBLE impact today, since no repository-deletion or API-key-hard-delete endpoint exists anywhere in this codebase (confirmed, matching the pre-existing Known Issues disclosure) -- but it was a real landmine for whenever one is eventually added, and is exactly the class of "correctness gap invisible today, real the moment a new feature exercises it" this project has repeatedly found and fixed proactively.
- **Implementation** (`codepulse/storage/db.py::StorageManager.__init__`): a SQLAlchemy `event.listens_for(self.engine, "connect")` listener runs `cursor.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON")` on every new DBAPI connection. Deliberately scoped to the INSTANCE (`self.engine`), not the global `sqlalchemy.engine.Engine` class -- this applies to every connection the application makes through any `StorageManager`, while leaving Alembic's own, entirely separate `engine_from_config(...)` connection (`alembic/env.py`) completely untouched, eliminating any interaction with this project's own hard-won migration-safety lessons (three prior `create_all()`-alongside-Alembic incidents, unrelated cause but the same "be careful near migrations" instinct applied here). Guarded with `if self.engine.dialect.name != "sqlite": return` -- a no-op for a hypothetical future Postgres backend (Phase 7.1, not implemented), which enforces foreign keys by default already and has no such pragma.
- **Manually verified before running the test suite** (not assumed): a disposable in-memory `StorageManager` confirmed `PRAGMA foreign_keys` reads back `1`, and that inserting a `FileModel` row with a nonexistent `repo_id` genuinely raises `IntegrityError` -- proving the fix is real before spending ~10 minutes on the full suite.
- **Full suite re-run with ZERO changes needed anywhere else**: every one of the 540 pre-existing tests passed completely unmodified -- no test fixture anywhere in this project's suite violated FK insertion ordering or relied on an orphaned-row scenario the fix now prevents. This was verified empirically (the real risk this milestone carried), not merely asserted.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: no changes to any `relationship()`'s own ORM-level cascade settings (already correct); no new migration (this is a connection-level pragma, not a schema change -- the `ondelete="CASCADE"` DDL clauses already existed in every relevant migration); no repository-deletion or API-key-hard-delete endpoint was added (still doesn't exist -- this fix only makes the SCHEMA correctly enforce integrity whenever such an endpoint is eventually built, it doesn't build one); the job-crash reaper (the one remaining item from the original audit) was not started, since it was never explicitly requested this session.
- **What was verified**: full suite **546 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 540 + 6 new, all in new `tests/test_foreign_key_enforcement.py`: the pragma reads back `1` for every `StorageManager` connection; an orphan `FileModel`/`SymbolModel` insert is genuinely rejected with `IntegrityError`; deleting a `RepositoryModel` row now genuinely cascade-deletes its `CallEdge` rows via SQLite's own `ON DELETE CASCADE` (the concrete, previously-inert gap this fix closes, proven directly -- delete the repository via plain ORM `session.delete()`, no manual `CallEdge` cleanup, confirm the edge is actually gone); the pre-existing `FileModel`→`SymbolModel` ORM-level cascade still works correctly, unaffected by the pragma being added alongside it; a real Alembic upgrade-to-head/downgrade-to-base round trip against a fresh database, proving migration execution is genuinely unaffected by this change (not merely "the listener is attached to a different object" reasoning alone). Real `codepulse.db` confirmed untouched throughout (every test uses a disposable temp/in-memory database).
- **Exact files changed this session**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/storage/db.py` (`event` import + the `connect`-event listener in `StorageManager.__init__`), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).
  - Added: `tests/test_foreign_key_enforcement.py` (6 new tests).
  - No other file touched -- confirmed via `git status --short`/`git diff --stat`.
- **Exact tests run**: `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests/test_foreign_key_enforcement.py -q` → 6 passed, ~2s; then `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest -q` (full suite, twice -- once before adding the new test file to confirm zero regressions from the pragma alone, once after) → 540 passed/1 skipped, then 546 passed/1 skipped, both 0 failed.
- **What remains**: **committing this work to git** (the checkpoint commit immediately follows this document update). The one remaining backend-gap-audit item (a job-crash reaper for a `running` `IndexJob` whose background task was killed by a real process crash) has NOT been requested by the user this session and is NOT started.

### Restart-survivable lexical search via SQL rehydration (not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: item 6 from the same backend-gap audit as the three entries below. Before implementing, a fresh, code-verified investigation corrected the original audit framing ("search indexes are in-memory only, full re-embed on every restart"): `IndexingEngine.__init__` already calls `hydrate_all()`, which rebuilds `vector_index` from the durable `code_embeddings` SQL table WITHOUT ever calling the embedding model (Phase 0.5, unchanged) -- semantic search already survived a restart correctly. Only `bm25_index` had zero durable backing at all (`tests/test_restart_idempotency.py`'s own pre-existing "BM25 index has no backing table and must not survive restart" assertion). Presented to the user as an explicit choice between (a) rehydrating the lexical index from already-durable SQL rows at startup, or (b) true on-disk Tantivy/sqlite-vec index-segment persistence (new config for where index files live, direct tantivy-py on-disk API verification needed, backup/restore doc interaction) -- the user chose (a), the lower-risk option.

- **`IndexingEngine.hydrate_repository`/`hydrate_all` extended** (`codepulse/indexer/engine.py`): after the existing vector-hydration step, a new step queries every `SymbolModel`/`FileModel` row for the repository (not just ones with an embedding -- matches `index_directory()`'s own behavior of adding every symbol to `bm25_index` regardless of embedding success, for degraded-mode keyword search), reconstructs a `Symbol` object per row, and calls `self.bm25_index.add_symbol(doc_id, symbol_obj, repo_id=...)` using the exact same `f"{repo_id}::{file_path}::{name}"` doc_id format `index_directory()` uses -- then one `bm25_index.build()` call. No re-parsing, no chunking, no embedding-model call -- pure re-tokenization of text already sitting durably in `symbols`/`files`. A malformed `type` value (defensive, not currently reachable -- every parser only ever produces valid `SymbolType` values) is skipped and logged, mirroring the embedding-hydration loop's own existing `except`/`continue`/`logger.warning` pattern.
- **`SymbolModel`/`FileModel` remain the actual source of truth throughout** -- `bm25_index` is still a "derived, rebuildable search artifact" exactly as its own class docstring has always said, just rebuilt from SQL at engine-startup time now, in addition to at `index_directory()` time. `codepulse/search/bm25.py`'s class docstring updated to describe this precisely (there are still NO on-disk Tantivy index segments -- this is SQL-backed rehydration, not literal index-file persistence).
- **The call graph and `symbols_by_id` remain genuinely NOT durable, unchanged** -- rebuilding either needs the full parse-result/import-resolution machinery `index_directory()` owns, not a plain row-to-object mapping the way symbols are; still requires a fresh `index_directory()` call after a restart, exactly as before. Not this milestone's scope (a separate, larger, already-disclosed Phase 3.1/3.5 limitation).
- **`docs/BACKUP_RESTORE.md` updated** to correct its own pre-existing, slightly-optimistic wording (it already described `hydrate_all`/`hydrate_repository` as rebuilding "the in-memory lexical/vector index segments," which was only true for vectors until this change) and to precisely state what changed: still no literal on-disk index-segment persistence, but the PRACTICAL restart gap (keyword search returning nothing until a fresh index) is now closed by SQL rehydration instead.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: no true on-disk Tantivy/sqlite-vec persistence (the user's own explicit choice, disclosed above); no changes to `index_directory()`'s own indexing path (only the startup hydration path changed); the call graph's restart-survivability (Phase 3.1's own disclosed, separate, larger limitation) is unchanged; `hydrate_repository`/`hydrate_all` remain NOT safe to call more than once per engine instance (neither `bm25_index.add_symbol` nor the pre-existing `vector_index.add_batch` dedup against an already-hydrated entry) -- unchanged from before this addition (only ever called once, from `__init__`, in production) but now disclosed explicitly in a new test's own docstring and in Known Issues below, since it wasn't previously worth documenting when only the vector half existed.
- **A pre-existing test's expected behavior corrected, deliberately, not weakened**: `tests/test_restart_idempotency.py::test_restart_restores_semantic_state_but_not_lexical_or_graph_state` renamed to `test_restart_restores_semantic_and_lexical_state_but_not_the_call_graph` and its assertions updated (`bm25_index.num_docs == 0`/`keyword_results == []` after restart → now asserts the document count is rehydrated and keyword search actually returns results) -- a real, intentional behavior change this milestone exists to make, exactly the CLAUDE.md §9 carve-out for updating an assertion when the underlying fact it checks genuinely and correctly changes.
- **What was verified**: full suite **540 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 536 + 4 new (`tests/test_embedding_persistence.py`: fresh-engine lexical hydration populates `bm25_index`; keyword search works immediately after hydration alone, no re-index; repository isolation holds for hydrated lexical search across two repositories sharing identical symbol names; `hydrate_repository`'s own return value reports the correct `hydrated_symbols` count). The one pre-existing test whose expected behavior changed was updated, not counted as new. Real `codepulse.db` confirmed untouched throughout (every test uses a disposable temp database).
- **Exact files changed this session**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/indexer/engine.py` (`hydrate_repository`/`hydrate_all` extended, `SymbolType` import added), `codepulse/search/bm25.py` (class docstring updated), `docs/BACKUP_RESTORE.md` (corrected/clarified restart-hydration wording), `tests/test_restart_idempotency.py` (one test renamed + assertions updated + module docstring updated), `tests/test_embedding_persistence.py` (4 new tests), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).
  - No new file was added this milestone.
  - No other file touched -- confirmed via `git status --short`/`git diff --stat`.
- **Exact tests run**: `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests/test_restart_idempotency.py tests/test_embedding_persistence.py tests/test_tantivy_lexical_index.py tests/test_repository_scoped_search.py -q` → 53 passed, ~121s; then `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests/test_embedding_persistence.py -q` → 11 passed, ~41s (after a test fix); then `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest -q` (full suite) → 540 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed, 57 warnings, ~642s.

### Module-clustering query surface — REST + CLI + MCP (not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: item 5 from the same backend-gap audit as the two entries below, tackled next per the user's explicit choice -- and explicitly across **all three interfaces at once** (REST + CLI + MCP), not REST-only, per the user's own answer when asked to choose the scope. Phase 4.3 built `ModuleGraphBuilder`/`IndexingEngine.analyze_module_structure` as a fully tested, working capability with a DELIBERATE "library/shared-core only" scope boundary -- that milestone's own docstring says outright *"no REST route, CLI command, or MCP tool calls this yet... a future one can, through this exact method, without duplicating any of `ModuleGraphBuilder`'s own construction logic."* This milestone is that future caller, for all three interfaces.

- **No changes to `IndexingEngine` or `ModuleGraphBuilder` themselves** -- every new interface is a genuinely thin wrapper calling `engine.analyze_module_structure(repo_id)` and returning `{"clusters": ..., "ranked_by_inbound": ..., "ranked_by_outbound": ...}` (`.clusters`, `.rank_by_inbound()`, `.rank_by_outbound()` -- all pre-existing, unmodified `ModuleGraphBuilder` public members), directly regression-tested to match a direct engine call byte-for-byte in all three interfaces.

Returns an empty graph (never an error) for a repository with no cached parse results this process lifetime, matching `impact-analysis`/`references`'s own established restart-behavior contract.
- **CLI**: `codepulse modules <path>` (`codepulse/cli.py`, new `cmd_modules`), following the exact same established pattern every other read-path command uses (`index_directory` then delegate to the shared engine method); Rich tree/table display by default, `--json` for scripting.

Explicitly disclosed as going beyond §13's own literal "7 named tools" list -- both in the module's own docstring and in the updated test that used to assert "exactly the seven named tools" (renamed to "exactly the eight," per this project's own convention of updating an assertion's name/text when the underlying fact it asserts genuinely changes, not weakening it).
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: no ranking/clustering logic added anywhere outside `ModuleGraphBuilder` (unchanged, per Phase 4.3's own scope); no `--csv`/visual-graph rendering in the CLI (out of scope, a plain tree/table matches every other CLI command's existing style); the remaining backend-gap-audit items (on-disk index persistence, `PRAGMA foreign_keys`, job-crash reaper) were explicitly deferred, per the user's own choice of "this one next."

### API key management — `POST`/`GET`/`DELETE /v1/admin/keys` + `codepulse.auth.revoke_api_key` (not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: items 3 and 4 from the same backend-gap audit as the entry below, tackled next per the user's explicit choice. §15's own literal minimal-viable role model states `admin` means *"all + manage keys/repos"* -- before this milestone, that role had nothing to actually gate: `create_api_key` was a Python-function-only operation (no REST/CLI surface at all), and `revoke_api_key` did not exist as a function anywhere (only the `APIKeyModel.revoked_at` schema column existed, disclosed as missing since Phase 1.1).

- **`codepulse/auth.py` additions**: `APIKeyGrant`/`APIKeySummary` (frozen dataclasses, non-secret views -- never expose the raw key or its hash), `get_api_key_repository_ids(storage, key_id)` (returns `None` for an unknown key vs. `[]` for a real but unscoped one -- the distinction a REST endpoint needs to return 404 vs. an authorization decision), `list_api_keys_for_repositories(storage, repository_ids)` (every key holding at least one grant among the given repositories, each with its FULL, unfiltered grant list -- filtering to what a specific caller may see is explicitly the caller's job, documented on the function itself), and `revoke_api_key(storage, key_id)` (sets `revoked_at` if not already set, idempotent -- calling it again on an already-revoked key leaves the original timestamp untouched -- returns `None` for an unknown key). `verify_api_key` already respected `revoked_at` defensively since Phase 1.1, so a revoked key stops authenticating immediately once this runs; no change needed there.
- **`codepulse/api/app.py` additions**: `POST /v1/admin/keys` (create, 201, returns the raw key exactly once -- same one-time-reveal lifecycle `CreatedAPIKey` has always documented), `GET /v1/admin/keys` (list), `DELETE /v1/admin/keys/{key_id}` (revoke, idempotent, 404 for an unknown id). All three are thin wrappers over the `codepulse.auth` functions above, matching this API's established "logic lives outside app.py" pattern.
- **Authorization model, deliberately simple and symmetric** (no "key owner" concept exists in the schema -- adding one would need its own migration, judged out of this addition's scope): creating a key scoped to specific repositories, or revoking a key holding any grant, both require the caller to hold `admin` role on **every** repository involved -- never a subset, so one admin can never silently create or revoke access to a repository a *different* admin actually administers. An unscoped key (`repository_ids: []`) has no specific repository to check `admin`-on; for that one case, the caller must hold `admin` role on at least one repository somewhere (proving it is genuinely an admin-role key, not merely authenticated) -- a new small `_require_admin_somewhere` helper, used by both the create and revoke routes.
- **`GET /v1/admin/keys`'s own disclosure boundary**: a key is listed if it shares at least one `admin`-held repository with the caller, but its `grants` field is filtered down to ONLY the repositories the caller itself administers -- a key visible via repo A that also happens to be granted on repo B (which the caller has no rights over) never discloses that B grant to this caller. Directly regression-tested (`test_list_keys_never_discloses_a_visible_keys_grants_on_repos_the_caller_does_not_administer`).
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: no new `created_by`/owner column or migration (see above); no CLI (`codepulse keys ...`) or MCP exposure -- REST only, matching exactly what the user asked for and what the backend-gap audit itemized; no key expiry or audit logging (still open, disclosed, unrelated gaps); `create_api_key`/`verify_api_key` themselves are unchanged.
- **A mid-session connectivity interruption and full re-verification**: the first full-suite run after this change was killed (background process terminated by a connectivity issue, not a test failure) before it could report a result. Per CLAUDE.md §10's "never assume the next context remembers the conversation" / §13's "reconstruct actual state from Git, files, tests" discipline, the working tree was re-inspected from scratch (`git status`/`git diff --stat` confirmed exactly the expected 4 files, nothing lost or partially applied) and every test re-run cleanly in the foreground before this entry was written -- see "What was verified" below for the actual, confirmed numbers.
- **What was verified**: 84/84 passed on a targeted run (`tests/test_api_key_management.py` + `tests/test_api_keys.py` + `tests/test_rbac.py` + `tests/test_api_authorization.py`, confirming no regression in the existing authorization/RBAC surface this addition sits directly next to), then the full suite **530 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 504 + 26 (8 new unit tests in `tests/test_api_keys.py` for the new `codepulse.auth` functions; 18 new REST-level tests in new `tests/test_api_key_management.py` covering create/list/revoke authorization boundaries, the grant-disclosure filter, idempotent revoke, 404/401/403 paths, and end-to-end proof that a revoked key's raw value genuinely stops authenticating). Real `codepulse.db` confirmed untouched throughout (every test uses a disposable temp or `isolated_app`-monkeypatched database).
- **Exact files changed this session**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/api/app.py` (3 new routes + `CreateAPIKeyRequest` + `_require_admin_somewhere` helper + import), `codepulse/auth.py` (`APIKeyGrant`/`APIKeySummary`/3 new functions + docstring update), `tests/test_api_keys.py` (8 new unit tests + import update), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).

### Backend hardening — `VectorIndex.remove_repo` + `StorageManager` Alembic-race guard (not a roadmap sprint item)

**Scope**: the first two items from a fresh, code-verified "developer-ready polish" backend-gap audit (grep-confirmed against the actual current source, not recalled from this document alone) that the user explicitly chose to tackle first, out of a larger prioritized list presented and not yet all acted on (see "Known Issues"/"Deferred Work" below for the remaining items: API key management endpoints, key revocation wiring, module-clustering query surface, on-disk search-index persistence, `PRAGMA foreign_keys`, job-crash reaper).

- **`VectorIndex.remove_repo(repo_id)`** (`codepulse/search/vector.py`): new method, mirroring `BM25Index.remove_repo`'s exact semantics -- finds every currently-indexed doc_id whose stored `metadata["repo_id"]` matches, then delegates to the existing `remove_docs` for the actual rowid/vectors-array bookkeeping. Closes a real, previously-disclosed gap open since Phase 2.2: `add`/`add_batch` are pure-append with no dedup-on-add, and until now there was no bulk "delete everything for this repo_id" operation the way `BM25Index` has always had -- so a FULL (non-incremental) re-index of an already-indexed repository silently accumulated duplicate vector entries forever, degrading semantic/hybrid search quality over repeated re-indexing. **Wired into `IndexingEngine.index_directory()`** (`codepulse/indexer/engine.py`, step 4, immediately before the vector-index population block): `if not incremental_plan.is_incremental: self.vector_index.remove_repo(repo_id)` -- placed and guarded identically to the pre-existing `bm25_index.remove_repo` call in step 5 (an incremental run's `_purge_stale_entries`/`remove_docs` calls already remove exactly the stale entries; a whole-repo wipe there would incorrectly discard every unchanged file's embeddings). `add`/`add_batch` themselves remain intentionally pure-append -- this fix only changes what the ENGINE does around a full re-index, not the class's own low-level contract (verified by `test_known_limitation_repeated_add_of_the_same_doc_id_accumulates_duplicates`, updated but not weakened, still passing).
- **`StorageManager.create_tables()` Alembic-race guard** (`codepulse/storage/db.py`): `create_tables()` now checks `inspect(self.engine).has_table("alembic_version")` first and returns immediately (a safe no-op) if it's present -- `Base.metadata.create_all()` only ever runs on a database Alembic has NEVER touched. This is the actual root cause fix for a `create_all()`-alongside-Alembic incident that has recurred THREE times in this project's own history (Phase 1.3's `index_jobs`, Phase 3.1's `repository_snapshots`, Phase 3.4's `call_edges` -- each required manual remediation, disclosed in full in "Known technical debt" below): an ad-hoc `StorageManager()` construction against the REAL, already-migrated `codepulse.db` (e.g. importing `codepulse.api.app` without an isolated `CODEPULSE_DB_URL`) could silently create a table a pending Alembic migration hadn't created yet, racing ahead of Alembic and leaving `alembic_version` stale. **Verified this does not break the "disposable test DB" convenience every test fixture in this suite relies on** (`tests/conftest.py`'s `isolated_app`, and ~20+ other direct `StorageManager(db_url=...)` constructions against fresh temp files): a database with no `alembic_version` table yet (true for every one of those) still gets a full, zero-setup `create_all()`, completely unchanged from before this fix -- confirmed both by the full suite passing unmodified and by a new dedicated test (`test_storage_manager_still_creates_tables_on_a_genuinely_fresh_database`).
- **A direct regression test reproducing the exact incident shape** (`tests/test_migrations.py::test_storage_manager_never_races_ahead_of_a_pending_migration`): migrates a temp database only as far as `de22df2fc3ce` (the revision immediately BEFORE `c286d277ea21` added `repository_snapshots`), leaving it with a real `alembic_version` table but deliberately missing `repository_snapshots`; then constructs `StorageManager` against it and asserts the missing table is STILL missing afterward (proving `create_tables()` no longer races ahead) and that `alembic_version` is untouched. Before this fix, this exact sequence would have silently created the table -- confirmed by tracing the pre-fix code path, not merely asserted.
- **An engine-level regression test proving the vector-index fix through the real `IndexingEngine`, not just the `VectorIndex` unit tests** (`tests/test_restart_idempotency.py::test_repeated_reindex_is_idempotent_and_identifiers_stay_stable`, extended): the existing 5-iteration full-reindex loop already asserted `engine.bm25_index.num_docs` stays at exactly 3 across every iteration; a matching assertion was added for `len(engine.vector_index.doc_ids)` staying stable from iteration 0 onward (non-fixed count, since a symbol may legitimately produce more than one chunk) -- this specific assertion did not exist before and would have caught the original gap immediately.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: no changes to `IndexingEngine`'s incremental-run path (`_purge_stale_entries`, `_diff_and_purge_file_symbols`, `_rekey_renamed_files`) -- the incremental case was already correct, only the full-reindex case had this gap; no change to `BM25Index` (already correct since Phase 2.1); no migration, since neither fix touches the schema; the remaining backend-gap-audit items (API key management endpoints, key revocation wiring, module-clustering query surface, on-disk index persistence, `PRAGMA foreign_keys`, job-crash reaper) were explicitly deferred to their own future, separately-scoped work, per the user's own explicit choice of "these two first."
- **What was verified**: full suite **504 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 499 + 5 new (3 `VectorIndex.remove_repo` unit tests, 2 `StorageManager`/migration-race tests; the `test_restart_idempotency.py` extension modified an existing test rather than adding a new one); real `codepulse.db` confirmed untouched throughout (every test in this milestone uses a disposable temp database).
- **Exact files changed this session**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/search/vector.py` (`remove_repo` + docstring updates), `codepulse/indexer/engine.py` (one new guarded call + comment), `codepulse/storage/db.py` (`create_tables()` guard + `inspect` import), `tests/test_sqlite_vec_semantic_index.py` (3 new tests + 1 docstring update), `tests/test_migrations.py` (2 new tests), `tests/test_restart_idempotency.py` (1 existing test extended), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).
  - No new file was added this milestone.
  - No other file touched -- confirmed via `git status --short`/`git diff --stat`.
- **Exact tests run**: `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest -q` → 504 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed, 57 warnings, ~567s.
- **What remains**: **committing this work to git** (the checkpoint commit immediately follows this document update). The remaining backend-gap-audit items (see above) are NOT started and require their own explicit instruction to begin, per this project's one-milestone-at-a-time discipline.

### Phase 7.5 — Backup/restore documentation + CI hook

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 item 7.5 *"Backup/restore documentation; CI hook for commit-triggered incremental indexing"*.

- **New `docs/BACKUP_RESTORE.md`**: a real, VERIFIED procedure for this project's actual, fully-working local-first mode (SQLite `codepulse.db` -- confirmed `journal_mode=DELETE`, not WAL, so a single-file copy is a complete, consistent snapshot as long as no write is in-flight). Verification performed for the document itself, not merely asserted: a disposable copy of this project's OWN real `codepulse.db` was copied (simulating backup), copied again (simulating restore), and `PRAGMA integrity_check` returned `ok` with row counts and the Alembic head revision confirmed identical to the source. Also documents that restoring is operationally identical to a restart -- `IndexingEngine.hydrate_all` already rebuilds the in-memory lexical/vector indexes from durable state (Phase 0.5), so no separate re-index step is needed for previously-indexed data. The team/Postgres-mode procedure (§26) is included as CONCEPTUAL TEXT ONLY, explicitly labeled as NOT independently verified, since Phase 7.1/7.3 are blocked in this environment -- not silently presented as tested.
- **New `.github/workflows/ci.yml`**: a test job (`pip install -e ".[dev]"` + `pytest tests/ -q`) and a SEPARATE "commit-triggered incremental indexing" job that dogfoods CodePulse against its OWN repository via `python -m codepulse.cli ... update . --json` (§37 item 7.5's own literal wording), reusing Phase 6.1's real CLI command, not a new indexing code path. **Disclosed limitation, matching the Phase 5.3/6.2/6.3/7.1/7.3 pattern**: this file has been YAML-syntax-validated and every one of its own shell steps has been run locally in this exact sequence (see below) -- but it has NOT been observed actually running on GitHub's own Actions infrastructure, since no CI system exists anywhere in this repository and this environment cannot trigger a real GitHub Actions run. The workflow file itself says so, in its own header comment.
- **A real, previously-disclosed, long-broken packaging gap fixed as a genuine prerequisite, not opportunistic cleanup**: a working `pip install -e .` is what makes the CI job's very first step possible at all. Two real problems, found in sequence:
  1. `pip install -e .` has failed since Phase 2.1 (setuptools' automatic package discovery finding two top-level packages -- `alembic`, `codepulse` -- at the repo root and refusing to guess which to install). Fixed with an explicit `[tool.setuptools.packages.find]` `include = ["codepulse*"]` directive.
  2. Fixing #1 revealed a SECOND, previously-masked problem: `pydantic-settings>=2.3.0,<2.4.0` (declared since early in this project) directly conflicts with Phase 5.3's own `mcp` dependency, which genuinely requires `pydantic-settings>=2.5.2`. Confirmed by grep that `pydantic_settings` is NEVER actually imported anywhere in `codepulse/` or `tests/` -- a vestigial, unused declaration (this project's own `config.py` uses plain `pydantic.BaseModel`) -- removed entirely rather than loosened, since `mcp` already declares its own correct requirement as its own transitive dependency.
  - **Verified, not assumed**: `pip install -e . --dry-run` and `pip install -e ".[dev]" --dry-run` both now resolve completely and successfully (previously both failed outright) -- the exact commands README.md's own "Getting Started" section has told users to run since before this session, now finally working for the first time in this project's history.
- **The real self-indexing CLI command was run directly, not just included in an unverified YAML file**: `python -m codepulse.cli --db-url <disposable> update . --json` against CodePulse's own actual repository succeeded end-to-end -- 93 files, 996 symbols, 2552 chunks, 4928 call-graph edges, `embedding_status: "success"`, `degraded_mode: false` -- proving the CI job's own real command works correctly against this real codebase, not merely that the YAML parses.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: no CI actually triggered (no infrastructure to trigger it in this environment, disclosed above); no changes to `IndexingEngine`/`CallGraphBuilder`/any Phase 4-7 intelligence-core code; the team/Postgres backup procedure remains unimplemented text, not a working feature (Phase 7.1/7.3 still blocked).

### Phase 7.4 — Observability wiring

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 item 7.4 *"Observability wiring (metrics/logs/health, §18-19 finally turned on in code)"*; §18 Observability Architecture in full -- literal metric names, literal structured-log field set, literal two health endpoints; §45 backlog B27 DoD: *"`/health/ready`, `/metrics` endpoints live and accurate."*

- **Real dependency activation, not just a declaration**: `prometheus-client` and `structlog` were both declared in `pyproject.toml` since Phase 0.9 ("declared but not yet wired in") but were **not actually installed in this environment** until this milestone (confirmed: `import prometheus_client` failed before this session installed it) -- genuinely installed and genuinely imported for the first time, not merely re-affirmed.
- **New `codepulse/observability.py`**: a dedicated `CollectorRegistry` (not `prometheus_client`'s global default -- the standard, collision-safe pattern) holding every metric §18 names, spelled exactly as listed: `search_requests_total{search_type}`, `search_latency_seconds`, `search_zero_result_total`, `indexing_jobs_total{status}`, `indexing_duration_seconds`, `files_indexed_total`, `symbols_indexed_total`, `embedding_duration_seconds`, `job_queue_depth`, `api_errors_total{endpoint,status_code}`.
- **Every metric wired to a REAL, already-happening event -- never a fabricated/synthetic value** (CLAUDE.md's "no fabricated metrics" applied to observability data too): `search_requests_total`/`search_latency_seconds`/`search_zero_result_total` in `POST /v1/search` itself, around the real `indexing_engine.search()` call; `indexing_jobs_total{status}` at the exact three real state transitions in `codepulse/jobs.py`'s `mark_running`/`mark_succeeded`/`mark_failed`; `indexing_duration_seconds`/`files_indexed_total`/`symbols_indexed_total` around the real `index_directory()` call inside `/v1/repositories/index`'s background task, using `index_directory`'s own real, measured `total_files`/`total_symbols` (never estimated); `embedding_duration_seconds` around the real `SentenceTransformer.encode()` call inside `CodeEmbedder.encode()`; `api_errors_total{endpoint,status_code}` at the ONE central choke point every error response already flows through (`codepulse/api/errors.py`'s `_record_api_error`, called by all three exception handlers AND the rate-limit middleware -- no second error-counting path). `job_queue_depth` is a Gauge, deliberately recomputed FRESH from a real `IndexJobModel` count at `/metrics` scrape time (not incrementally maintained, which would risk silent drift) -- a point-in-time CURRENT value, not a running total.
- **Structured logging**: `configure_logging()` sets up `structlog` with a JSON renderer, idempotent (safe to call from multiple import sites without reconfiguring). `get_logger()` returns a bound logger; §18's own exact field set (`request_id`, `repository_id`, `job_id`, `api_key_id`, `operation`, `duration_ms`, `error_type`) is bound per call site via keyword arguments -- not hardcoded into the logger itself, since not every log line has every field (e.g. a search request has no `job_id`).
- **Health endpoints, §18's own exact two, plus `/metrics`, all carrying NO `Depends(require_api_key)`** (§21: exempt like every other `/health/*` route; `/metrics` extended the same exemption for the same reason real Prometheus scraping is never per-repository-scoped): `GET /health/live` (process up, no dependency checks). `GET /health/ready` -- §18's own exact three checks (DB reachable via a real `SELECT 1`; embedding model loaded, checked against the model's REAL lazy-load state -- verified directly that this is honestly `False` before any `encode()` call and flips to `True` immediately after one actually happens, never assumed true just because the process started; index storage writable, a real `os.access` check on the DB file's directory) -- returns 503 (not 200) when any check fails, per §18's own "a ready failure should stop new traffic/jobs."
- **What was verified, directly, not just inferred**: 11 new tests in `tests/test_observability.py`. Because `REGISTRY` is a process-lifetime singleton shared by the WHOLE test session (many other test files also hit `/v1/search`/`/v1/repositories/index`), every metric assertion checks a **DELTA** (before/after snapshot) around one specific action, never an absolute counter value -- the only assertion style that is correct and execution-order-independent for a real, shared, monotonically-increasing registry. Verified: both health endpoints and `/metrics` require no API key; `/health/ready` genuinely tracks the embedding model's lazy-load transition; `/metrics` parses as valid Prometheus exposition format (via `prometheus_client.parser`); search/indexing/error metrics all increment by exactly the expected delta for a real action; `job_queue_depth` reflects a real queued job; `configure_logging`/`get_logger` actually produce usable output.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: no changes to `IndexingEngine`'s own public method signatures (`observability` is imported lazily, inline, at each instrumentation call site -- avoiding a module-level import cycle and keeping every non-instrumented code path's import cost unchanged); no distributed tracing infrastructure (§18 itself says log lines alone should suffice "without needing distributed tracing infrastructure at this scale"); no dashboards/alerting rules (out of this milestone's literal scope -- "wiring," not a deployed observability stack); `/metrics`/`/health/*` are not yet actually scraped by anything (no Prometheus/monitoring stack runs in this environment -- the endpoints are real, tested, and correct, but unexercised by real infrastructure, exactly like Phase 5.3's MCP server being built-and-tested without yet being started as a long-running process).

### Phase 7.2 — RBAC roles + repo permission model

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 item 7.2 *"RBAC roles + repo permission model"*; §15's own literal minimal-viable model: *"roles `viewer` (search/read), `indexer` (viewer + trigger indexing), `admin` (all + manage keys/repos). Enforced at the repository-permission level, not globally."*; §30 Multi-Tenancy: *"'tenancy' = repository-scoped API keys... through Phase 7."*; §45 backlog B25 DoD: *"Viewer/indexer/admin permission boundaries tested."*

- **Architecture, matching §15's own "not globally" literally**: `role` is a new column on `APIKeyRepositoryModel` (the per-(api_key, repository) grant row) -- NOT on `APIKeyModel` itself. A single key can legitimately hold `viewer` on one repository and `admin` on another (verified directly, `test_authorize_role_is_independent_per_repository`).
- **Migration `b9014c049e33`**: adds `api_key_repositories.role` (`NOT NULL DEFAULT 'admin'`). The backfill target for EXISTING rows is deliberately `'admin'`, not `'viewer'` -- every grant created before this milestone had, in actual practice, unrestricted access (no role concept existed to restrict anything), so `'admin'` is the historically honest value, not a guess. Verified upgrade -> downgrade -> re-upgrade on a disposable copy first; the real `codepulse.db` had zero `api_keys`/`api_key_repositories` rows at migration time, so the backfill affected nothing there in practice. Applied to the real database; verified post-migration.
- **`codepulse.auth.create_api_key(..., role="viewer")`**: the NEW, safe, least-privilege default for grants created from now on -- deliberately different from the migration's own historical-backfill default, and validated against `VALID_ROLES` (`ValueError` for anything else).
- **`AuthorizedKey.authorize_role(repo_id, required_role)`** (`codepulse/api/security.py`): the role-aware counterpart to the existing `authorize_repository` (reused, unmodified, as the membership check performed first) -- ranks `viewer < indexer < admin` and 403s if the held role doesn't meet the bar. Every purely-read endpoint keeps using plain `authorize_repository` unchanged (every role, including a future stricter one, already implies read access -- a coarser but still-correct check for those routes; not retrofitted onto a dozen+ endpoints for zero behavioral gain).
- **The one existing endpoint where a role distinction is actually meaningful today**: `POST /v1/repositories/index`. A genuinely NEW repository (no existing `RepositoryModel.url` match) remains open to any authenticated key -- unchanged, matching this endpoint's own pre-existing Phase 1.2 rationale (no scope exists yet to check a not-yet-indexed path against). RE-indexing an ALREADY-KNOWN repository now requires "indexer" role (or higher) for that specific repository -- verified directly: a viewer-role key gets 403, an indexer-role key gets 202, and a key with zero grants at all can still create a brand-new repository.
- **`tests/conftest.py::make_api_key_headers` defaults to `role="admin"`** (deliberately NOT `create_api_key`'s own `"viewer"` default) -- preserves every pre-RBAC test's implicit "this key can do anything for its granted repos" assumption across the whole existing suite; only tests that specifically exercise role boundaries (`tests/test_rbac.py`) pass `role=` explicitly.
- **3 pre-existing tests needed updating, all for the SAME, well-understood reason** (not weakened -- corrected to keep testing what they were actually about): `test_migrations.py`'s two hardcoded head-revision assertions bumped to `b9014c049e33` (the same kind of update this project's history shows is needed every time a new migration becomes head); one `test_job_endpoints.py`/`test_error_format.py` test each needed an explicit `role="indexer"` grant added to a key that re-indexes a repository it itself created with an UNSCOPED key -- a pattern that made sense before RBAC existed (any key could do anything) but is now, correctly, rejected; the fix grants the SAME repository/role a real operator would provision before attempting to re-index anything, not a workaround.
- **What was verified, directly**: 10 new tests in `tests/test_rbac.py` -- invalid-role rejection, the viewer default, uniform-role application across a multi-repository grant, the full viewer/indexer/admin rank ordering (including the negative "indexer cannot satisfy admin" case), per-repository role independence, the REST-level indexer-vs-viewer re-index boundary (both directions), the new-repository-remains-open case, and a viewer-role key's read access still working correctly.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: no new REST endpoints for key/repo management (no such surface exists yet anywhere in this codebase to gate -- `create_api_key` remains a Python-function-level operation, matching its own pre-existing, still-accurate scope note); no changes to `IndexingEngine`, `CallGraphBuilder`, or any Phase 4/5/6 code; `admin`'s own literal "manage keys/repos" capability has no REST surface to enforce against yet (disclosed as a known gap in Known Issues below, not silently ignored).

### Phase 6.1 — Full CLI command set

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 item 6.1 *"Full CLI command set (update, refs, callers, callees, status, doctor)"*; §22 CLI Strategy's full literal command list (`init`, `index`, `update`, `search`, `symbol`, `refs`, `callers`, `callees`, `impact`, `similar`, `status`, `serve`, `doctor`); §45 backlog B21's DoD, which reads MORE broadly than §37's own parenthetical: *"All §22 commands implemented with `--json` output."* Built to B21's fuller standard (all of §22's commands, not just 6.1's own narrower parenthetical subset), since B21 is the sprint's own explicit backlog-level Definition of Done and does not contradict §37 6.1 -- it's a superset.

- **Architecture, consistent with every command already in `cli.py`**: `search`/`impact` already follow "construct `IndexingEngine()`, call `index_directory(path)` (idempotent/cheap for an unchanged repo, per Phase 3's own incremental machinery -- not a new mechanism), then operate" -- every new command follows the SAME pattern rather than inventing a persistent "current project" session/config file (§25's own `init`->`index`->`serve` narrative doesn't require one; `RepositoryModel.url` already serves as the natural per-path identity key an argument-driven CLI can resolve against on every invocation).
- **11 new commands, each a thin wrapper over an ALREADY-SHARED `IndexingEngine` method -- zero business logic duplicated in the CLI layer**: `refs`->`analyze_references` (Phase 4.4); `callers`/`callees`->`analyze_impact` projections (matching Phase 5.3's MCP tools' own identical approach); `similar`->`find_similar_symbols` (Phase 5.1); `symbol`/`status`->plain, disclosed DB reads (matching Phase 5.3's own disclosed `get_symbol` precedent -- a parameterized query, not a graph algorithm). `index`/`update` both call `index_directory` directly (this project's own single indexing entry point since Phase 0).
- **`init`**: writes a starter `codepulse.toml` reflecting `CodePulseConfig`'s REAL current defaults (never a hardcoded/stale template) -- "no infra required" (§25), refuses to overwrite an existing file without `--force`.
- **`status`**: reports `RepositoryModel.file_count`/`symbol_count` (real DB columns) and the most recent `RepositorySnapshotModel.commit_sha`/`indexed_at` (Phase 3.1's own snapshot table, reused as-is). Deliberately does NOT report "job history" -- local CLI indexing runs synchronously and never creates an `IndexJobModel` row (that's REST's async ingestion flow, Phase 1.3); fabricating job history for a code path that never has any would violate CLAUDE.md's "no fabricated metrics" rule.
- **`doctor`**: real checks only -- config values in effect, DB connectivity (`SELECT 1`), embedding-model load + a real encode call, and `.codepulseignore`/`.gitignore` rule loading -- each check's `ok`/`detail` reflects what actually happened, never an assumed-pass.
- **`serve`**: starts the REST API (`uvicorn.run("codepulse.api.app:app", ...)`) by default; `--mcp --api-key <key>` instead starts the MCP server over stdio via the exact same `build_mcp_server` Phase 5.3 already built and tested -- no second server-construction path.
- **`--json`**: added to every new command plus retrofitted onto the pre-existing `search`/`impact` commands, printing machine-parseable JSON (plain `print(json.dumps(...))`, not Rich-formatted) instead of the Rich table/tree presentation.
- **What was verified, directly**: 12 new tests in `tests/test_cli.py` -- every read-path command's JSON output checked against real indexed data; `init`'s file-write and overwrite-refusal behavior; `status`'s honest "not indexed" case for a path that was never indexed; and CLI-level repository isolation (two repositories sharing one database -- a real scenario for any developer using one shared local `codepulse.db` across projects -- `symbol`/`status` run against repo A's own path never see repo B's data). `serve` itself (both branches) is explicitly NOT unit-tested -- starting a real, long-running server process isn't practically unit-testable the way every other command is; both its REST and `--mcp` branches are thin wrappers over already-tested functions (`uvicorn.run`, `build_mcp_server`), manually smoke-tested during implementation, not covered by an automated test. Disclosed here, not silently skipped.
