- **A real, minor duplicate-vector-entry artifact surfaced during smoke-testing `similar`** (not a Phase 6.1 bug): the same symbol can appear more than once in `similar`'s results if it has multiple indexed chunks -- this is the EXACT same, already-disclosed Phase 5.1 `find_similar_symbols` characteristic (a pre-existing `SemanticChunker` behavior), now visible through a new presentation layer, not a new defect.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: `CallGraphBuilder` remains an unused import in `cli.py` (confirmed pre-existing before this milestone, via `git show` against an earlier commit) -- not opportunistically removed, per CLAUDE.md §12's change-scope discipline. No changes to `IndexingEngine`, `CallGraphBuilder`, `ModuleGraphBuilder`, or any REST/MCP code. No migration.

### Phase 5.3 — MCP server

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 item 5.3 *"MCP server wrapping the same tools"*; §13 MCP Strategy in full: *"yes, expose an MCP server -- but strictly as an adapter, not a parallel implementation. Each MCP tool calls the exact same internal Python functions the REST API calls."* Named tools: *"search_code, get_symbol, find_callers, find_callees, impact_analysis, find_similar, get_context"*. *"Permissions/repository selection: MCP connections are scoped to one or more repositories at connection-setup time... not per-tool-call."* *"Failure behavior: tool calls return structured errors (`{"error": "repository_not_indexed", "message": "..."}`) rather than throwing opaque exceptions."*

- **New dependency, verified before use**: the official Python MCP SDK (`mcp`, PyPI), pinned `>=1.9.0,<2.0.0` for its modern `FastMCP` server API. Installed and import-verified in this environment before writing any code against it (not assumed compatible). Declared explicitly in `pyproject.toml`, not relied on transitively from anything else.
- **New `codepulse/mcp_server.py`, `build_mcp_server(storage, engine, api_key) -> FastMCP`**: resolves the given API key to an `AuthorizedKey` (repository set) EXACTLY ONCE, via the SAME `verify_api_key` function (`codepulse/auth.py`, Phase 1.1) REST's own `require_api_key` dependency calls -- not a second authentication mechanism. Every one of the 7 tools authorizes against that SAME resolved set on every call, never re-authenticating -- satisfying §13's explicit "not per-tool-call" requirement (this is structurally different from REST's per-request `Depends(require_api_key)`, since one MCP connection serves many tool calls over its lifetime).
- **Every tool is a thin wrapper over an existing `IndexingEngine` method, zero new graph/retrieval logic**: `search_code`->`engine.search`, `impact_analysis`->`engine.analyze_impact`, `find_similar`->`engine.find_similar_symbols` (Phase 5.1), `get_context`->`engine.get_context_bundle` (Phase 5.1). `find_callers`/`find_callees` have no standalone REST/engine counterpart of their own (§13 names them as separate TOOLS, not evidence of separate business logic) -- both are thin projections of `analyze_impact`'s own already-computed `call_chain`/`dependencies` fields, not a second call-graph traversal. `get_symbol`'s `called_by`/`calls` query mirrors `GET /v1/symbols/{id}`'s tested query exactly, including the Phase 4.1 compatibility correction's `relationship_type == "calls"` filter -- a small, deliberate, DISCLOSED duplication of a plain parameterized DB read (not a graph algorithm) rather than a deeper refactor pulled into this milestone; flagged in Known Issues below, not silently accepted.
- **Structured errors, verified directly, not just implemented**: every tool returns `{"error": "symbol_not_found" | "repository_not_authorized", "message": "..."}` for its failure cases -- confirmed via `call_tool` returning a plain dict, never an unhandled Python exception, for a missing symbol id and for an unauthorized repository, across every one of the 7 tools.
- **Repository isolation, zero tolerance, verified across every single tool in one test**: two repositories with identical file/symbol names, a connection scoped to repo A only, asked about repo B's real symbol id through EVERY tool (`search_code` included) -- every one returns `repository_not_authorized`, never repo B's real data, never a crash; the SAME connection's own repo A queries are verified to still work correctly (not "everything returns an error").
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: no REST/CLI changes; `IndexingEngine` itself is completely unchanged by this milestone (the MCP module only calls existing public methods); no changes to authentication/authorization primitives (`verify_api_key`, `AuthorizedKey` reused as-is); no transport/serving wiring yet (`mcp.run_stdio_async()`/`run_sse_async()` exist on the built `FastMCP` instance but nothing in this codebase calls them yet -- `codepulse serve`, Phase 6's own CLI work, is the natural place to actually start a running server process; this milestone builds and tests the server OBJECT, not a long-running process).

### Phase 5.1/5.2/5.4 — Context-bundle assembly, `/v1/agent/context`, token-budget truncation + snapshot freshness

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 items 5.1 *"Context-bundle assembly function (shared by REST + MCP)"*, 5.2 *"/v1/agent/context endpoint"*, 5.4 *"Token-budget truncation logic + snapshot freshness metadata"*; §12's full literal JSON example and its 4 numbered token-efficiency rules (body truncated to signature+docstring by default; callers/callees capped and sorted by confidence-then-call-count; `snapshot_commit` always present; nothing silently omitted without a `truncated` flag).

**Process note**: implemented under the user's explicit continuous-execution authorization (standing notice near the top of this document). 5.1/5.2/5.4 were built as one coherent unit (5.4's "token-budget truncation logic + snapshot freshness metadata" IS the assembly function's own truncation/`snapshot_commit` behavior -- there was no separable "5.4 work" once 5.1 was built correctly per §12's own literal rules) and share one commit; 5.3 (MCP server) is a genuinely separate deliverable (new module, new dependency) and gets its own commit.

- **Shared assembly function**: new `IndexingEngine.get_context_bundle(symbol_name, file_path, repo_id, signature, docstring, body, include_body=False, max_related=10) -> dict`. Composes THREE already-tested capabilities with zero new graph/retrieval algorithm: `get_callers`/`get_callees` at `max_depth=1` (same reuse pattern `analyze_references` already established) for `callers`/`callees`, and the new `find_similar_symbols` for `related_symbols`.
- **A real, pre-existing repository-isolation gap found and fixed while extracting `find_similar_symbols`**: `POST /v1/symbols/{id}/similar`'s vector search (`indexing_engine.vector_index.search(...)`, inline in the REST route since long before this session) had NO `repo_id` filtering at all -- unlike `IndexingEngine.search()`'s own "semantic" branch, which has been repo-scoped since Phase 0.6. A caller could receive another repository's "similar" symbols. Fixed as part of extracting the shared method: over-fetch the entire vector-index pool (matching `search()`'s own established `_repo_filter` pattern), filter by `meta.get("repo_id") == repo_id`. The pre-existing `/similar` REST route now delegates to this same corrected method -- verified at BOTH the engine level and the REST level that identical near-duplicate functions in two repositories no longer cross-contaminate results.
- **§12's 4 token-efficiency rules, each verified directly**: (1) `definition.body` is `None` unless `include_body=True` -- verified; (2) `callers`/`callees` capped at `max_related`, reusing Phase 4.2's own confidence-then-call-count deterministic ordering unmodified; (3) `snapshot_commit` always present -- reads the most recent `RepositorySnapshotModel.commit_sha` for the repository (reusing the exact query shape `_plan_incremental_run` already established), honestly `None` (never fabricated) for an unindexed repository, verified against a REAL git commit SHA from a real indexed repo; (4) `truncated=True` whenever ANYTHING was shortened (body omitted, or callers/callees capped below their real count) -- verified directly for both triggering conditions independently.
- **REST**: new `POST /v1/agent/context` (roadmap's own literal path, no `{symbol_id}` placeholder -- so `symbol_id` is a request-body field, `AgentContextRequest`, unlike every other symbol-scoped route in this API) -- a thin adapter, matching every other Phase 4/5 route's established isolation pattern.
- **Repository isolation**: verified with two repositories sharing identical function/class names throughout, at both the `get_context_bundle`/`find_similar_symbols` engine level and the REST level (200/403/401/404 contract tests for `/v1/agent/context`, plus a dedicated REST-level isolation proof for the now-fixed `/similar` endpoint).
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: no migration, no schema change (every field needed already existed -- `RepositorySnapshotModel.commit_sha` since Phase 3.1); no CLI/MCP surface added in THIS commit (MCP's `get_context` tool, built next as Phase 5.3, calls this exact function); no changes to `CallGraphBuilder`, `ModuleGraphBuilder`, or `analyze_impact`/`analyze_references`'s own logic.

### Phase 4.4 — References endpoint

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 item 4.4, literally *"References endpoint"*; §21 API Strategy: *"Endpoint groups: ... `references` (new), `graph` (callers/callees/impact) ..."* -- `references` named explicitly as its OWN group, separate from `graph`; §11: *"Symbol intelligence: definitions and references are already well-supported by the parser"* -- the roadmap's only substantive gloss on what "references" means (usages, distinct from "implementations/overrides/inheritance," which Phase 4.1 separately built).

**Process note**: implemented under the user's explicit continuous-execution authorization (see the standing-deviation notice near the top of this document) -- no separate per-phase approval round-trip this time. The architecture below was derived directly from clear roadmap text plus this project's own established Phase 4.1/4.2 patterns, satisfying CLAUDE.md's own "Architectural Decision Rule" bar for proceeding without a fresh approval gate (clear intent, clear existing pattern, preserves every existing contract and repository-isolation guarantee, fully testable, no new infrastructure).

- **Central design choice, grounded in the roadmap's own text**: "references" = DIRECT (single-hop) usages only, deliberately NOT a transitive/depth-bucketed traversal the way `analyze_impact` (Phase 4.2) is. Keeping these two concepts distinct is itself the point -- directly matching this session's own repeated discipline against conflating call graph / inheritance graph / impact analysis, and the current authorization's own explicit instruction to preserve exactly this distinction.
- **Two direct-reference kinds**: `callers` (symbols that directly call this one) and `subclasses` (classes that directly list this symbol as a base class) -- giving Phase 4.1's inheritance data its FIRST real query surface anywhere in the system (extracted and resolved since Phase 4.1, explicitly excluded from impact analysis by Phase 4.2's own Decision 1, and disclosed as "no query surface yet" in this document's own Known Issues since Phase 4.2).
- **Zero new traversal algorithms**: `callers` reuses `CallGraphBuilder.get_callers(..., max_depth=1)` completely unmodified -- the exact same tested BFS/confidence/deterministic-ordering machinery from Phase 4.2, just capped to one hop. `subclasses` uses a new but genuinely small method, `CallGraphBuilder.get_direct_subclasses` (`codepulse/parser/graph.py`): a plain adjacency lookup (not a BFS, since references are direct-only by design) over `self.reverse_inheritance_adjacency`, paired with a new `self.inheritance_edge_meta` index (the inheritance-side counterpart to Phase 4.2's `edge_meta`, built in the SAME loop that already populates `inheritance_adjacency` -- no second pass) for confidence, reusing the existing `_CONFIDENCE_SORT_RANK` for deterministic ordering. Verified directly that a grandchild class (`Cat(Dog)`, `Dog(Animal)`) does NOT appear in `Animal`'s direct subclasses -- only `Dog` does.
- **Separation invariant preserved, verified directly**: `get_direct_subclasses` reads ONLY the inheritance-specific structures, never `self.reverse_adjacency` -- confirmed with a dedicated test proving an ordinary function-calls-function relationship never appears as a "subclass."
- **Shared intelligence core**: new `IndexingEngine.analyze_references(symbol_name, file_path, repo_id) -> dict`, matching `analyze_impact`'s exact established shape -- repository-scoped via `self.call_graphs.get(repo_id)` (never the singular `self.call_graph` alias), computed fresh from the current snapshot every call, no persistence, no incremental-update machinery (none needed -- it reads the same always-fresh in-memory graph `analyze_impact`/`analyze_module_structure` already read). A cold cache (never indexed this process, or after a restart) degrades to empty `callers`/`subclasses` lists, verified directly, never an error.
- **REST**: new `GET /v1/symbols/{id}/references` (`codepulse/api/app.py`) -- a thin adapter, resolve symbol + `auth.authorize_repository(sym.file.repo_id)` + delegate to `analyze_references` + return its result directly, matching `GET /v1/symbols/{id}`'s and `POST .../impact-analysis`'s own established isolation pattern exactly.
- **No CLI command, no MCP tool this milestone -- deliberate, not an oversight**: `codepulse refs <name>` is explicitly Phase 6.1's "full CLI command set" item (§22), not 4.4's, per this session's own repeatedly-confirmed distinction; no MCP server exists until Phase 5.3, and §13's own named MCP tool list (`search_code`, `get_symbol`, `find_callers`, `find_callees`, `impact_analysis`, `find_similar`, `get_context`) does not include a distinct "references" tool at all -- confirmed by re-reading that list before implementing, not assumed.
- **What was verified, directly, not just inferred**: 12 new tests in `tests/test_references.py` -- same-file ("low") and import-resolved ("high") direct-subclass confidence; direct-only exclusion of a grandchild class; the calls/inheritance separation invariant; direct-caller-only exclusion of a 2-hop transitive caller (proving this is genuinely distinct from `analyze_impact`'s own bucketing); repository isolation with identical class/function names across two repositories; cold-cache behavior; and REST-level 200 (correct shape), 403 (wrong repository), 401 (no key), and 404 (missing symbol) contract tests.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why**: no migration, no schema change; no changes to `CallGraphBuilder`'s calls-only structures, `ModuleGraphBuilder`, or `analyze_impact`/`analyze_module_structure`'s own logic; no CLI/MCP surface (deferred to their own roadmap-assigned milestones, per the bullet above).

### Phase 4.3 — Deterministic module clustering via import graph

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 item 4.3, literally *"Deterministic module-clustering via import graph"*; §11: *"module/dependency-cluster detection via the existing import graph (connected-components / community detection over the import graph is a well-understood graph algorithm, no LLM needed) to identify likely 'modules' and 'entry points'"*; §45 backlog B18: *"New graph-analysis module... Returns coherent module clusters on CodePulse's own repo as a test case."*

**Process, not just outcome**: preceded by a dedicated, explicit, read-only Phase 4.3 design audit that did something none of the earlier Phase 4 audits needed to do -- it **empirically ran both algorithms §11 names** against CodePulse's own repository BEFORE recommending either one, rather than reasoning about them abstractly. Plain weakly-connected components over this project's own 28-file `codepulse/` package produced exactly ONE 27-file component plus one singleton -- a measured, concrete failure of B18's own self-referential DoD ("coherent module clusters," plural, meaningfully separated). Seeded Louvain community detection (`networkx.algorithms.community.louvain_communities`, `seed=0`) over the same graph produced 5 clusters, several visibly coherent (a "parser subsystem" cluster of `models.py`+`parser/*`; an "API + storage" cluster of `app.py`/`errors.py`/`security.py`/`auth.py`/`jobs.py`/`db.py`). This measured evidence -- not a preference -- is why Louvain was recommended and approved over connected components.

- **Approved architecture (explicit, point-by-point user sign-off)**: new `codepulse/parser/module_graph.py`, `ModuleGraphBuilder`, kept deliberately SEPARATE from `CallGraphBuilder` (file-level import graph, not the symbol-level call/inheritance graph `CallGraphBuilder` already owns). Reuses `ImportResolver.resolve_import_path` completely unmodified -- no second import resolver, no duplicated resolution logic.
- **Import graph semantics**: nodes are every file `ImportResolver` actually parsed (including files with zero imports in either direction -- isolated files are never omitted). An edge `source -> target` exists if `source` contains AT LEAST ONE import statement resolving to `target` -- multiple import statements between the same pair collapse to exactly one edge (dependency EXISTENCE, not `CallGraphEdge.call_count`-style raw statement counting; documented explicitly in the class's own docstring so this distinction is never assumed). An import that doesn't resolve to a real file in this repository (external package, stdlib, genuine parser gap) never creates a node or edge -- verified directly with a fixture (`import os`/`import requests`) proving zero phantom nodes.
- **Degree signals, raw only**: `in_degree`/`out_degree` computed from that exact edge set (never from raw import-statement counts), exposed with NO invented score/label/threshold anywhere. §11's own literal heuristic wording ("many inbound, few outbound = looks like an API/entry point; many outbound, few inbound = looks like a utility/leaf module") is preserved EXACTLY as written, not silently reversed to match the more common convention where "entry point" means low-inbound/high-outbound (`cli.py`/`app.py`-shaped) -- confirmed empirically that under the roadmap's own literal wording, `config.py` (in=10, out=0) and `models.py` (in=8, out=0) rank as this repository's own top "API/entry point" signals, which a developer's ordinary intuition would likely call "utility/leaf" instead. This tension is documented, not resolved by picking a side.
- **Clustering, approved algorithm**: seeded Louvain (`_LOUVAIN_SEED = 0`, a fixed class constant, deliberately NOT exposed as a caller-supplied parameter this milestone) over an UNDIRECTED view of the same edge set -- community detection, not dependency-direction analysis; directed in/out-degree is computed and preserved completely separately, never lost by the undirected clustering view. NetworkX's own internal community container order is never used as the public cluster id -- each cluster's files are sorted lexicographically, clusters are sorted by `(size descending, lexicographically-smallest member)`, and only THEN assigned sequential ids 0..n-1, so the same graph and seed always produce the same clusters in the same order with the same ids regardless of NetworkX's own iteration order.
- **Determinism, verified the same rigorous way Phase 4.2 verified `get_callers`/`get_callees`**: same seed -> byte-identical output across repeated same-process runs AND across three independent process runs with different `PYTHONHASHSEED` values (`1`, `2`, `3`) -- not just single-process reasoning. Also verified a DIFFERENT seed CAN produce a different partition of the identical graph, confirming the seed is genuinely load-bearing, not decorative.
- **Repository isolation**: `IndexingEngine.analyze_module_structure(repo_id)` (new, thin method) filters `self.parse_results` to this repository's own `"{repo_id}::"`-prefixed entries -- the EXACT SAME pattern already used to build `self.call_graphs[repo_id]`, reused rather than reimplemented. Isolation holds by construction (each `ModuleGraphBuilder` instance only ever sees one repository's files), verified with two repositories sharing IDENTICAL file/module names, via a shared `IndexingEngine` instance.
- **No incremental machinery, no persistence, no migration** -- computed fresh from the current in-memory snapshot on every call, exactly per the approved scope. A cold cache (never indexed this process, or after a restart -- `self.parse_results` does not survive one, matching `self.call_graphs`'s own already-documented behavior) degrades to an empty, non-crashing graph, verified directly.
- **`networkx` added as an explicit direct `pyproject.toml` dependency** (`>=3.0,<4.0`) rather than relied on implicitly -- it was ALREADY installed in this environment as `torch`'s own transitive dependency (confirmed via `pip show networkx` -> `Required-by: torch`), so this changes nothing about what actually gets installed; it only makes an already-real reliance honest and non-fragile against a future `torch` version dropping that transitive requirement. Verified: `pyproject.toml` still parses correctly and the installed `3.6.1` satisfies the new constraint.
- **No REST route, CLI command, or MCP tool added** -- library/shared-core only, per the explicit approved scope and B18's own "Technical Scope: New graph-analysis module" wording (no `api/app.py`/`cli.py` named, unlike B17's row). `IndexingEngine.analyze_module_structure` is the one new integration point a future REST/CLI/MCP surface can call without duplicating any construction logic.
- **The DoD's own self-referential test case, made permanent**: `tests/test_module_graph.py::test_codepulse_own_repository_produces_coherent_non_trivial_clusters` walks and parses this actual repository's own `codepulse/` package and asserts STRUCTURAL properties only (every file appears exactly once; no file in two clusters; more than one non-singleton cluster exists; the largest cluster is NOT the whole repository; degree information is present for every file; re-running against identical input is byte-identical) -- deliberately does NOT hard-code which specific files land in which cluster, so an ordinary, harmless future dependency change in this codebase can never break this test the way a membership-exact assertion would.
- **What was verified, directly, not just inferred from the algorithm's design**: 13 new tests in `tests/test_module_graph.py` -- 7 small synthetic fixtures (isolated file; a 3-file chain; a fully-connected triangle staying in one cluster; two genuinely disconnected groups forming two separate clusters; duplicate imports between the same file pair collapsing to one edge; external/unresolved imports creating zero phantom nodes; relative imports resolving to the correct package file); degree-ranking ordering for both `rank_by_inbound`/`rank_by_outbound`; repository isolation; restart/cold-cache behavior; cross-process determinism; and the CodePulse-self-repository regression test above.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why** (deliberate, disclosed scope boundaries, matching the user's own explicit instructions): no migration, no schema change, no persistence of clusters or degrees anywhere; no REST/CLI/MCP surface; no changes to `CallGraphBuilder`, `models.py`, `storage/db.py`, inheritance logic, or impact-analysis logic; the Louvain seed is not user-configurable this milestone; no "importance"/"centrality"/"API probability" score was invented; Phase 4.4 (references endpoint) was NOT started.

### Phase 4.2 — Impact analysis: direct vs. transitive bucketing

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 item 4.2, literally *"Impact analysis: direct vs. transitive bucketing"*; §11: *"extend beyond the current caller/callee list to explicitly bucket directly affected (depth 1) vs. transitively affected (depth 2+) symbols/files"*; §36 Phase 4 DoD: *"impact analysis output clearly separates confidence tiers and depth buckets."*

**Process, not just outcome**: preceded by a dedicated, explicit, read-only Phase 4.2 design audit (14 numbered questions, covering impact semantics, the calls-vs-inheritance decision, the response contract, confidence propagation, shared-core architecture, repository isolation, deterministic ordering, and API compatibility) before any code was touched -- zero files modified during the audit, ending with an explicit STOP for approval, honored. The audit also surfaced a real, live, Phase-4.1-introduced regression outside its own stated scope (see the dedicated compatibility-correction entry below), which the user explicitly directed be fixed and committed FIRST, separately, before Phase 4.2 itself.

- **Decision 1 (approved, explicit, point-by-point)**: impact analysis is **CALLS ONLY**. `IndexingEngine.analyze_impact` traverses only `CallGraphBuilder.get_callers`/`get_callees`, which already only read the calls-only adjacency (`self.adjacency`/`self.reverse_adjacency`) -- never `self.inheritance_adjacency`/`self.reverse_inheritance_adjacency` (Phase 4.1's own separation invariant, unchanged by this milestone). Inheritance-aware impact analysis is explicitly deferred as a future, separately-scoped capability, not part of Phase 4.2.
- **Decision 2 (approved, explicit)**: every legacy response field (`symbol`, `direct_callers`, `indirect_callers`, `call_chain`, `dependencies`) is preserved with its EXACT pre-4.2 meaning -- `IndexingEngine.analyze_impact` computes them identically to the old inline REST logic, from the same underlying `callers`/`callees` lists. New, purely additive fields: `callers: {direct, transitive}` and `callees: {direct, transitive}`, using exactly two depth buckets (`direct = depth == 1`, `transitive = depth >= 2`, §11's own literal definition -- no additional buckets, no files rollup). Each bucketed entry now also carries `confidence`.
- **Confidence propagation, the smallest architecture-consistent addition, not a new system**: `CallGraphBuilder.__init__` gained `self.edge_meta: dict[(src_key, tgt_key), (confidence, call_count)]`, populated in the SAME loop that already builds `self.adjacency` (no second pass over parsed files) -- safe because `_build_graph`'s own existing `call_counts`/`call_confidence` dedup dicts already guarantee exactly one edge per `(src_key, tgt_key)` pair, so this lookup is never ambiguous. `get_callees` looks up `self.edge_meta.get((curr_key, next_key))` per hop (the traversal direction already matches). `get_callers`' queue items were extended to also carry `points_to` (the key the caller directly calls) alongside `(key, depth)`, so the SAME lookup works in the reverse-traversal direction too -- this is the only change to the BFS's own bookkeeping; the visited-set/queue mechanics, shortest-path depth assignment, and cycle safety are byte-for-byte unchanged from before this milestone (verified: `_CONFIDENCE_SORT_RANK`/`edge_meta` additions did not touch the `visited`/`queue` logic at all). Reuses the exact existing `high`/`low`/`unresolved` vocabulary -- no fourth tier.
- **Deterministic ordering, a real gap this milestone's own audit discovered and closed**: pre-4.2, `self.adjacency`/`self.reverse_adjacency` values were plain Python `set`s of `(file, symbol)` tuples -- iterating a set of strings has an order dependent on Python's per-process hash randomization (`PYTHONHASHSEED`), so `get_callers`/`get_callees`' result order was stable WITHIN one process run but not GUARANTEED identical across two different process invocations against the byte-identical repository snapshot, in tension with CLAUDE.md's "deterministic retrieval for the same query/snapshot" invariant. Fixed via a new `CallGraphBuilder._sort_and_strip` helper (shared by both methods): sorts by `(confidence rank, call_count descending, file, symbol)` -- reusing §12's own stated "callers/callees... sorted by confidence then call-count" convention for the adjacent `/v1/agent/context` bundle, not inventing a new ranking. **Verified directly, not just reasoned about**: a new test spawns the SAME fixture in two separate `python -c` subprocesses with explicitly DIFFERENT `PYTHONHASHSEED` values (`1` and `4242`) and asserts byte-identical JSON output -- the only way to actually prove cross-process determinism, since a single pytest run has one fixed hash seed for its whole session regardless of what the code does.
- **Shared intelligence core, closing the architecture gap the audit named**: new `IndexingEngine.analyze_impact(symbol_name, file_path, repo_id, max_depth=3) -> dict` (`codepulse/indexer/engine.py`), following the exact same established pattern as `IndexingEngine.search()` (a plain method, repo-scoped internally, returning a plain dict both REST and CLI consume as-is). `POST /v1/symbols/{id}/impact-analysis` (`codepulse/api/app.py`) is now a genuinely thin adapter: resolve symbol, authorize, call `indexing_engine.analyze_impact(...)`, return its result directly -- zero graph-analysis logic left in the route function. CLI's `cmd_impact` (`codepulse/cli.py`) redirects from calling `CallGraphBuilder.get_callers`/`get_callees` directly to `engine.analyze_impact(...)`, reusing `result["repository_id"]` already returned by its own `index_directory()` call -- **no new CLI flag or command was needed**, confirming the audit's own prediction that CLI reuse required no additional CLI feature. The CLI's Rich `Tree` presentation is otherwise unchanged, with one small, non-structural addition: each line now also shows `confidence=...`, using data that is now available rather than discarding it.
- **Repository isolation**: `analyze_impact` is called with `repo_id` resolved from the REQUESTED symbol's own `sym.file.repo_id` (REST) or the indexing call's own `result["repository_id"]` (CLI) -- never a client-suppliable or ambiguous value -- and looks up `self.call_graphs.get(repo_id)` (the per-repository dict), never the singular `self.call_graph` alias. Verified end-to-end: two repositories, colliding symbol names throughout (`mod.py`/`service.py` byte-identical in both), a key scoped to repo A gets a 403 for repo B's symbol id (unchanged pre-existing authorization behavior), and a correctly-scoped key for repo B sees its own complete, correct direct/transitive buckets with zero trace of repo A's data.
- **Inheritance exclusion, verified end-to-end through the actual REST response** (not just the in-memory graph, which `tests/test_inheritance_graph.py` already covers separately): a repository with both an inheritance relationship (`Dog(Animal)`) and an unrelated call relationship indexed together -- `POST .../impact-analysis` for `Animal` returns every field (`direct_callers`, `indirect_callers`, `call_chain`, `dependencies`, `callers`, `callees`) completely empty, proving the inheritance edge never leaks into any impact-analysis field, consistent with Decision 1.
- **Incremental indexing, reusing Phase 3.5's already-proven machinery, not a new mechanism**: `analyze_impact` reads whatever `self.call_graphs[repo_id]` currently holds -- since Phase 3.5 already correctly rebuilds/corrects that structure on every incremental run, `analyze_impact`'s bucket membership updates automatically, with no impact-analysis-specific incremental code needed. Verified directly for four scenarios: a changed caller (bucket membership swaps to the new caller), a changed callee (same, for the callees side), a deleted target file (an unrelated, unchanged caller's edge is corrected to `unresolved` by Phase 3.5's own BFS-one-hop mechanism, reflected in the bucketed `callers.direct` field), and a renamed target file (same correction, verified against the symbol's new file path).
- **Restart behavior**: `IndexingEngine.call_graphs` does not survive a process restart (the existing, already-established `tests/test_restart_idempotency.py` contract, re-confirmed here) -- `analyze_impact` against a cold cache returns the same all-empty-buckets shape as the pre-4.2 handler's own fallback, never an error. A fresh engine instance, after re-indexing, produces identical results to the original engine.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why** (deliberate, disclosed scope boundaries, matching the user's own explicit instructions): no migration, no schema change (every field 4.2 needed already existed); no `files` rollup bucket (explicitly declined); no new CLI command or flag; inheritance-aware impact analysis (explicitly deferred, Decision 1); Phase 4.3 (module clustering) and 4.4 (references endpoint) were NOT started.

### Phase 4.1 compatibility correction — isolate call relationships in symbol details (not a numbered roadmap sprint item)

**Origin**: found by Phase 4.2's own read-only design audit, not a user report. `GET /v1/symbols/{id}` (`codepulse/api/app.py`) queried `CallEdge` for `called_by`/`calls` filtering only by `target_symbol_name`/`source_symbol_name` + `repo_id` -- no `relationship_type` filter. Phase 4.1 started writing `relationship_type="inherits"` rows into that SAME table; without this filter, a base class's `called_by` (or a subclass's `calls`) could silently include its subclasses (or base classes), mislabeled as ordinary call relationships, with no field distinguishing them. The user explicitly instructed fixing this FIRST, narrowly, before Phase 4.2 itself, and committing it separately.

- **Fix**: added `.filter(CallEdge.relationship_type == "calls")` to both the `called_by` and `calls` queries. No other behavior change -- not a redesign, no new functionality, per the user's explicit instruction. Every pre-Phase-4.1 row already has `relationship_type="calls"` (the migration's own safe, unambiguous backfill), so this filter excludes nothing that predates Phase 4.1.
- **What was verified**: 4 new regression tests in `tests/test_call_edge_confidence.py` -- a subclass's `calls` field excludes its base class; a base class's `called_by` field excludes its subclasses; an ordinary call relationship in the SAME repository still appears correctly (the fix doesn't overcorrect into hiding real calls); and repository isolation holds with inheritance data present in two repositories with identical class names. Full suite **400 passed, 1 skipped** -- exactly 396 + 4.

### Phase 4.1 — Base-class/inheritance extraction (Python + JS/TS)

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 item 4.1, literally *"Base-class/inheritance extraction (Python + JS/TS)"*; §11's Code Intelligence Architecture section; §29 Data Model: *"`Symbol` | Yes (exists, needs upsert key + base-class field) | Core"* and *"`CallEdge` | ... add `confidence`"* (the discriminator this milestone adds, `relationship_type`, reuses that same row rather than a new table).

**Process, not just outcome**: this milestone was preceded by TWO separate, explicit, read-only design audits the user required before any code was touched -- a Phase-4-wide pre-flight audit (symbol model, call-graph, parsers, persistence, tests, migrations) and a Phase-4.1-specific audit that posed the central architecture question directly rather than letting it be decided implicitly: *should inheritance be (A) `Symbol.base_classes` only, or (B) represented in the graph via `CallEdge`?* Both audits were pure reads -- zero files modified -- and both ended with an explicit STOP for approval, honored.

- **Approved architecture (explicit, point-by-point user sign-off, not inferred)**: a hybrid, reusing this project's own existing raw-vs-resolved layering precedent (exactly how `CallReference.callee_name` feeds resolved `CallEdge` rows for calls) rather than inventing a new pattern. `Symbol.base_classes: list[str]` (new field, `codepulse/models.py`) holds RAW, unresolved base-class text exactly as written, populated only for `SymbolType.CLASS` symbols, persisted via a new `SymbolModel.base_classes` (nullable `Text`, JSON-serialized list -- the same convention `EmbeddingModel.embedding_json` already established, not a new serialization approach). Each raw name is then RESOLVED by `CallGraphBuilder` into a `CallEdge` row, distinguished from an ordinary call edge by a new `relationship_type` column (`"calls"` | `"inherits"`, `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'calls'`) -- reusing `CallEdge`'s existing `repo_id` scoping, `confidence` vocabulary, and (critically) Phase 3.5's already-built incremental-persistence machinery, rather than a second/parallel persistence mechanism.
- **Resolution reuses the EXACT existing 3-tier confidence vocabulary (high/low/unresolved), deliberately NOT a 4th "ambiguous" tier**: `CallGraphBuilder._resolve_target(name, file_path, local_symbols, import_map) -> (target_file, confidence)` was factored out of what was previously call-edge-only inline logic (a sanctioned internal refactor, explicitly permitted by the user's own instruction, IMMEDIATELY verified behavior-preserving by re-running the full pre-existing call-graph/confidence/incremental-update suite -- 22 tests, zero regressions -- before proceeding further) and is now called identically by both the call-edge loop and the new inheritance-edge loop in `CallGraphBuilder._build_graph`. "high" = resolved through an import mapping to a known file+symbol; "low" = same-file bare-name match; "unresolved" = everything else (including a resolved import file whose exact symbol couldn't be confirmed, and a true external base with no local/import match).
- **Hard adjacency-separation invariant, enforced by construction, not convention**: `CallGraphBuilder.__init__` now also builds `self.inheritance_edges` (list), `self.inheritance_adjacency`, `self.reverse_inheritance_adjacency` -- entirely separate dict/list instances, populated only by the new inheritance-resolution loop, never merged with `self.edges`/`self.adjacency`/`self.reverse_adjacency`. `get_callers()`/`get_callees()` were not modified at all and traverse ONLY the calls-only structures -- verified both by the full pre-existing test suite passing unmodified AND by two new dedicated regression tests proving a subclass never appears as a "caller" of its base class (and vice versa) via those two methods.
- **Python extraction** (`codepulse/parser/python.py`, `_extract_base_classes`): `class_definition`'s `superclasses` field (confirmed via a direct tree-sitter grammar probe during the design audit) is an `argument_list`; only `identifier`/`attribute` children are kept (dotted/qualified bases like `pkg.Mixin` supported), `keyword_argument` (e.g. `metaclass=Meta`) is deliberately excluded -- not a base class.
- **JS/TS extraction** (`codepulse/parser/javascript.py`, `_extract_base_classes`): a REAL, non-obvious grammar difference between the two languages was found and handled, not assumed identical -- plain JavaScript's `class_heritage` has the base expression as a DIRECT child; TypeScript wraps it in a sibling `extends_clause` alongside an optional `implements_clause`. Only ever searching within `extends_clause` when present is what keeps `implements_clause` categorically unreachable (interface implementation is a different relationship, out of this milestone's exact scope per §37 item 4.1's literal wording) -- not an extra exclusion check bolted on.
- **Migration `135a7b064ea7`**: purely additive (`symbols.base_classes` nullable `Text`; `call_edges.relationship_type` `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'calls'`, an unambiguous, honest backfill since every row this table has ever held has always been a call). Verified via upgrade -> downgrade -> re-upgrade on a disposable copy of the real `codepulse.db` (row counts preserved throughout: repositories=1, files=18, symbols=128, call_edges=528) before being applied to the real database, using ONLY `codepulse.storage.migrations` functions and plain `sqlite3` for inspection -- `codepulse.api.app` was never imported at any point (avoiding a fourth occurrence of the `create_all()`-alongside-Alembic incident class, per the deliberate mitigation already established since the repository-isolation fix).
- **`IndexingEngine._persist_call_graph_edges` extended to gather from BOTH `self.call_graph.edges` and `self.call_graph.inheritance_edges`** -- not a second incremental mechanism. This was correct, not merely convenient, because the existing delete-by-source-key and local-BFS-one-hop-by-target-key logic (Phase 3.5) was ALREADY relationship-type-agnostic (it filters only by `(repo_id, source_file, source_symbol_name)`/`(target_file, target_symbol_name)`, never by `relationship_type`), so a changed class's stale calls AND inherits edges are correctly deleted/recomputed together, and the BFS one-hop query already finds a caller through an ordinary call and a subclass extending a renamed/deleted base equally, via the identical query. Only the two `CallEdge(...)` insertion loops needed extending, each now setting `relationship_type=edge.relationship_type` explicitly from the in-memory edge.
- **What was verified, directly, not just inferred from the algorithm's design**: 26 new tests in new `tests/test_inheritance_graph.py` -- parser-level raw extraction (Python: single/multiple/dotted bases, metaclass excluded, no base; JS: extends/namespace-qualified extends/no extends; TS: extends, implements-NOT-a-base-class, extends+implements together only captures extends); graph-level confidence tiering (high/low/unresolved, and an explicit assertion the tier set never exceeds those three); the adjacency-separation invariant (inheritance edges never appear in `builder.edges`, never leak into `builder.adjacency`); the `get_callers`/`get_callees` regression (a subclass is never a "caller" of its base, a base is never a "callee" of its subclass); end-to-end persistence through `index_directory()` for a full index (base_classes + inherits edge both land correctly, with correct confidence) and a full re-index (replaces, doesn't duplicate); three incremental scenarios matching §9's own algorithm applied to inheritance specifically -- a base class SWAPPED (old edge gone, new edge present), a base class ADDED to a previously base-less class, a base class REMOVED entirely (edge gone, `Symbol.base_classes` empty/null); repeated incremental edits never accumulate duplicate inherits edges; and repository isolation specifically for inheritance data -- two repositories both defining identically-named `Base`/`Child` classes, proving Repo A's `Child` resolves only against Repo A's `Base` (never leaking into or being satisfied by Repo B's identically-named class), including after a simulated process restart (a fresh `IndexingEngine` instance against the same DB).
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why** (deliberate, disclosed scope boundaries, matching the user's own explicit instructions): no API changes, no CLI changes (`refs`/`callers`/`callees` CLI exposure is explicitly assigned to Phase 6, not Phase 4, per the roadmap's own sprint table); TypeScript's `implements` is never treated as inheritance (a different relationship, not this milestone's scope); no new inheritance-specific table (the existing `CallEdge` table was proven sufficient, per the approved architecture); Phase 4.2 (direct-vs-transitive impact bucketing), 4.3 (module clustering), and 4.4 (references endpoint) were NOT started -- per the user's own explicit stop condition after this commit.
- **Pre-existing test fallout, fixed, not weakened**: two `tests/test_migrations.py` assertions hardcoded the previous Alembic head revision (`2cd38eb12a7e`) -- exactly the same kind of update this project's own history already shows was needed when THAT revision became head after the prior one. Both bumped to `135a7b064ea7`, plus new assertions confirming the two new columns (`call_edges.relationship_type`, `symbols.base_classes`) actually exist post-migration. This is keeping a hardcoded literal accurate, not loosening a check.

### Phase 3.5 — Local BFS incremental graph update

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 item 3.5, literally *"Local BFS incremental graph update"*; §9's "Call graph updates" section: *"rather than rebuilding the whole graph, recompute only edges whose *source* symbol is in the changed set, plus a local BFS one hop out to catch edges whose *target* changed (a symbol being renamed/deleted must have inbound edges from unrelated files corrected too)."*

- **Two distinct layers, only one of which this milestone touches**: the IN-MEMORY call graph (`CallGraphBuilder`, `self.call_graph`) is already fully rebuilt from merged/cached parse data every run (Phase 3.1's own design) -- cheap (no embedding involved), and left completely unchanged by this milestone. §9's actual ask operates one layer down, at the DURABLE `CallEdge` persistence step: **which DB rows get touched**, not how the in-memory graph is computed. This distinction is made explicit in code comments at every relevant site so a future reader doesn't conflate the two.
- **New `IndexingEngine._persist_call_graph_edges`** (`codepulse/indexer/engine.py`), replacing the prior unconditional "append every in-memory edge every run" block that every milestone since Phase 3.1 has pointed at this one to fix:
  - **Full run**: deletes every existing `CallEdge` row for `repo_id` (now possible at all only because the repository-isolation fix gave `CallEdge` a `repo_id` to scope by), then inserts the complete fresh set -- a full, correct, one-time replace, mirroring `BM25Index.remove_repo`'s existing Phase 2.1 pattern.
  - **Incremental run**: a genuinely surgical update. (1) Deletes existing rows whose `(source_file, source_symbol_name)` is in `changed_symbol_keys` (the source-side half of §9's algorithm). (2) **"Local BFS one hop out"**: queries the remaining rows for this repo whose `(target_file, target_symbol_name)` is in `changed_symbol_keys` -- i.e. OTHER, unrelated symbols' edges pointing at something that just changed, was renamed, or disappeared -- collects their own `(source_file, source_symbol_name)` as `one_hop_keys`, and deletes those too. (3) Inserts every edge from the fresh in-memory `self.call_graph.edges` whose source is in `changed_symbol_keys | one_hop_keys`. A symbol untouched by either step keeps its existing, already-correct rows exactly as they were -- no unnecessary rewrite, no accumulation.
  - Correctness of step (2)/(3) depends on the in-memory graph being COMPLETE even for files not reparsed this run -- already guaranteed by Phase 3.1's own cache-completeness design (`self.parse_results`) plus `_plan_incremental_run`'s existing "no in-memory call graph cached yet" restart-safety guard, both reused as-is, not modified.
- **`changed_symbol_keys` computation, threaded through THREE existing methods that each already computed the relevant identities internally but previously discarded them**: `_purge_stale_entries` (deleted files' symbols), `_rekey_renamed_files` (a renamed file's symbols' OLD identity -- the literal "renamed... must have inbound edges... corrected" case from §9's own parenthetical), and `_diff_and_purge_file_symbols` (symbols that disappeared within a still-existing, modified file) now all return the `(file_path, symbol_name)` keys they identify, accumulated in `index_directory` into `removed_symbol_keys`. Combined with `{(sym.file_path, sym.name) for sym in all_symbols if id(sym) not in unchanged_symbol_ids}` (every genuinely new-or-changed symbol under its CURRENT identity -- stable across a body-only change), this gives the complete, correct `changed_symbol_keys` set passed to `_persist_to_db` -> `_persist_call_graph_edges`. `_doc_ids_for_file` (the shared helper these three methods already called) was extended to return each symbol's `(file_path, name)` alongside the doc_ids it already computed, avoiding a second DB query.
- **What was verified, directly, not just inferred from the algorithm's design**: 8 new engine-level integration tests in `tests/test_call_graph_incremental_update.py`, most importantly a DIRECT reproduction of §9's own named scenario, in BOTH halves of its literal "renamed/deleted" wording -- a symbol (`helper()`) disappears from a still-existing file (`lib.py`), and separately its file is RENAMED (`lib.py` -> `library.py`) -- while an entirely UNCHANGED file (`service.py`, never reparsed this run, confirmed via `total_files`) still calls it in its own unmodified source; its existing `CallEdge` row is found via the local BFS one hop out and its `confidence` is correctly corrected from `"high"` to `"unresolved"` in every case (the call site is real and unchanged, so the edge itself must still exist -- only its resolution correctness changes). The same correction is verified for a whole-file deletion too. Also verified: a full re-index REPLACES rather than duplicates a repo's edges; an unrelated symbol's row is left with the SAME row id (not deleted+recreated) when nothing about it or its callees changed; a changed symbol's own source-side edges are correctly recomputed to a NEW call target; repeated incremental edits to the same symbol never accumulate duplicate edges; an empty changeset touches zero rows.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why** (deliberate, disclosed scope boundaries): the in-memory call graph's own restart-survivability (`self.parse_results`/`self.call_graphs`, lost on a process restart, with `_plan_incremental_run`'s existing guard forcing a full run in that case) -- unchanged by this milestone; this fixes the DURABLE table's update strategy, not the in-memory cache's durability, and no roadmap item currently claims to fix that. Extending the multi-hop `get_callers`/`get_callees` to carry confidence or bucket depth (Phase 4.2's territory, disclosed since Phase 3.4). A symbol whose `start_line` shifts without a name/type change is still not recognized as identity-stable by the EXISTING `(file_id, name, start_line)` Symbol natural key (Phase 3.2/3.3's own disclosed, unrelated limitation) -- this milestone's `changed_symbol_keys` correctly follows whatever that key produces, it doesn't change it.
- **No database migration** -- `CallEdge.repo_id` already exists (added by the repository-isolation fix); this milestone is pure application logic using it.

### `CallEdge` repository-isolation fix (not a roadmap sprint item — a confirmed security/correctness fix)

**Origin**: a Phase 3.4 pre-commit audit (10-point correctness check, requested by the user before that milestone's commit) confirmed LIVE, via a real HTTP request through the actual FastAPI `TestClient` app (not just the raw query in isolation): a caller holding an API key scoped ONLY to one repository received another, unrelated repository's call-graph edge (symbol name + file path) in `GET /v1/symbols/{id}`'s `called_by` field, because `CallEdge` (né `DependencyModel`, since Phase 0.4) had no `repo_id` column at all, and that endpoint filtered by symbol name alone. This directly violates CLAUDE.md's "Repository isolation" permanent invariant and §15/§30's "every query... filtered by repository_id" rule. The user explicitly instructed fixing this before Phase 3.5.

- **New `CallEdge.repo_id` column** (`codepulse/storage/db.py`): nullable, FK to `repositories.id` (`ondelete="CASCADE"`), indexed -- the same pattern every other `repo_id` column in this schema already uses. `IndexingEngine._persist_to_db` now sets it to `repo_record.id` on every new `CallEdge` row (`self.call_graph` at that point in `_persist_to_db` is guaranteed to be the SAME repository's graph, since it's only ever read-then-written within one `index_directory()` call, never across two interleaved ones -- verified by re-reading the exact call sequence, not assumed).
- **Migration `2cd38eb12a7e`**: adds the column + FK (via batch mode, since SQLite needs a table rebuild to add a real FK constraint after the fact) and an index, then backfills existing rows CONSERVATIVELY and HONESTLY (CLAUDE.md: no fabricated metrics) -- for each distinct `source_file` value already in `call_edges`, looks up matching `files.path` rows: exactly one distinct `repo_id` match -> backfill it; two or more distinct matches (the same relative path exists in more than one repository -- genuinely ambiguous) or zero matches (the file was since renamed/moved/deleted) -> leave `repo_id = NULL`, never a guess. Verified with THREE distinct synthetic scenarios on a disposable copy before touching the real DB: an unambiguous match backfills correctly, a deliberately-constructed ambiguous match (two repositories both having a file at the identical relative path) correctly stays `NULL`, and a reference to a since-deleted file correctly stays `NULL`. Verified full upgrade AND downgrade round-trip on a disposable copy of the real `codepulse.db` before applying it there.
- **Query fixes, two distinct endpoints, two distinct root causes**:
  1. `GET /v1/symbols/{id}` (`codepulse/api/app.py`): both `called_by`/`calls` queries now add `.filter(CallEdge.repo_id == sym.file.repo_id)` -- the confirmed leak's direct fix.
  2. `POST /v1/symbols/{id}/impact-analysis` (`codepulse/api/app.py`): a **related but distinct** bug found while fixing the first one -- this endpoint used `indexing_engine.call_graph` (the "most recently indexed repository" SINGULAR alias, Phase 0.6's own disclosed limitation) instead of the already-existing, correctly-scoped `indexing_engine.call_graphs.get(repo_id)` dict. In a long-lived, multi-repository server process this could return an entirely different repository's BFS traversal results. Fixed by switching to the per-repo dict -- no schema change needed, this one was purely an application-logic bug using a structure that already existed for exactly this purpose.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why (deliberate, disclosed scope boundaries for this fix)**: `codepulse/cli.py`'s `cmd_impact` also reads `engine.call_graph` (singular) -- NOT fixed, because the CLI only ever indexes ONE repository per process invocation, so there is no cross-repository ambiguity to fix there (confirmed by inspection, not assumed). `IndexingEngine.search()`'s own `graph_scores` computation for `repository_id=None` (an explicit, documented "search across every repository" feature) also falls back to `self.call_graph` -- NOT changed, because that code path never claimed per-repository isolation in the first place (the caller explicitly asked for a cross-repository search); it's a ranking-signal *accuracy* question (a different, much smaller concern), not an isolation *violation*. `DependencyModel`/`CallEdge` rows still accumulate/duplicate on every re-index (unrelated pre-existing gap, Phase 3.5's job).
- **What was verified**: 4 new regression tests in `tests/test_call_edge_confidence.py`, directly reproducing the exact two-repository, colliding-symbol-name scenario the audit used to confirm the leak (`GET /v1/symbols/{id}` no longer leaks; the SAME endpoint still correctly returns a symbol's OWN repository's real edges, proving the fix didn't overcorrect into hiding legitimate data; `POST /v1/symbols/{id}/impact-analysis` no longer leaks via the `call_graphs` fix; a `CallEdge` row with `repo_id=NULL` -- standing in for an unbackfillable historical row -- is invisible to every repository's query, not just the "wrong" one). One pre-existing test (`test_pre_migration_style_row_with_no_confidence_surfaces_as_null_not_fabricated`) needed a one-line update (explicitly setting `repo_id` on its constructed row) to keep testing what it was actually about (a missing `confidence` value) rather than accidentally colliding with the new `repo_id`-scoping behavior. Full suite **362 passed, 1 skipped** -- exactly 358 + 4. Real `codepulse.db` migrated to `2cd38eb12a7e`; all 528 `call_edges` rows backfilled unambiguously to `repo_id=1` (the only repository that has ever been indexed in this database), zero left `NULL`, every other row count confirmed unchanged.

### Phase 3.4 — CallEdge.confidence scoring

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 item 3.4, literally *"CallEdge.confidence scoring"*; §11: *"every `CallEdge` should carry a `confidence` field — `high` when resolved through an explicit import mapping to a known file+symbol, `low` when resolved only by bare-name match against same-file symbols, `unresolved` when the callee couldn't be matched to any known symbol at all. This directly addresses the false-positive risk found in §3.5, and it's a **cheap** addition (the resolution logic already distinguishes these cases internally in `CallGraphBuilder._build_graph`; it just needs to be surfaced rather than silently discarded)"*; §29 Data Model: *"`CallEdge` | Yes (rename from `DependencyModel`, add `confidence`) | §11"*; §42 Risk Register: *"Call graph false positives | Medium | Medium | Confidence scoring surfaced to callers (§11), not hidden."*

- **Rename, per §29's explicit target entity name**: `DependencyModel` → `CallEdge` (table `dependencies` → `call_edges`, its two indexes renamed to match what `index=True` on a model actually named `call_edges` would auto-generate). Migration `f6e481353553`: purely a rename + one new nullable column, zero row-data loss (verified on a disposable copy: upgrade AND downgrade both correct, all 528 pre-existing rows preserved exactly either way).
- **Confidence computation, exactly matching §11's own three-tier definition** (`codepulse/parser/graph.py`, `CallGraphBuilder._build_graph`): `"low"` for a same-file bare-name match (the literal ambiguity §3.5's audit finding names — two unrelated classes defining a same-named method in one file would be conflated here); `"high"` for a callee resolved via an explicit import mapping to a target file **AND** confirmed present in `ImportResolver.symbol_index` as an actual extracted symbol there (not merely "some file was found that matches the import path"); `"unresolved"` for a true external call (no local match, no import match) **and** for an import that resolved to a real file but could not confirm the exact symbol in it (a wildcard import, a re-exported alias, or similar) — deliberately NOT falling back to `"low"` for that last case, since `"low"` is defined as a same-FILE match and this is cross-file; overclaiming confidence there would be worse than honestly saying `"unresolved"`.
- **A previously dead structure put to real, intended use**: `ImportResolver.symbol_index` (a `(file_path, symbol_name) -> Symbol` lookup) has existed since this class's very first version but was never read by anything anywhere in the codebase until this milestone — a strong signal it was originally built for exactly this "confirm a known file+symbol, not just a known file" check, never wired up before now.
- **Every value threaded through the full pipeline, not just computed and discarded**: `CallGraphEdge` (`codepulse/models.py`) gained a `confidence: str = "unresolved"` field; `IndexingEngine._persist_to_db` now passes `edge.confidence` into each persisted `CallEdge` row; `GET /v1/symbols/{id}` (`codepulse/api/app.py`) now includes `"confidence"` in every `called_by`/`calls` item — directly satisfying §42's "surfaced to callers, not hidden" mitigation and matching §12's own shown JSON shape (`{"symbol": ..., "file": ..., "confidence": "high"}`) for the (later, Phase 5) agent context endpoint.
- **`confidence` is nullable, honestly, per CLAUDE.md's "no fabricated metrics" rule**: rows persisted before this migration have no historical resolution-path information to reconstruct, so they show `confidence: null` in every API response and DB row until their repository's next re-index recomputes real values -- never backfilled with a guessed tier.
- **A THIRD occurrence of the `StorageManager.create_tables()`-alongside-Alembic incident, this time with a real-data-movement twist**: applying the migration to the real `codepulse.db` hit `there is already another table or index with this name: call_edges` -- a `StorageManager()` had been constructed against the real DB at some point after `db.py`'s `CallEdge`/`call_edges` rename was written (traced to this session's own ad-hoc `import codepulse.api.app` sanity-check command, whose module-level `storage_manager = StorageManager()` uses the real DB by default -- the exact same root cause as the Phase 1.3 and Phase 3.1 incidents), silently `create_all()`-ing an EMPTY `call_edges` table with the new schema while the real, populated `dependencies` table (528 rows) sat untouched alongside it. Unlike the previous two incidents (fixable by `stamp_database` alone, since nothing needed to move), this one could NOT be resolved by stamping -- the migration's job is to RENAME `dependencies` into `call_edges` and move its 528 real rows there, and `call_edges` already existing (empty) would block that rename outright. **Remediated by**: verifying the accidental table was genuinely empty and schema-identical to what the migration would produce, backing up the real DB, dropping the accidental empty `call_edges` table, then running the real migration normally. Real `codepulse.db` confirmed unchanged in substance before and after (`repositories=1, files=18, symbols=128`, `call_edges` -- ex-`dependencies` -- `=528`, etc.); the backup was deleted once the result was verified.
- **What was explicitly NOT done** (deliberate scope decisions matching neighboring roadmap items, disclosed in full above and in Known Issues/Current Risks, not oversights): extending the multi-hop, BFS-based `get_callers`/`get_callees` (used by the CLI's `impact` command and the API's impact-analysis endpoint) to carry or aggregate confidence across a multi-edge path -- that reads as roadmap item 4.2's own "direct vs. transitive bucketing" territory, a different, later milestone, not 3.4's single-edge scope; recomputing only the edges touching changed symbols (avoiding the pre-existing, disclosed Phase 0.4 accumulation-on-every-re-index gap) -- explicitly roadmap item 3.5's "local BFS-based incremental graph updates," not 3.4's; correcting a renamed file's stale `CallEdge` rows (Phase 3.2's own disclosed, still-open finding) -- same reason.
- **What was verified**: 7 new focused tests -- 4 in `tests/test_graph.py` (each of the three confidence tiers individually, including the "resolved file but unconfirmed symbol still isn't `high`" case) and 3 in the new `tests/test_call_edge_confidence.py` (persisted `CallEdge` rows carry the correct computed confidence end-to-end through `IndexingEngine`, `GET /v1/symbols/{id}` surfaces it correctly for both a caller and a callee, and a pre-migration-style row with no confidence value surfaces as `null`, never fabricated). Full suite **358 passed, 1 skipped** -- exactly 351 + 7. Real `codepulse.db` migrated and verified as described above.

### Phase 3.3 — Symbol-level body_hash comparison to skip unchanged embeddings

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 item 3.3, literally *"Symbol-level body_hash comparison to skip unchanged embeddings"*; §9: *"within a changed file, compare `body_hash` per extracted symbol against the previous snapshot's symbols (matched by `(name, type)` first, falling back to signature similarity if the name changed — a simple heuristic, not a semantic diff). Unchanged symbols skip re-embedding and re-chunking entirely — this is the single biggest cost saver on incremental runs"*; §8's pipeline diagram: *"Chunking (only for new/changed symbols, by body_hash comparison)"* / *"Embedding (only chunks whose body_hash changed)."*

- **A real, necessary architectural consequence discovered while planning this milestone, before any code was written**: Phase 3.1's existing `_purge_stale_entries` purges a MODIFIED file's lexical/vector doc_ids at FILE granularity (every symbol in the file, unconditionally) BEFORE the parse loop re-adds them. If this milestone's skip-decision simply left an "unchanged" symbol's doc_id untouched while `_purge_stale_entries` had ALREADY wiped it moments earlier for the whole file, that symbol's search entry would be gone forever with nothing left to put it back (since skipping means no re-add either) -- a real, would-be data-loss regression if shipped as originally structured. **Fixed by restructuring**: the file-level purge for MODIFIED files (and a rename's new path) was removed from the pre-parse-loop pass entirely -- `_purge_stale_entries` is now called ONLY for genuinely `deleted_relpaths` (whole files gone). A NEW, SYMBOL-granular replacement, `_diff_and_purge_file_symbols(repo_id, rel_path, new_symbols)`, runs per-file INSIDE the parse loop, immediately after that file is parsed, and purges only the doc_ids of symbols that actually changed or disappeared -- leaving untouched symbols' doc_ids (and DB rows) completely alone from start to finish.
- **New `IndexingEngine._diff_and_purge_file_symbols`** (`codepulse/indexer/engine.py`): for a given file, compares its freshly parsed symbols against the EXISTING, durable `SymbolModel` rows for that file (if any), matched by `(name, start_line)` plus a `type` equality check -- **the exact same natural key `_persist_to_db`'s own Symbol upsert already uses**, a deliberate, disclosed scope choice (see the scope-fork bullet below), NOT the broader, start_line-independent `(name, type)` matcher §9's text also describes. Classifies each symbol as: **unchanged** (matched, same `body_hash` -- returned to the caller as `{id(sym), ...}` so chunking/embedding/BM25-readd can be skipped for it); **changed** (matched, different `body_hash` -- its stale doc_ids are purged now, so the pipeline's normal re-chunk/re-embed/re-add for it lands cleanly); **disappeared** (an existing row with NO matching new symbol at all -- deleted from the file, or renamed to a different name WITHIN the file -- its `SymbolModel` row is deleted directly, cascading to `Embedding`, and its doc_ids purged); **new** (no existing row -- untouched, handled by the normal pipeline as always). A brand-new file (no existing `FileModel` row) or a symbol whose `start_line` shifted (not matched by this key) safely falls through to the pre-existing, unchanged behavior for that case.
- **A genuine, previously-disclosed, long-standing gap closed as a necessary side effect, not a separate fix**: the "disappeared" case above directly closes what `_persist_to_db`'s own docstring has said since Phase 0.4 -- *"this mirrors Phase 0.4's decision not to delete stale Symbol rows... real stale-data cleanup is Phase 3"*. A symbol removed from (or renamed within) a still-existing file now has its stale row and embedding actually deleted, not silently orphaned forever. Regression-tested (`test_disappeared_symbol_within_a_modified_file_is_cleaned_up`).
- **`index_directory()` wiring**: the parse loop now calls `_diff_and_purge_file_symbols` for every file being parsed during an INCREMENTAL run (skipped for a rename's new path -- see below), accumulating a run-wide `unchanged_symbol_ids: set[int]`. For each symbol in that set: `self.chunker.chunk_symbol(sym)` is never called (no chunks -> no embedding -> no `vector_index.add_batch` call for it -- the actual expensive work §9 calls "the single biggest cost saver"), and the BM25 population loop's `self.bm25_index.add_symbol(...)` call is also skipped for it. `self.symbols_by_id`/`self.symbol_repo_map` are still refreshed for every symbol regardless (cheap, keeps them pointing at the freshest parsed object) -- only the two genuinely expensive/duplicate-risking calls are skipped.
- **A real, disclosed scope fork, resolved the same way as Phase 3.2's own (a deliberate, conservative choice, not silently picked)**: this milestone applies the skip optimization ONLY during incremental runs, and ONLY via the SAME `(name, start_line)`-based matching `_persist_to_db` already uses -- NOT §9's fuller `(name, type)` (start_line-independent) matcher with a signature-similarity fallback. A symbol whose `start_line` shifts because unrelated code above it in the file changed is therefore NOT recognized as unchanged and gets a fresh re-chunk/re-embed (with a fresh id if `_persist_to_db`'s own key also misses it) -- exactly today's pre-existing Phase 0.4 behavior for that case, not a new regression, but also not the fuller cost-saving §9 describes. Building the fuller `(name,type)` matcher (which would ALSO have let Phase 3.2 preserve ids across a rename-with-edit that shifts a symbol's line) is a disclosed, deferred enhancement -- not built now, consistent with the Phase 3.2 precedent of preferring the conservative, already-correct-by-construction option.
- **A second deliberate scope boundary**: the skip optimization is NEVER applied to a file that was renamed this run (pure or with edits) -- a rename changes every symbol's doc_id string (which embeds the file path) regardless of body content, so "leave the doc_id untouched" cannot apply; `_rekey_renamed_files` (Phase 3.2) already purges those doc_ids in full and the file is fully re-chunked/re-embedded, unaffected by this milestone. Reusing a durably-stored embedding vector under a renamed file's new doc_id (skipping the model call even though the doc_id itself must still move) is a disclosed, deferred enhancement, not built now. Regression-tested (`test_pure_rename_does_not_apply_the_symbol_skip_optimization`).
- **A third deliberate scope boundary**: the optimization is NEVER applied during a FULL (non-incremental) re-index. A full run's existing, unconditional `self.bm25_index.remove_repo(repo_id)` call (Phase 2.1/3.1) wipes every symbol's lexical document up front, so skipping any symbol's BM25 re-add during a full run would permanently lose it. Since `unchanged_symbol_ids` is only ever populated when `_diff_and_purge_file_symbols` is called, and that is only ever called during an incremental run, this is enforced by construction, not a runtime check. Regression-tested (`test_full_reindex_never_applies_the_symbol_skip_optimization`).
- **`index_directory()`'s return dict**: new `"symbols_skipped_unchanged": int` key, disclosing exactly how many symbols this run avoided re-chunking/re-embedding.
- **What was verified**: 5 new focused tests in `tests/test_incremental_indexing.py` -- an unchanged symbol within a modified file skips embedding (spied: the embedder is asserted to never see its text) and BM25 re-add, while a changed symbol in the SAME file still updates correctly and a third, untouched file's own symbol is unaffected; a disappeared symbol's row/embedding are actually deleted, not orphaned; a pure rename applies zero skips (locking in the Phase 3.2 boundary); a forced full re-index applies zero skips and still produces exactly the right number of lexical documents (no loss, no duplication); the in-memory call graph remains fully correct for a skipped symbol (edges are built from the complete parsed-symbol list, unaffected by the skip decision). Full suite **351 passed, 1 skipped** -- exactly 346 + 5. Real `codepulse.db` untouched throughout (no migration exists for this milestone to apply).

### Phase 3.2 — Rename/move detection + symbol re-keying

**Scope, quoted from the roadmap and held to strictly**: §37 item 3.2, literally *"Rename/move detection + symbol re-keying"*; §9: *"for git repos, use git's own rename detection (`git diff -M`) to map `(old_path → new_path)` and re-key existing `Symbol`/`Embedding` rows to the new path instead of delete+recreate — this preserves symbol IDs"*; Phase 3 DoD: *"renamed files retain their symbol/embedding history"*; §42 Risk Register: *"Rename/move detection incorrectly merges unrelated symbols | Low | Medium | Conservative matching heuristic (git rename detection + name/type match) with fallback to delete+recreate rather than a risky guess."* A full read-only audit (quoting all of the above, plus inspecting `SymbolModel`'s existing `(file_id, name, start_line)` natural key, `FileModel`'s `(repo_id, path)` key, `DependencyModel`'s plain-text `source_file`/`target_file` columns, and the existing BM25/Vector doc_id format) was performed and presented to the user BEFORE any code was written, per the user's own explicit instruction.

- **A real, disclosed scope fork surfaced by the audit, resolved by explicit user decision (not silently picked)**: re-keying `FileModel.path` in place fully satisfies "preserves symbol IDs" for a **pure rename** (git similarity `R100`, byte-identical content) — every symbol's `start_line` is unchanged, so the existing `(file_id, name, start_line)` key matches every row perfectly with zero new matching logic. For a **rename + content edit** (similarity `R50`-`R99`), a symbol whose own `start_line` shifts would not be matched by that key and would fall back to today's existing, already-disclosed Phase 0.4 behavior (a fresh id, old row orphaned — never cleaned up by any existing mechanism). The risk register's literal "name/type match" wording suggested 3.2 itself might need a start_line-independent `(name, type)` matcher to close that gap, but that reads like it overlaps with §9's own separate "Symbol-level change detection" section, which is 3.3's dedicated job. **Presented to the user as an explicit choice; the user chose file-level re-keying only** ("sufficient for this milestone") over building a `(name,type)` matcher now. This is the scope actually implemented.
- **`codepulse/git_diff.py` rewritten for rename detection**: `git diff -M --name-status` replaces Phase 3.1's `--no-renames` (an explicit, deliberate inversion of that milestone's own scope boundary — expected, roadmap-driven, not a regression). Parses `R<similarity>\t<old_path>\t<new_path>` status lines (3 tab-separated fields, unlike every other status) into a new `ChangeSet.renamed: list[tuple[str, str]]`. Uses git's own default 50% similarity threshold, unmodified — a move below that threshold is git's own plain delete+add, and this module does not override that judgment (directly matching the risk register's own mitigation: rely on git's rename detection, not a custom heuristic). Copy detection (`-C`) is deliberately NOT enabled — not named by the roadmap. `changed_or_added`/`total_changed` updated to include a rename's new path / count a rename as one changed unit.
- **New `IndexingEngine._rekey_renamed_files(repo_id, renamed_pairs)`** (`codepulse/indexer/engine.py`): for each `(old_path, new_path)`, finds the EXISTING `FileModel` row at `old_path`, computes its symbols' current (old-path-encoded) BM25/Vector doc_ids via a new shared helper (`_doc_ids_for_file`, factored out of `_purge_stale_entries` so both share the exact same doc_id-reconstruction logic), removes those stale doc_ids from both search indexes (doc_id strings embed the file path, so they go stale the moment the path changes even though nothing about the underlying rows needs to change), then updates the row's `path` column to `new_path` **in place** (a plain `UPDATE`, not delete+insert) — preserving `file_id` and therefore every associated `Symbol.id`/`Embedding.id` via the unchanged `file_id` FK. `new_path` is included in this run's `changed_relpaths` (via `ChangeSet.changed_or_added`) so it gets parsed normally immediately after — `_persist_to_db`'s existing File-upsert-by-`(repo_id, path)` lookup then finds the already-re-keyed row and correctly treats it as an update-in-place, not a fresh insert.
- **A real ordering bug found and fixed during wiring, not shipped**: `_purge_stale_entries` is also called (for genuinely modified/deleted paths) in the same `index_directory` block. Since a rename's `new_path` is included in `changed_relpaths`, and `_purge_stale_entries`'s *un-narrowed* input would have included it too, calling it AFTER `_rekey_renamed_files` would have immediately DELETED the very row just carefully re-keyed (a lookup by the now-current `new_path` would find and remove it) — silently undoing the whole point of re-keying on every single rename. **Fixed**: `_rekey_renamed_files` runs first, then `_purge_stale_entries` is called with `(changed_set - renamed_new_paths) | deleted_relpaths`, explicitly excluding renamed files' new paths. Regression-tested (`test_renamed_file_and_an_unrelated_addition_together` proves the symbol id survives when a rename and an ordinary add happen in the same commit).
- **Conservative fallback, per the risk register's own text**: if `_rekey_renamed_files` finds no existing row at `old_path` (e.g. it was excluded by `.codepulseignore` and never actually indexed, even though git still tracked and renamed it), it logs a warning and skips re-keying for that pair — `new_path` still gets indexed normally as a fresh file via the ordinary parse loop (it is already in `changed_relpaths` regardless). Regression-tested (`test_rekey_renamed_files_is_a_safe_no_op_when_the_old_path_was_never_indexed`).
- **`IncrementalPlan`** (dataclass): new `renamed: list[tuple[str, str]]` field. All existing `IncrementalPlan(...)` construction call sites in `_plan_incremental_run` were converted from positional to keyword arguments as part of this change — inserting `renamed` positionally before the pre-existing `reason` field would otherwise have silently rebound every one of the 9 existing call sites' `reason` string into the new `renamed` field. Caught and fixed before running any test.
- **`index_directory()`'s return dict**: new `"renamed_files": [{"old": ..., "new": ...}, ...]` key, mirroring the existing `"deleted_files"` key.
- **What was explicitly NOT done** (the resolved scope fork above, plus deliberate boundaries matching 3.3-3.5's own separate roadmap items): `(name, type)`-based, start_line-independent symbol matching for a rename that also edits content (explicit user decision, see above — left to whatever 3.3 builds for the general modified-file case, if anything); `DependencyModel` (call graph edge) rows recorded before a rename keep referencing the OLD path string — call graph edges are never deduped/corrected across ANY re-index (a pre-existing Phase 0.4 gap, not made worse in kind by renames specifically), and correcting inbound edges after a rename is explicitly §9's "Call graph updates" section, mapped to roadmap items 3.4/3.5, not 3.2; the in-memory `self.call_graph`/`self.call_graphs[repo_id]` (rebuilt fresh from `cg_parsed_files` each run, per Phase 3.1's own merge) IS correct after a rename since it's derived from the freshly re-parsed file under its new path — only the DURABLE `DependencyModel` rows carry old-path debris, and that table is already read live by `GET /v1/symbols/{id}` (`codepulse/api/app.py`), so this is a real, disclosed, pre-existing-in-kind limitation, not a new one; skip-re-embedding for a byte-identical pure rename was NOT implemented (the file still gets fully re-parsed/re-chunked/re-embedded like any other file in `changed_relpaths` — consistent with Phase 3.1's own established behavior that ANY re-parsed file is fully re-embedded regardless of which individual symbols actually changed; true embedding-skip is explicitly 3.3's job).
- **What was verified**: 9 new focused tests (3 in `tests/test_git_diff.py`: pure rename detected as rename — inverting Phase 3.1's own prior test, rename+small-edit still detected as a rename, a move below git's similarity threshold correctly falls back to delete+add, multiple renames together with an add; 6 in `tests/test_incremental_indexing.py`: pure rename preserves File/Symbol/Embedding ids, search results move to the new path with stale old-path doc_ids gone, a rename+content-edit updates the matched symbol's body in place while preserving its id, the defensive no-existing-row fallback, the in-memory call graph reflects the new path with the same edge count, and the ordering-bug regression test).
