## Known Issues

**Open findings from the 2026-10-02 audit (A-H, details + root causes in "Audit 2026-10-02" under Current Implementation State; tracked in `CODEPULSE_FUTURE_CHANGES.md`):** A nonexistent-path ingestion creates phantom repos (Medium); B README overclaims pgvector/Redis/Docker (docs); C fresh DBs never Alembic-stamped, no CLI to stamp/upgrade (latent); D default rate limit vs extension traffic, no 429 retry/`listRepositories` cache (Low-Med); E no `codepulse` console-script entry point, so the extension's default `serverCommand` fails (Medium); F routing-level 404/405 bypass the §21 envelope (Low); G NAT64 addresses rejected by the SSRF check (latent); H 403-vs-404 existence oracle (info). Also open from earlier: per-chunk (not per-symbol) search scoring, and backslash-vs-slash repository path duplicates (`CODEPULSE_FUTURE_CHANGES.md` items 1-2).

**Roadmap work remaining:** NONE of the non-blocked kind. Phases 1-5 and Phase 6.1/7.2/7.4/7.5 are all complete; Phase 6.2 was later built in the separate `codepulse-vscode` repo (see the correction at the top of this document), and Phase 6.3 (Web UI) and Phase 7.1/7.3 (Postgres backend, Docker Compose) remain BLOCKED by genuine platform/scope limitations -- see the standing notice near the top of this document, unchanged since it was written. Beyond the roadmap itself, a separate "developer-ready polish" track (not roadmap sprint items) has also run to completion: the original 6-item backend-gap audit (`VectorIndex.remove_repo`, API key management, module-clustering query surface, restart-survivable lexical search, `PRAGMA foreign_keys`, job-crash reaper) plus a follow-up recheck's 3 findings (the autocomplete/stats repository-isolation leaks, query-side embedding-failure degradation) are all complete -- see "Current Implementation State" above for every entry. `refs`/`callers`/`callees` and module-clustering DO now have CLI/MCP exposure (Phase 6.1 and the module-clustering query-surface milestone respectively) -- the two sentences that used to say otherwise here were stale and have been corrected as part of a documentation-staleness cleanup pass.

**Known technical debt (unchanged from Phase 1.6, re-verified still accurate unless noted):**
- **RESOLVED by the "Backend hardening" milestone** (see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history: `StorageManager.create_tables()` used to unconditionally call `Base.metadata.create_all()` alongside Alembic -- demonstrated risk (Phase 1.3's incident, recurred in Phase 3.1, recurred AGAIN in Phase 3.4 -- three occurrences). Now gated on `inspect(engine).has_table("alembic_version")`: a no-op on any database Alembic has ever touched, unchanged `create_all()` behavior on a genuinely fresh one. Directly regression-tested (`tests/test_migrations.py::test_storage_manager_never_races_ahead_of_a_pending_migration`).
- **RESOLVED by the "`PRAGMA foreign_keys` enforcement" milestone** (see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history: SQLite's `PRAGMA foreign_keys` used to never be enabled anywhere in this codebase, so every `ON DELETE CASCADE` clause declared in the schema was not actually enforced. Now enabled via a `connect`-event listener scoped to each `StorageManager`'s own engine. No repository-deletion endpoint still exists, so this had no PRIOR user-facing impact -- but the schema now correctly enforces integrity whenever one is eventually built, and `CallEdge`/`APIKeyRepositoryModel`/`IndexJobModel`/`RepositorySnapshotModel` rows are no longer silently orphan-able via cascade delete.
- **RESOLVED by the "Dead-code cleanup" milestone** (see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history: `SymbolType.VARIABLE`/`INTERFACE` enum values used to be defined but never produced by either parser -- removed (grep-confirmed unreferenced anywhere, and confirmed absent from the real `codepulse.db`'s own data, before removal). `SymbolType.MODULE` appears similarly unproduced but was NOT part of this cleanup -- flagged, not acted on.
- **RESOLVED by the "Dead-code cleanup" milestone** (see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history: `IndexRepoRequest.repository_id` field in `api/app.py` used to be dead/unused -- removed.
- **RESOLVED by Phase 3.5**, kept here for history: `CallEdge` rows used to duplicate on every re-index. A full run now replaces every row for the repository; an incremental run recomputes only the edges touching changed symbols plus a local BFS one hop out (see "Current Implementation State" above).
- **PARTIALLY RESOLVED by the "Autocomplete + repository-stats repository-isolation fixes" milestone** (see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history: `/v1/autocomplete` and `get_repository_stats`'s `languages` breakdown used to leak every in-memory repository's data regardless of the caller's actual grants (Phase 0.7 finding) -- this was a real, live cross-repository leak, not merely a "data completeness" gap, discovered by a fresh recheck and fixed. `get_index_status`'s equivalent fallback-branch leak remains technically present but is unreachable through normal authorized use (no repository-deletion endpoint exists to make an already-granted `repository_id` vanish) -- left alone, disclosed, not fixed.
- **PARTIALLY RESOLVED by the "Restart-survivable lexical search" milestone** (see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history: `BM25Index`/`CallGraphBuilder` used to have no durable backing and neither survived restart (Phase 0.10 finding). `BM25Index` itself remains in-memory-only (no on-disk Tantivy segments), but `IndexingEngine.hydrate_repository`/`hydrate_all` now rebuild it from durable `symbols`/`files` rows at every engine startup, so it functionally DOES survive a restart now (rehydrated, not merely empty). `CallGraphBuilder` remains genuinely not durable -- unchanged, a separate, larger, already-disclosed Phase 3.1/3.5 limitation.
- **New from the "Restart-survivable lexical search" milestone (not a defect, a disclosed characteristic, newly relevant now that `hydrate_repository`/`hydrate_all` do more work than before):** neither method is safe to call more than once on the same `IndexingEngine` instance -- `bm25_index.add_symbol`/`vector_index.add_batch` both lack dedup-on-add, so a second hydration call would duplicate every entry. Not a regression (the vector half of this always had the same characteristic since Phase 0.5); only ever called once, from `__init__`, in production. Flagged here because a future caller (e.g. a hypothetical "reload index" admin endpoint) must not assume these methods are idempotent without adding a guard first.
- **RESOLVED by the "Query-side embedding-failure degradation" milestone** (see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history: `IndexingEngine.search()` used to not catch `EmbeddingUnavailableError` for the query-side embed call at all, for any search_type. `"hybrid"`/`"rrf"` now gracefully degrade to keyword-only results; `"semantic"` still raises deliberately (no fallback source exists), but `POST /v1/search` now catches it and returns a clear 503 instead of an opaque 500.
- **RESOLVED by Phase 7.4, kept here for history (this bullet was itself stale until a documentation-staleness cleanup pass corrected it):** both `GET /health/live` and `GET /health/ready` now exist (`codepulse/api/app.py`), correctly exempted from the auth requirement per §21's "except `/health/*`" wording, plus `GET /metrics` alongside them. See Phase 7.4's own "Current Implementation State" entry for the full detail.
- No stale-data cleanup for previously-indexed files that become newly ignored (Phase 0.8 finding).
- No nested/per-directory `.gitignore`/`.codepulseignore` support (Phase 0.8 finding).
- The rate limiter's identity extraction (Phase 1.4) reads the raw `X-API-Key` header without validating it -- key rotation can evade per-identity throttling.
- Rate-limit state (Phase 1.4) is entirely in-memory/per-process; does not survive restart or span multiple workers ("no Redis yet" -- explicit, current-scope deferral).
- The advisory one-active-job-per-repository lock (Phase 1.3) remains a TOCTOU-prone application-level check.
- **RESOLVED by the "Job-crash reaper" milestone** (see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history: a `running` job whose background task was killed by a real process crash used to remain stuck at `running` forever (Phase 1.3 finding). Now reaped automatically at every fresh `IndexingEngine` construction (`codepulse.jobs.reap_stale_jobs`), reclaiming the advisory lock for its repository.
- **New from Phase 1.5 (not a defect, a scope note):** `validate_git_url`'s SSRF check protects against a real attack shape (the classic cloud-metadata/internal-service probe) but has no live consumer yet -- there is no git-clone implementation for it to gate in production. It is real, tested, working code, ready for whenever fetching is implemented; do not describe git-URL ingestion itself as supported.
- **New from Phase 1.5 (not a defect, a scope note):** DNS rebinding is not defended against -- `validate_git_url` resolves the host once, synchronously, at request time; a hostname that resolves safely at validation time but is rebound to a private/internal address by the time a (currently nonexistent) fetch step would run is not caught by this check. This is a known, accepted limitation of host-based SSRF validation in general (not specific to this implementation) and is unreachable today regardless, since no fetch step exists to exploit the gap.
- **New from Phase 1.5 (not a defect, a scope note):** the file-count cap is enforced *after* the discovery walk, inside `index_directory()`, not synchronously before a job is created (see "Design decisions" #4 above) -- an oversized repository still consumes one `IndexJob` row (marked `failed`) and the cost of a directory walk before being rejected, unlike the allow-list/SSRF checks which reject before any work or DB write happens.
- **New from Phase 1.5 (not a defect, a scope note):** `ingestion_allowed_roots` defaults to unrestricted (see "Design decisions" #2 above) -- a fresh install is not protected by this setting until an operator explicitly configures it for a non-local-first deployment. This is a deliberate default preserving the zero-configuration local experience, not an oversight; do not describe the endpoint as "path-restricted" without checking whether this setting is actually configured.
- **Open, disclosed specification ambiguity from Phase 1.5, still unresolved by the user as of this update:** whether an unconfigured `ingestion_allowed_roots` should mean unrestricted (current, shipped behavior) or deny-all-until-configured. A dedicated compliance review during Phase 1.5 concluded this is a genuine `SPECIFICATION AMBIGUITY` -- neither the roadmap nor CLAUDE.md states it explicitly either way, and real textual evidence exists on both sides. Shipped as unrestricted, disclosed as a judgment call, not a claimed exact-compliance fact. Not touched by Phase 1.6.
- **New from Phase 1.6 (not a defect, a scope note):** the 500 handler's generic client-facing message ("Internal server error") deliberately omits the real exception type/text to avoid an information-disclosure regression; the real exception is only visible server-side via `logger.exception`. If deeper client-visible diagnostics are ever wanted, that would need its own deliberate scoping (e.g. an opt-in debug mode), not silently added later.
- **New from Phase 1.6 (not a defect, a scope note):** `Starlette`'s `TestClient` defaults to `raise_server_exceptions=True`, which re-raises a genuinely unhandled exception to the calling test even when the app's own exception handler already produced a clean response. Any *future* test that deliberately triggers a 500 to inspect its body must construct its `TestClient` with `raise_server_exceptions=False`, matching `tests/test_error_format.py`'s own `client_no_reraise` fixture -- otherwise the test will see the raw exception instead of the structured response.
- **New from Phase 1.7 (not a defect, a design/interpretation note, disclosed rather than silently decided):** §36 Phase 1's "Tests:" summary line separately names "job lifecycle tests" alongside security tests; this was read as describing what Phase 1.3 already delivered, not a new obligation for 1.7, since no additional job-lifecycle behavior is named anywhere specifically for 1.7. If that reading is wrong, no job-lifecycle test gap has been re-audited under this milestone's name.
- **New from Phase 1.7 (not a defect, a design/interpretation note):** "oversized repo rejected" (§20) was read as mapping only to the file-*count* cap (which fails the job) since that is the one behavior that literally matches the word "rejected" -- the per-file-*size* cap's "skip, job still succeeds" behavior (a deliberate Phase 1.5 design decision) was left as-is, per this milestone's own explicit instruction not to treat it as something to change.
- **New from Phase 1.7 (not a defect, an organizational note):** no dedicated `tests/test_security.py` was created. "Security test suite" was read as the *collection* of security-relevant tests in their natural topical homes (`test_api_authorization.py`, `test_ingestion_safety*.py`), not a mandate for one physical file -- the roadmap does not state which reading is correct.
- **New from Phase 2.1 (a genuine, pre-existing gap, discovered and fixed this milestone -- not previously disclosed anywhere):** the pre-Tantivy `BM25Index` had no doc_id-based replace/upsert logic at all -- repeated `index_directory()` calls on an already-indexed repository silently accumulated duplicate lexical documents in memory, forever, undetected by any existing test (`tests/test_restart_idempotency.py`'s own 5x-reindex test asserted SQL stability only). **Fixed** in Phase 2.1 via `BM25Index.remove_repo()` + delete-by-`repo_id`-then-add-then-commit semantics, and now explicitly regression-tested. Not itself required by Phase 2.1's literal roadmap text, but fixed because it was essentially free and closed a real correctness gap with zero ranking-contract impact.
- **New from Phase 2.1 (not a defect, a scope note):** this project's own `pip install -e .` (editable install) is currently broken by a pre-existing, unrelated `pyproject.toml` packaging configuration issue -- setuptools' automatic package discovery finds two top-level packages (`alembic`, `codepulse`) and refuses to guess which to include, requiring an explicit `packages`/`find` directive that does not currently exist. Discovered while installing the already-declared `tantivy` dependency (installed directly via `pip install "tantivy>=0.22.0,<0.23.0"` instead, per CLAUDE.md §12's rule against opportunistic unrelated fixes). Whoever next needs a clean editable install of this project will hit this.
- **New from Phase 2.1 (not a defect, a disclosed interface-compatibility note):** `BM25Index.k1`/`.b` remain real, settable attributes (for `tests/test_config.py` compatibility) but no longer influence actual scoring -- Tantivy's Python binding (confirmed via direct introspection of the installed 0.22.2 package) exposes no configurable BM25 k1/b parameter anywhere in its `Query`/`Searcher`/`Index` API surface. Tantivy's own fixed internal BM25 implementation is used as-is.
- **New from Phase 2.1 (not a defect, a disclosed candidate-generation note):** Tantivy's own BM25 scoring produces genuinely different raw numeric values than the old hand-rolled Okapi BM25 formula -- explicitly permitted by the roadmap's own Phase 2 framing ("without changing the ranking contract" means the *shape/meaning* `HybridRanker` consumes, not bit-identical arithmetic). The existing `test_bm25_search` ranking-outcome fixture was reproduced and re-verified as a regression proof (same document wins for the same query), not reproduced bit-for-bit.
- **New from Phase 2.2 (an environment-forced dependency decision, disclosed in full):** `hnswlib` (one of §7's two named candidate ANN libraries) could not be installed on this development machine -- no prebuilt wheel exists for Windows/CPython 3.11, and building from source requires an absent MSVC C++ compiler (confirmed by a direct, failed install attempt). `sqlite-vec` (§7's other named candidate) was used instead -- it installed cleanly (a prebuilt `win_amd64` wheel exists).
- **New from Phase 2.2 (a disclosed, real nuance about the library actually used):** `sqlite-vec` 0.1.9's `vec0` virtual table is an **exact** (brute-force) similarity search, not an approximate/graph-based ANN structure, at this version. Confirmed via direct testing (identical, not merely similar, results to the pre-Phase-2.2 NumPy implementation for every query tried). Still satisfies §7's literal wording (one of its two named options), and remains a genuine embedded, compiled-native index -- but is not literally "approximate" the way `hnswlib`'s HNSW algorithm is. A beneficial side effect: retrieval determinism (CLAUDE.md §4) is preserved trivially, not merely "with high probability" the way an approximate structure's would be.
- **ROADMAP DEVIATION — FORMALLY ACCEPTED (post-commit reconciliation, explicit user decision):** after `9fb498a` was committed, a dedicated reconciliation gate re-audited the roadmap and found the weight of evidence (§3, §33's ADR, §34, §35's architecture diagram, §36, §37, §38, §44, §45/B13 -- nine independent occurrences of "ANN"/"HNSW," all contrasting the target against "brute-force"/"linear scan") favors an actual approximate/graph-based structure as Phase 2.2's real intent, not merely "any embedded vector library" -- classified **A. EXPLICITLY REQUIRES ACTUAL ANN**, not merely permitted by §7's looser "e.g., sqlite-vec" mention. `hnswlib` (the roadmap's first-named candidate) was retried after the user installed Microsoft C++ Build Tools and restarted the machine -- **installation still failed identically** (`Microsoft Visual C++ 14.0 or greater is required`), and repeated environment forensics (PATH, `vswhere.exe`, both Program Files roots, both registry hives, whole-`C:\` search) found no trace of a Visual Studio/MSVC installation on this machine, before or after the reported install. A follow-up evaluation of `faiss-cpu` (1.15.0) and `usearch` (2.26.2) in an isolated, outside-the-repo venv found **both install cleanly with prebuilt Windows wheels** (no compiler needed), and `faiss.IndexHNSWFlat` was directly confirmed via introspection (a real `.hnsw` sub-object exposing `efConstruction`, `efSearch`, `entry_point`, `max_level`, `levels`, `neighbors` -- genuine HNSW graph internals, not a linear-scan wrapper) to be an actual approximate structure, not brute-force. **This evaluation was deliberately stopped mid-way and NOT completed** -- the user explicitly instructed: *"Just use sqlite-vec, document the deviation, and proceed."* This is recorded here as a conscious, informed, user-made decision, not a silent one and not a claim that `sqlite-vec` is equivalent to ANN: **Phase 2.2 (`9fb498a`) is accepted as final with a known, explicit, deliberate deviation from the roadmap's literal "embedded ANN"/"HNSW" language** -- `VectorIndex` provides an embedded, deterministic, exact (not approximate) vector search. If a genuine ANN structure is wanted later, `faiss-cpu`'s `IndexHNSWFlat` is the nearest-verified, installable-today candidate (unlike `hnswlib`, which remains blocked on this machine) -- this was not pursued further only because the user chose to stop here, not because it was found unviable.
- **New from Phase 2.2 (a genuine, disclosed, pre-existing gap NOT fixed this milestone, unlike its Phase 2.1 lexical counterpart):** `VectorIndex.add`/`add_batch` remain pure-append operations with no doc_id/repository-based replace logic -- re-indexing an already-indexed repository silently accumulates duplicate vector entries, exactly as it already did before Phase 2.2 (this was never fixed for the vector index the way Phase 2.1 fixed it for `BM25Index.remove_repo`). This is a deliberate Phase 2.2 scope decision (CLAUDE.md §12: not required by this milestone's literal text, so not opportunistically expanded into), explicitly flagged to the user in this session's own final report as a candidate fast-follow-up, not applied unilaterally. Now explicitly regression-tested (`tests/test_sqlite_vec_semantic_index.py::test_known_limitation_repeated_add_of_the_same_doc_id_accumulates_duplicates`) so it cannot silently change without a test noticing. **A real, practical consequence**: re-indexing a repository multiple times will, over time, degrade semantic search quality/performance for that repository (duplicate near-identical candidates crowding out other results) in a way lexical search no longer does after Phase 2.1.
- **New from Phase 2.2 (a necessary, disclosed, verified design adjustment):** `VectorIndex`'s underlying `sqlite3.Connection` is constructed with `check_same_thread=False`, since FastAPI/Starlette's shared threadpool means the same `IndexingEngine`/`VectorIndex` instance can legitimately be called from more than one OS thread across its lifetime -- verified experimentally (a real cross-thread write-then-read round trip) to behave correctly for this codebase's actual usage pattern. This avoids a new hard-crash mode Phase 2.2 would otherwise introduce (the pre-existing NumPy/list implementation had no thread-affinity restriction); it is not a claim of full concurrent-write safety, matching the codebase's existing (unchanged) lack of locking around `bm25_index`/`vector_index`.
- **New from Phase 2.3 (not a defect, a disclosed scope note):** `codepulse/cli.py`'s `--type`/`-t` search-type argument still only accepts `["hybrid", "keyword", "semantic"]` -- `"rrf"` was deliberately not added to its `choices=[...]` list. §37 item 2.3's literal text says "selectable via **API** `search_type`/config," naming the API specifically; `IndexingEngine.search()` itself supports `"rrf"` uniformly for every caller already (CLAUDE.md §3's shared-intelligence-core rule is satisfied), but the CLI's own argparse restriction was left as a narrow, deliberate scope boundary rather than silently expanded. Trivial follow-up whenever wanted: widen `search_p.add_argument("--type", ...)`'s `choices`.
- **New from Phase 2.3 (not a defect, a disclosed design note):** an RRF-produced result's `final_score`/`relevance_score` is the real, unclamped, computed RRF score (`sum(1/(k+rank))`), genuinely on a different numeric scale than `hybrid`/`keyword`/`semantic`'s weighted-linear scores (which are clamped to `[0, 1]`). This is not a defect or an inconsistency to fix -- inventing a rescaling to make RRF's score "look like" the other search_types' would itself be a fabricated normalization the roadmap does not ask for. A client comparing relevance scores *across* different `search_type` values for the same query should be aware the two scales are not directly comparable; comparing rankings *within* one `search_type`'s results remains fully meaningful.
- **New from Phase 2.4 (not a defect, a disclosed scope note):** "gated in CI" (§36/§45's phase-level DoD wording) was not literally implemented -- no `.github/workflows` or any CI system exists anywhere in this repository, confirmed before this milestone began. The regression gate is expressed as a real pytest assertion (`tests/test_relevance_eval.py::test_hybrid_search_relevance_meets_the_measured_baseline`) against a measured baseline instead -- this project's only existing gating mechanism. The moment any CI system is wired up to run `pytest`, this gate is "in CI" automatically; building that CI infrastructure from scratch was judged out of this milestone's literal scope (§37 item 2.4 says "dataset + eval harness," not "CI pipeline").
- **New from Phase 2.4 (not a defect, a disclosed scope decision):** only `hybrid` search_type's Recall@10/MRR is gated by an assertion. `keyword` (0.9645/0.8324), `semantic` (0.8511/0.6605), and `rrf` (0.9433/0.5976) were all measured and are reported in "Current Implementation State" above for transparency, but none is individually enforced -- §20's own wording ("a starting relevance dataset... a regression gate") reads as establishing one primary baseline, matching `hybrid` being the API's own default `search_type`, not a mandate to gate all four search strategies independently. Revisit if a future milestone wants per-search_type gating.
- **New from Phase 2.4 (not a defect, a genuine, disclosed finding):** RRF's MRR (0.5976) measured lower than every other search_type despite RRF's Recall@10 (0.9433) being the second-highest of the four -- a real, measured result, not a bug: MRR specifically rewards placing the single best match at rank 1, and RRF's rank-based (not score-based) fusion formula does not optimize for that the same way weighted-linear fusion does. Not investigated further; out of this milestone's scope (this milestone measures, it does not redesign ranking).
- **New from Phase 2.5 (A REAL BUG, DISCOVERED AND FIXED THIS MILESTONE -- not previously disclosed anywhere):** `sqlite-vec`'s `vec0` KNN query enforces a hard `k <= 4096` limit. `IndexingEngine.search()`'s repository-scoped candidate-pool sizing (Phase 0.6) requests `top_k = len(vector_index.doc_ids)` (the entire corpus) for scoped queries, which exceeds 4096 at realistic scale (~10K files -> ~40,000 embedded chunks) -- meaning **any repository-scoped semantic/hybrid search against a moderately large real repository has been silently crashable with `sqlite3.OperationalError` since Phase 2.2's commit (`9fb498a`)**, undetected by every Phase 2.2 test (all used small corpora). Discovered only because Phase 2.5's benchmark harness actually ran a realistic-scale scoped query for the first time. **Fixed** by clamping `top_k` at `min(top_k, len(doc_ids), 4096)` inside `VectorIndex.search()` itself. See "Current Implementation State" above for the full disclosed trade-off (a theoretical, unreachable-in-this-milestone completeness risk for a very large multi-repository deployment).
- **New from Phase 2.5 (not a defect, a disclosed scope/sizing decision):** the "large" benchmark tier used **25,000 files, not the roadmap's literal ~100K+** -- §19's own table characterizes that tier's index time as "Hours (background, not blocking)"; real throughput calibration in this session (~48 symbols/sec) put a literal ~100K-file run at an estimated 70-90+ minutes with untested memory/scaling behavior at that size, judged too large a single-run risk for this session. The harness (`tests/benchmark/run_benchmark.py`) is fully capable of running the literal scale later -- edit `TIERS` and run it.
- **New from Phase 2.5 (a real, measured, disclosed finding -- not spun or hidden):** every one of the three benchmark tiers' search p95 latency measured OVER its §19 budget (~1K: 237.0ms vs. <100ms budget; ~10K: 704.2ms vs. <200ms budget; large/25K: 1,080.5ms vs. <400ms budget for the literal ~100K+ tier). Index time was within budget for the ~1K tier only marginally missed it (70.4s vs. <60s) and the ~10K tier met it (447.9s vs. <600s budget). Reported factually per this milestone's own DoD ("measured... numbers... replacing the old unverified target," whichever way they land) -- no optimization work was attempted; that is not this milestone's scope.
- **SUPERSEDED by Phase 3.2, kept here for history only:** Phase 3.1 originally, deliberately, detected a rename/move as a delete+add (via `git diff --no-renames`), so that roadmap item 3.2 was not silently done early. Phase 3.2 has since implemented real rename detection (`git diff -M`) and re-keying -- see the Phase 3.2 bullets immediately below for the current, actual behavior. Do not read this bullet as describing the current codebase.
- **New from Phase 3.1 (not a defect, a disclosed cost-saving limitation):** incremental re-indexing skips unchanged FILES entirely, but does not skip re-embedding unchanged SYMBOLS *within* a changed file -- every symbol in a modified file is re-chunked and re-submitted for embedding, even one whose own body is byte-identical to before. §9 literally describes symbol-level `body_hash` comparison as "the single biggest cost saver on incremental runs" -- that is roadmap item 3.3, not yet implemented. The DB-level symbol upsert (Phase 0.4) still correctly avoids a redundant *write* for an unchanged symbol, but the embedding *model call* itself is not currently skipped.
- **New from Phase 3.1 (a genuine, disclosed limitation of the call-graph-completeness fix):** keeping `self.call_graphs[repo_id]` complete across an incremental run relies on `self.parse_results`, an in-memory, non-durable, per-process cache (now repo-qualified as `"{repo_id}::{rel_path}"`, a required change since this milestone made it load-bearing for the first time -- previously write-only). After a process restart, this cache is empty; `_plan_incremental_run`'s new "no in-memory call graph cached yet for this repository" fallback guard (checked whenever `repo_id not in self.call_graphs`) is what prevents a silently-incomplete call graph in that case, by forcing a full run instead. Regression-tested (`test_falls_back_to_full_reindex_after_a_fresh_engine_with_no_cached_call_graph`). True durable, restart-safe incremental call-graph maintenance is roadmap item 3.5's job, not this milestone's.
- **New from Phase 3.1 (a real, self-identified-and-fixed correctness gap -- not left as a known limitation):** without an explicit fix, a modified file's re-parsed symbols would have accumulated as duplicate lexical/vector entries alongside their own stale, pre-modification versions (since `doc_id` is stable across a content-only change). Fixed via `_purge_stale_entries`, applied to modified files BEFORE they are re-parsed (as well as to permanently-deleted files) -- see "Current Implementation State" above for the full explanation and its regression test.
- **New from Phase 3.1 (a real, self-identified-and-fixed correctness gap -- not left as a known limitation):** `RepositoryModel.file_count`/`symbol_count` would have silently reported only the changed-file subset's count after an incremental run (a fabricated-metrics violation) had `_persist_to_db` not been changed to compute them from a real `SELECT COUNT(*)` against the DB instead of `len(parsed_files)`/`len(all_symbols)`. See "Current Implementation State" above.
- **New from Phase 3.1 (a real, self-identified-and-fixed correctness gap -- not left as a known limitation):** `IndexingEngine.index_directory`'s pre-existing `self.bm25_index.remove_repo(repo_id)` call (Phase 2.1) would have silently wiped every unchanged file's lexical documents on an incremental run had it not been gated on `not incremental_plan.is_incremental`. See "Current Implementation State" above.
- **New from Phase 3.1 (not a defect, a pre-existing incident recurring in a new form):** applying the `c286d277ea21` migration to the real `codepulse.db` hit `table repository_snapshots already exists` -- the exact same class of incident as Phase 1.3's `index_jobs` incident (a `StorageManager()` constructed against the real DB, at some point after the new model was added to `db.py`, silently created the table via `create_all()`, bypassing Alembic). Remediated identically (`stamp_database`, verified schema/data integrity first). `StorageManager.create_tables()`'s `create_all()`-alongside-Alembic behavior remains the recurring root cause, still not fixed (see "Known technical debt" below, tracked since Phase 1.3).
- **New from Phase 3.2 (a real, disclosed, user-confirmed scope boundary -- not silently picked):** a rename that ALSO edits content such that a symbol's own `start_line` shifts is not specially matched -- that symbol gets a fresh id (the old row orphaned, never cleaned up), exactly as an ordinary modified file already behaves today under the pre-existing `(file_id, name, start_line)` Symbol natural key (no `(name, type)`-based, start_line-independent matching exists anywhere in this codebase). A full read-only audit surfaced this fork explicitly and the user chose file-level re-keying only over building a start_line-independent matcher now (see "Current Implementation State" above) -- **only a PURE rename (byte-identical content move) is guaranteed to preserve every symbol's id.**
- **New from Phase 3.2 (not a defect, a disclosed, pre-existing-in-kind limitation, now also reachable via renames specifically):** `DependencyModel` (call graph edge) rows recorded before a rename keep referencing the file's OLD path string -- `GET /v1/symbols/{id}` (`codepulse/api/app.py`) reads this table live and will show the stale old path in `called_by`/`calls` for edges recorded before the rename. Dependency rows are never deduped/corrected across ANY re-index (a pre-existing Phase 0.4 gap, unchanged, not made worse IN KIND by this milestone -- an ordinary modified file already has this same staleness risk for its own edges). Correcting inbound edges after a rename is explicitly assigned by §9 ("Call graph updates... a symbol being renamed/deleted must have inbound edges from unrelated files corrected too") to roadmap items 3.4/3.5, not 3.2. The IN-MEMORY call graph (`self.call_graph`/`self.call_graphs[repo_id]`) is NOT affected -- it is rebuilt fresh each run from the renamed file's newly re-parsed content under its new path, so live in-process queries are correct; only the durable `DependencyModel` table carries old-path debris.
- **STILL TRUE after Phase 3.3, by that milestone's own deliberate exclusion:** skip-re-embedding for a byte-identical pure rename is still not implemented -- a renamed file (pure or edited) still gets fully re-parsed/re-chunked/re-embedded, because Phase 3.3's own symbol-level skip mechanism deliberately excludes any file renamed this run (every symbol's doc_id embeds the file path, so it changes regardless of body content -- see the Phase 3.3 bullets below). Reusing a durably-stored embedding vector under a renamed file's new doc_id is a disclosed, deferred enhancement, not built by either milestone.
- **New from Phase 3.3 (a real, disclosed, deliberate scope boundary, mirroring the Phase 3.2 precedent -- not silently picked):** the symbol-level skip mechanism matches by `(name, start_line)` plus `type` -- the SAME key `_persist_to_db`'s Symbol upsert already uses -- not §9's fuller, start_line-independent `(name, type)` matcher with a signature-similarity fallback. A symbol whose `start_line` shifts (because unrelated code above it in the file changed) is not recognized as unchanged and gets a fresh re-chunk/re-embed, exactly the pre-existing Phase 0.4 behavior for that case -- not a new regression, but not the fuller cost-saving §9 describes either. See "Current Implementation State" above.
- **New from Phase 3.3 (not a defect, a disclosed design boundary):** the skip mechanism is applied ONLY during incremental runs -- never during a full re-index, since a full run's unconditional `bm25_index.remove_repo` wipe would make skipping any symbol's BM25 re-add a real data-loss bug. This is enforced by construction (the diff method that populates `unchanged_symbol_ids` is only ever called during an incremental run), not a runtime check alone.
- **RESOLVED by Phase 3.3, kept here for history:** a symbol that disappears from (or is renamed within) a still-existing, re-parsed file now has its stale `SymbolModel`/`Embedding` rows actually deleted -- closing, for that specific case, the long-standing gap `_persist_to_db`'s own docstring has disclosed since Phase 0.4 ("this mirrors Phase 0.4's decision not to delete stale Symbol rows... real stale-data cleanup is Phase 3"). This does NOT generalize to every symbol-identity-change scenario (e.g. a symbol whose `start_line` shifts is still not matched, per the bullet above, and its old row is still not cleaned up in that case) -- only the "genuinely absent from the new parse" case is now handled.
- **`DependencyModel` renamed to `CallEdge` in Phase 3.4** (table `dependencies` -> `call_edges`) -- any external tooling, ad-hoc SQL, or documentation still referring to the old names needs updating; the Python class/table names are gone, not aliased.
- **New from Phase 3.4 (not a defect, a disclosed scope boundary matching a different, later roadmap item):** `CallEdge.confidence` is computed and persisted per single edge only -- the multi-hop, BFS-based `get_callers`/`get_callees` (used by the CLI's `impact` command and the API's impact-analysis endpoint) do NOT carry or aggregate confidence across a path. Extending them to do so reads as roadmap item 4.2's "direct vs. transitive bucketing" territory, a different, later milestone.
- **New from Phase 3.4 (not a defect, a disclosed design boundary, deliberately conservative):** an import that resolves to a real target file but whose exact callee name can't be confirmed as a known symbol in it (e.g. a wildcard import, a re-exported alias) is classified `"unresolved"`, not `"low"` or `"high"` -- `"low"` is defined as a SAME-FILE match, so this cross-file, partially-resolved case doesn't fit either of the other two tiers honestly.
- **New from Phase 3.4 (a real, disclosed, THIRD occurrence of a recurring incident):** applying the `f6e481353553` migration to the real `codepulse.db` hit the exact same `create_all()`-alongside-Alembic incident class as Phase 1.3 (`index_jobs`) and Phase 3.1 (`repository_snapshots`) -- this time an accidentally-created, empty `call_edges` table blocked the migration's table-rename step outright (unlike the previous two, `stamp_database` alone could not fix this one, since real row data needed to move). Remediated by verifying the accidental table was genuinely empty/schema-correct, backing up the real DB, dropping the accidental table, then running the real migration normally -- see "Current Implementation State" above for the full incident. This is the THIRD occurrence of this exact root cause; see "Known technical debt" above.
- **New from the repository-isolation fix (a deliberate mitigation, proving the root cause is avoidable with care, NOT a fix to the root cause itself):** applying the `2cd38eb12a7e` migration to the real `codepulse.db` did NOT hit a fourth `create_all()` incident -- `codepulse.api.app` was deliberately never imported against the real database this time (its syntax was checked with `py_compile` instead of a real import, and all real-DB inspection used a plain `sqlite3` connection, never the ORM). This confirms the incident is entirely avoidable by discipline alone, which is exactly why it kept recurring -- discipline is not a substitute for actually fixing `StorageManager.create_tables()`'s unconditional `create_all()` call.
- **New from Phase 3.5 (not a defect, a disclosed scope boundary):** the in-memory call graph cache's own restart-survivability (`self.parse_results`/`self.call_graphs`, lost on a process restart, with `_plan_incremental_run`'s existing "no in-memory call graph cached yet" guard forcing a full run in that case) is completely unchanged by this milestone -- Phase 3.5 fixed the DURABLE `CallEdge` table's update strategy, not the in-memory cache's durability. No roadmap item currently claims to fix that.
- **New from Phase 3.5 (not a defect, a disclosed scope boundary matching a different roadmap item, consistent with Phase 3.4's own identical disclosure):** the incremental update's `changed_symbol_keys` correctly follows whatever the EXISTING `(file_id, name, start_line)` Symbol natural key produces -- it does not change that key. A symbol whose `start_line` shifts without a name/type change (Phase 3.2/3.3's own disclosed, unrelated limitation) is therefore still not recognized as identity-stable by this milestone's BFS either.
- **New from Phase 4.1 (not a defect, a disclosed scope boundary, explicit in the user's own approved plan):** inheritance data (`Symbol.base_classes`, `CallEdge.relationship_type="inherits"`) is extracted, resolved, and persisted, but NOT YET exposed through any API endpoint, CLI command, or MCP tool -- no query surface was built this milestone (explicitly out of scope; `refs`/`callers`/`callees` CLI exposure is Phase 6's territory per the roadmap's own sprint table). A caller wanting "what does X inherit from" today has no way to ask except direct DB/in-memory-graph inspection.
- **CONFIRMED by Phase 4.2, kept here for history:** `CallEdge.confidence` for an inheritance edge is a SINGLE-edge value only -- there is no multi-hop inheritance-chain confidence bucketing. Phase 4.2's own explicit, approved Decision 1 (impact analysis is calls-only) confirms this is NOT that milestone's territory either -- inheritance-aware impact/confidence bucketing remains a genuinely unassigned, deferred future capability, not implicitly covered by anything shipped so far.
- **New from Phase 4.1 (not a defect, a disclosed language-scope boundary):** TypeScript's `implements` (interface implementation) is deliberately never extracted as inheritance -- only `extends`. If a future milestone wants interface-implementation tracking, it needs its own explicit scoping; it is not "almost done" by this milestone's code.
- **New from Phase 4.1 (not a defect, a disclosed real-DB state note):** the real `codepulse.db`'s `symbols.base_classes` is `NULL` for all 128 existing rows as of this update -- the migration adds the column but does not (and should not) retroactively populate historical data; it will populate correctly the next time this repository is re-indexed with the new parser code.
- **New from Phase 4.2 (not a defect, a disclosed scope boundary, explicit user decision -- Decision 1):** impact analysis (`IndexingEngine.analyze_impact`) is CALLS ONLY. A class's inheritance relationships (Phase 4.1) never appear in `callers`/`callees`/`call_chain`/`dependencies`, even though a base class's behavior change genuinely can affect its subclasses in practice. Inheritance-aware impact analysis was explicitly discussed and explicitly deferred, not overlooked -- see the Phase 4.2 architecture audit and its approval for the full reasoning.
- **New from Phase 4.2 (not a defect, a disclosed scope boundary):** no `files` rollup was added to the impact-analysis response (explicitly declined during the Phase 4.2 approval) -- a caller wanting "which FILES are affected" must derive that itself by deduping the `file` field across the returned symbol-level entries.
- **New from Phase 4.2 (not a defect, a disclosed design note):** `IndexingEngine.analyze_impact`'s deterministic sort ranks `None` confidence (a legacy pre-Phase-3.4 row, or a callee with no resolved target file) LAST, after `high`/`low`/`unresolved` -- consistent with CLAUDE.md's "no fabricated metrics" rule (known-good information is never displaced by absent information) but worth knowing if a caller expected `None` to sort first/alphabetically instead.
- **New from Phase 4.3 (not a defect, a disclosed scope boundary, explicit user decision):** module clustering (`ModuleGraphBuilder`, `IndexingEngine.analyze_module_structure`) has NO REST/CLI/MCP surface at all -- not wired into anything a human or agent can currently call except direct Python. B18's own "Technical Scope: New graph-analysis module" wording (no `api/app.py`/`cli.py` named) is the textual basis for this scope decision; a future milestone (plausibly Phase 5's context-bundle work, or Phase 6's CLI/Web UI) is the natural place to add a caller, and can do so without duplicating any construction logic.
- **New from Phase 4.3 (not a defect, a disclosed, empirically-grounded design note):** the Louvain clustering seed (`ModuleGraphBuilder._LOUVAIN_SEED = 0`) is a fixed internal constant, not exposed as a parameter this milestone. Empirically confirmed during the Phase 4.3 audit that a DIFFERENT seed value can produce a genuinely different partition of the identical import graph -- the seed is load-bearing for determinism, not decorative, and must never be changed casually or made per-call-random without re-verifying every determinism test in `tests/test_module_graph.py`.
- **New from Phase 4.3 (not a defect, a disclosed literal-vs-conventional-terminology tension):** `ModuleGraphBuilder.rank_by_inbound()` preserves §11's own literal wording ("many inbound, few outbound = looks like an API/entry point") even though this is the OPPOSITE of how many developers intuitively use "entry point" (conventionally: low inbound, high outbound -- a `cli.py`/`main.py`/`app.py`-shaped file). Empirically, on CodePulse's own repository, this literal heuristic ranks `config.py`/`models.py` (not `cli.py`/`app.py`) as the top "API/entry point" signals. Implemented exactly as the roadmap states, not silently corrected to match conventional intuition -- flag for an explicit decision if this ever gets a label/name surfaced to an actual caller.
- **New from Phase 4.3 (not a defect, a disclosed dependency note):** `networkx` was already installed in every environment that has ever run this project's test suite (a transitive dependency of `torch`), so declaring it explicitly in `pyproject.toml` changes nothing about what gets installed anywhere today -- it only removes the risk of a future `torch` version silently dropping that transitive requirement out from under `codepulse/parser/module_graph.py`.
- **New from Phase 4.4 (not a defect, a disclosed scope boundary):** `GET /v1/symbols/{id}/references` has no CLI or MCP counterpart yet (Phase 6.1 and Phase 5.3's own respective jobs) -- REST-only for now, consistent with B17/B18's own precedent of building the shared-core capability first and exposing it incrementally.
- **New from Phase 4.4 (not a defect, a disclosed design note):** `references` returns DIRECT usages only (single-hop) -- it is not a substitute for `impact-analysis`'s transitive bucketing, and the two must never be conflated by a future caller. A symbol with zero direct callers can still have many transitive ones (visible only via `impact-analysis`).
- **RESOLVED by Phase 5.1**, kept here for history: `POST /v1/symbols/{id}/similar`'s vector search used to have NO `repo_id` filter at all -- a real, live cross-repository leak, present since this endpoint was first built, undetected by any prior test (no test existed that checked `/similar`'s actual result CONTENT, only its auth status code). Found and fixed while extracting `IndexingEngine.find_similar_symbols`; see "Current Implementation State" above.
- **New from Phase 5.1 (not a defect, a disclosed design note):** a single symbol can legitimately produce more than one indexed vector/chunk entry (a pre-existing `SemanticChunker` behavior, not introduced by Phase 5.1) -- `find_similar_symbols`/`related_symbols` can therefore return the SAME `symbol_name` more than once if multiple of its chunks independently clear the similarity threshold. Not deduplicated by this milestone (out of its scope; a caller wanting one-entry-per-symbol must dedupe client-side for now).
- **New from Phase 5.3 (not a defect, a disclosed scope boundary):** `mcp_server.py` builds a `FastMCP` server OBJECT and tests it via `call_tool`/`list_tools` directly -- nothing in this codebase yet actually STARTS a long-running MCP server process (`mcp.run_stdio_async()` et al. are never called anywhere). `codepulse serve` (Phase 6.1's own CLI work) is the natural place to wire this up; until then, the MCP server exists and is fully tested, but is not reachable by any real MCP client.
- **New from Phase 5.3 (not a defect, a disclosed, deliberate duplication):** `get_symbol`'s `called_by`/`calls` query is re-implemented (identically) inside `mcp_server.py` rather than extracted into a third shared location alongside the REST route's own copy -- a plain parameterized DB read (not a graph algorithm), judged not worth a deeper refactor mid-continuous-execution. A future drift risk if the REST route's filter ever changes without updating this copy; both are independently tested today.
- **RESOLVED (see the new "`CallEdge` repository-isolation fix" section in "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history:** a Phase 3.4 pre-commit audit found, and confirmed LIVE, that `GET /v1/symbols/{id}` leaked another repository's call-graph data across the repository-isolation boundary (`CallEdge` had no `repo_id` column at all; `called_by`/`calls` were queried by symbol name only). Fixed with a new `CallEdge.repo_id` column (migration `2cd38eb12a7e`, all 528 real rows backfilled), a query fix in `GET /v1/symbols/{id}`, and a related but distinct fix in `POST /v1/symbols/{id}/impact-analysis` (which used `indexing_engine.call_graph`, the singular "most recently indexed" alias, instead of the already-existing, correctly-scoped `call_graphs` dict). Both were pre-existing since Phase 0.4/0.6 respectively, NOT introduced by Phase 3.4 -- that milestone's own audit is what found them.

**Blockers:** none currently known for starting Phase 3.2.

## Deferred Work

Per roadmap §39 — do not implement until the roadmap explicitly reaches these:

- Kubernetes, Redis, distributed workers, microservices
- Enterprise SSO, full organization/user tenancy
- Learned/adaptive ranking, LLM reranking, LLM-generated explanations as source of truth
- PostgreSQL/pgvector (Phase 7 -- **Phase 2 in its entirety -- 2.1 through 2.5 -- is now DONE, removed from this list**)
- Running the benchmark harness at the roadmap's literal ~100K+ file tier -- Phase 2.5 used a reduced 25,000-file substitute (deliberate, disclosed, throughput-calibrated scope decision; see Known Issues). The harness itself supports the literal scale; revisit whenever someone is prepared to dedicate the estimated 70-90+ minutes of wall-clock time.
- Optimizing search latency to meet §19's budget table -- Phase 2.5 measured and published real numbers, several of which exceed the stated budgets (see Known Issues); no optimization work was attempted, as that is not this milestone's scope.
- A real CI pipeline (`.github/workflows` or otherwise) -- none exists anywhere in this repository; not built as part of Phase 2.4 (deliberate, disclosed scope decision -- see Known Issues). Revisit whenever the project actually needs automated CI, not assigned to a specific milestone by the roadmap.
- Gating `keyword`/`semantic`/`rrf` search_types' Recall@10/MRR individually (only `hybrid` is gated as of Phase 2.4, deliberate scope decision, disclosed above).
- `codepulse/cli.py`'s `--type` argument accepting `"rrf"` -- deliberately not done in Phase 2.3 (§37 item 2.3 names the API specifically, not the CLI); trivial fast-follow-up whenever wanted.
- **PARTIALLY ADDRESSED by the "Restart-survivable lexical search" milestone** (see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history: on-disk Tantivy/sqlite-vec index-SEGMENT persistence itself still does not exist -- no Tantivy segment files, no file-backed sqlite-vec table, index-segment versioning/corruption-recovery, or physical per-repository segmentation. What changed is that the PRACTICAL consequence (keyword search returning nothing until a fresh index after a restart) is now closed by a different mechanism -- automatic rehydration from `codepulse.db`'s own durable `symbols`/`files` tables at every engine startup, not literal index-file persistence. True on-disk index-segment persistence remains unbuilt and unassigned to any milestone; revisit only if a concrete need for it (vs. the SQL-rehydration approach already shipped) arises.
- `hnswlib` -- attempted for Phase 2.2, blocked by this development environment (no prebuilt wheel, no C++ compiler); `sqlite-vec` was used instead. Revisit only if a future need specifically requires `hnswlib`'s approximate/graph-based algorithm (e.g. a measured Phase 2.5 benchmark shows `sqlite-vec`'s exact scan is too slow at a real target corpus size) and a build toolchain becomes available.
- **RESOLVED by the "Backend hardening" milestone** (see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history: `VectorIndex` repository-replacement (delete-by-repo-then-add) idempotency -- not implemented in Phase 2.2, now implemented via `VectorIndex.remove_repo(repo_id)` + its call site in `IndexingEngine.index_directory()`.
- Actual git-URL cloning/fetching; request-body size caps; a global/per-tenant concurrent-job cap (distinct from Phase 1.3's per-repository lock) -- none assigned a milestone anywhere in this roadmap
- **RESOLVED**, kept here for history: a job reaper -- implemented as a startup-time reconciliation (`codepulse.jobs.reap_stale_jobs`), not a heartbeat/timeout mechanism; see "Current Implementation State"'s "Job-crash reaper" entry for why that design is correct for this project's actual single-process architecture.
- `(name, type)`-based, start_line-independent symbol matching, with a signature-similarity fallback -- explicitly NOT built by either Phase 3.2 (for a rename that also edits content) or Phase 3.3 (for an ordinary modified file whose symbol lines shifted); both milestones deliberately matched via the SAME existing `(file_id, name, start_line)` key instead, consistent choices disclosed in each one's own Known Issues entry. Only a symbol whose `start_line` is unchanged is guaranteed to be recognized as identity-stable today.
- Skip-re-embedding for a byte-identical pure rename -- explicitly NOT built by Phase 3.3, which deliberately excludes any file renamed this run from its symbol-level skip mechanism (every symbol's doc_id embeds the file path and must move regardless of body content). Reusing a durably-stored embedding vector under a renamed file's new doc_id is a disclosed, deferred enhancement.
- §17's full crash-safe "always serve latest ready snapshot" read semantics -- `RepositorySnapshotModel` (Phase 3.1) is a run-history table only; the broader read-path semantics are not implemented
- **RESOLVED by Phase 7.2**, kept here for history (this bullet was itself stale until a documentation-staleness cleanup pass corrected it): role-based permissions (viewer/indexer/admin) on API keys are implemented -- `APIKeyRepositoryModel.role`, `AuthorizedKey.authorize_role`, enforced on repository re-indexing. See Phase 7.2's own "Current Implementation State" entry.
- **RESOLVED (API key management milestone, see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history:** API key revoke() flow -- now implemented (`codepulse.auth.revoke_api_key` + `DELETE /v1/admin/keys/{id}`). Key expiry / audit logging remain open; a job cancel() flow remains open.
- The in-memory call graph cache's own restart-survivability (Phase 3.1's best-effort, single-process-lifetime stopgap, `self.parse_results`/`self.call_graphs`) -- STILL genuinely open; explicitly NOT what Phase 3.5 fixed (that milestone fixed the DURABLE `CallEdge` table's update strategy instead) and NOT addressed by the later "restart-survivable lexical search" milestone either (that fixed `bm25_index` specifically, not the call graph). Stale-data cleanup for newly-ignored files (Phase 0.8 finding) also remains open. Two items this bullet used to also list are now RESOLVED and removed from it as part of a documentation-staleness cleanup pass: multi-hop confidence bucketing for `get_callers`/`get_callees` (done, Phase 4.2) and module clustering's query surface (done, the module-clustering query-surface milestone).
- **RESOLVED by Phase 5.3**, kept here for history (this bullet was itself stale until a documentation-staleness cleanup pass corrected it): the MCP server exists -- `codepulse/mcp_server.py`, 8 tools (7 named by §13 plus a later `module_structure` addition), reachable via `codepulse serve --mcp` (Phase 6.1).
- Web UI (Phase 6.3) -- not started. (The VS Code extension, Phase 6.2, has since been built in the separate `codepulse-vscode` repo; see the correction at the top of this document.)
- **RESOLVED by Phase 7.4**, kept here for history (this bullet was itself stale until a documentation-staleness cleanup pass corrected it): observability infrastructure is wired in -- real Prometheus metrics (`codepulse/observability.py`), structured logging, `/health/live`, `/health/ready`, `/metrics`.

## Current Risks

- `_persist_to_db` still has four upsert blocks plus the dependency-insert block in one function/session — unchanged by Phase 2.4.
- **RESOLVED (`languages` half) by the "Autocomplete + repository-stats repository-isolation fixes" milestone**, kept here for history (this bullet was itself stale until a documentation-staleness cleanup pass corrected it): `get_repository_stats`'s `languages` breakdown no longer leaks other repositories' data. `get_index_status`'s equivalent fallback-branch leak remains technically present but unreachable through normal authorized use (see that milestone's own Known Issues entry above for why).
- **RESOLVED by the "Backend hardening" milestone**, kept here for history (this bullet was itself stale until a documentation-staleness cleanup pass corrected it -- see the fuller, already-correct duplicate of this entry further down this same section): `StorageManager.create_tables()`'s `create_all()`-alongside-Alembic behavior is no longer a risk, gated on `inspect(engine).has_table("alembic_version")`.
- Rate-limit identity evasion via key rotation (Phase 1.4) means the limiter is a resource-exhaustion speed bump, not a strong anti-abuse guarantee.
- `ingestion_allowed_roots`'s unrestricted-by-default behavior (Phase 1.5) remains an open, disclosed specification ambiguity, not yet resolved by the user. Phase 2.4 was not instructed to and did not touch it.
- `validate_git_url`'s SSRF check has no live fetch path to protect yet -- do not describe git-URL ingestion as supported.
- This project's `pip install -e .` is currently broken by a pre-existing, unrelated packaging configuration issue (discovered during Phase 2.1, re-confirmed during Phase 2.2 -- see Known Issues); anyone needing a fresh editable install will hit this until it's fixed under its own milestone.
- `IndexingEngine.symbols_by_id`/`.symbol_repo_map` retain stale entries for symbols removed by a re-index (pre-existing, unrelated to `BM25Index`, harmless in practice -- see Phase 2.1's "Current Implementation State" note above).
- **RESOLVED by the "Backend hardening" milestone** (see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history: `VectorIndex` re-indexing used to accumulate duplicate entries (Phase 2.2 finding) -- a real, practical semantic-search-quality risk for any repository re-indexed more than once, growing unboundedly the more often it happened. Fixed via new `VectorIndex.remove_repo(repo_id)`, called by `IndexingEngine.index_directory()` before repopulating on a FULL (non-incremental) re-index -- the same treatment Phase 2.1 already gave `BM25Index`. This also fixes `search_type="rrf"` (Phase 2.3), since RRF's candidate generation reuses the same `bm25_index`/`vector_index` as every other search_type. `add`/`add_batch` themselves remain intentionally pure-append with no dedup-on-add (unchanged, still regression-tested) -- the fix is at the engine's full-reindex call site, not the class's low-level contract.
- `sqlite-vec`'s `vec0` table performs exact, not approximate, search at the currently-installed 0.1.9 version (Phase 2.2 finding, disclosed above, formally accepted as a roadmap deviation) -- if the project later actually needs the query-time performance characteristics of a true approximate/graph-based ANN structure at large real corpus sizes (a decision the Phase 2.5 benchmark harness should inform, not a guess made now), `faiss-cpu`'s `IndexHNSWFlat` (confirmed installable and genuinely HNSW-based here) is the nearest-verified path, since `hnswlib` remains blocked on this development machine.
- `search_type="rrf"`'s `final_score`/`relevance_score` is on a different, unclamped numeric scale than `hybrid`/`keyword`/`semantic`'s (Phase 2.3 finding, disclosed above) -- not directly comparable across search_types, by design (not fabricating a rescaling), only within one search_type's own result set.
- `codepulse/cli.py`'s `--type` flag does not yet accept `"rrf"` (Phase 2.3, deliberate, disclosed scope boundary -- §37 item 2.3 names the API specifically).
- No CI system exists anywhere in this repository (Phase 2.4 finding, disclosed above) -- the relevance regression gate only actually runs when `pytest` is run, not automatically on every change, until CI is built under its own future milestone.
- The Phase 2.4 relevance dataset/gate covers only `hybrid` search_type -- a ranking regression specific to `keyword`, `semantic`, or `rrf` alone would not be caught by this gate (their numbers are measured/reported but not enforced).
- **Every measured search p95 latency (Phase 2.5) exceeds its §19 budget** -- real, disclosed, not yet addressed (see Known Issues for exact numbers). Whoever next works on search performance should be aware the gap is real and measured, not assumed.
- Prior to Phase 2.5's fix, **any repository-scoped semantic/hybrid search against a corpus with more than ~4096 embedded chunks (roughly 2000+ symbols) would crash** (`sqlite3.OperationalError`) -- now fixed and regression-tested, but worth remembering this was a real, silent, undiscovered production-affecting bug that shipped in Phase 2.2 (`9fb498a`) and went undetected for two full milestones (2.3, 2.4) before Phase 2.5's benchmark surfaced it. A reminder that small-corpus-only test coverage can hide real at-scale bugs.
- **RESOLVED by Phase 3.2:** a rename/move is now detected as such (git's own `-M` rename detection) and re-keyed rather than delete+recreated for a pure (byte-identical) rename. See the two new risks below for what remains true.
- **A symbol whose `start_line` shifts is still not recognized as identity-stable** by EITHER Phase 3.2 (a rename that also edits content) or Phase 3.3 (an ordinary modified file whose earlier code changed length) -- both milestones deliberately matched via the SAME existing `(file_id, name, start_line)` key rather than building §9's fuller, start_line-independent `(name, type)` matcher. In practice: any edit that changes an earlier symbol's line count in a file causes every symbol below it to lose its id and get fully re-embedded on the next incremental run, even though most of them are textually unchanged.
- **RESOLVED by Phase 3.5**, kept here for history: `CallEdge` rows used to keep referencing a renamed file's OLD path after Phase 3.2, so `GET /v1/symbols/{id}` could show a stale path for edges recorded before a rename. Phase 3.5's local BFS one-hop correction now finds and corrects exactly this case (a renamed symbol's OLD identity is treated as "changed," triggering the same correction as a disappeared symbol) -- see "Current Implementation State" above.
- **A renamed file (pure or edited) is never eligible for Phase 3.3's own skip-re-embedding optimization** (disclosed above) -- every symbol's doc_id embeds the file path, so a rename always requires the full re-chunk/re-embed pipeline regardless of body content.
- **Call-graph completeness across an incremental run depends on an in-memory, non-durable cache** (`self.parse_results`, Phase 3.1, disclosed above) -- mitigated by a fallback guard that forces a full run whenever that cache is cold (e.g. after a restart), but this means an incremental run's own performance benefit is unavailable on the very first `index_directory()` call after every process restart, even if only one file actually changed. This is a correctness safeguard, not a bug, but affects the practical incremental-vs-full ratio in a long-lived-but-frequently-restarted deployment.
- **RESOLVED by the "Backend hardening" milestone** (see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history: `StorageManager.create_tables()`'s `create_all()`-alongside-Alembic behavior caused THREE real incidents (Phase 1.3's `index_jobs`, Phase 3.1's `repository_snapshots`, Phase 3.4's `call_edges` -- the last one requiring more than a `stamp_database` fix, since it blocked a genuine table-rename-with-data-movement). Fixed by gating `create_all()` behind `inspect(engine).has_table("alembic_version")` -- a no-op on any database Alembic has ever touched. Directly regression-tested by reproducing the exact incident shape (migrate partway, leaving a real but incomplete `alembic_version`-bearing DB, then confirm `StorageManager()` no longer creates the missing table).
- **Every `CallEdge` row persisted before Phase 3.4's migration shows `confidence = NULL`** (528 real rows in the actual `codepulse.db` as of this update) -- honest, not a bug, but a caller reading `confidence` from the real database today will see `null` for every edge until that repository is next re-indexed. Not an issue for any row a fresh index run produces from here on.
- **RESOLVED by Phase 4.2**, kept here for history: `CallEdge.confidence` used to not be surfaced by the multi-hop `get_callers`/`get_callees` (only the single-edge `GET /v1/symbols/{id}` response carried it). Now threaded through both methods via `CallGraphBuilder.edge_meta`, surfaced in `IndexingEngine.analyze_impact`'s `callers`/`callees` buckets -- see "Current Implementation State" above.
- **RESOLVED (see the new "`CallEdge` repository-isolation fix" section above), kept here for history:** `GET /v1/symbols/{id}`'s `called_by`/`calls` used to leak another repository's call-graph data (found by a Phase 3.4 pre-commit audit, confirmed LIVE, fixed with a new `CallEdge.repo_id` column + query filters in both affected endpoints -- see "Current Implementation State" above for the full fix).
- **RESOLVED by the Phase 4.1 compatibility correction**, kept here for history: `GET /v1/symbols/{id}`'s `called_by`/`calls` could silently include Phase 4.1's new `relationship_type="inherits"` rows, mislabeled as ordinary calls (found by Phase 4.2's own read-only audit, not a user report). Fixed with an explicit `relationship_type == "calls"` filter -- see "Current Implementation State" above.
- **Pre-Phase-4.2 `get_callers`/`get_callees` result ordering was not guaranteed deterministic across different process invocations** (a real gap Phase 4.2's own audit found: plain Python `set` iteration order depends on per-process hash randomization) -- **RESOLVED by Phase 4.2**'s deterministic sort (`confidence`, `call_count` descending, `file`, `symbol`), verified directly via a cross-process test with different `PYTHONHASHSEED` values. Worth remembering as a class of bug (hash-randomization-dependent ordering silently violating a determinism invariant) that could recur elsewhere in this codebase if a `set`'s iteration order is ever relied on for output ordering without an explicit sort.
- **Impact analysis (`IndexingEngine.analyze_impact`, Phase 4.2) is calls-only by explicit, approved decision** -- a base class's behavior change is never reflected in its subclasses' impact analysis, even though that is a real, practical form of impact. Flagged, not silently decided; revisit only with an explicit future instruction to build inheritance-aware impact analysis as its own scoped capability.
- **RESOLVED by the "Module-clustering query surface" milestone** (see "Current Implementation State" above), kept here for history: `ModuleGraphBuilder`/`IndexingEngine.analyze_module_structure` used to have no query surface at all. Now exposed via `GET /v1/repositories/{repo_id}/modules`, `codepulse modules`, and the MCP `module_structure` tool -- all thin wrappers, no new clustering/ranking logic.
- **Plain connected components was empirically tried and rejected for module clustering (Phase 4.3)** -- it produced one 27-file component plus one singleton on CodePulse's own repository, not "coherent module clusters." Whoever later revisits clustering should not re-litigate this without re-running the same measurement this milestone already ran (`tests/test_module_graph.py::test_codepulse_own_repository_produces_coherent_non_trivial_clusters` keeps this proof alive as a regression test).
- **§11's literal "many inbound, few outbound = entry point" heuristic reads backwards from conventional usage** (Phase 4.3, disclosed above) -- implemented exactly as written, not silently corrected. A future caller surfacing this as a human-facing label should be aware `config.py`/`models.py`-shaped files, not `cli.py`/`app.py`-shaped ones, are what this literal heuristic calls "entry points."
- This closing line used to say "Whoever starts Phase 4.4 should re-verify..." -- stale for a long time (Phase 4.4 finished long ago), corrected as part of a documentation-staleness cleanup pass. The underlying instruction remains permanently true regardless of which phase is current: whoever picks this project back up should re-verify this document against the actual repository per CLAUDE.md §13 before trusting it, always -- this document is a snapshot, not a live view, and staleness like this entire cleanup pass just fixed can and does accumulate silently between updates.

## Context Handoff Notes

**A fresh summary was added here as part of a documentation-staleness cleanup pass -- the detailed historical entries below (Phase 4.3, Phase 4.2, the `CallEdge` repository-isolation fix, and more further down) are preserved unchanged as accurate history, not deleted; only this section's own opening framing, which had gone stale, is corrected.**

As of this update: the roadmap is complete except for genuinely blocked items (Phase 6.3, 7.1/7.3 -- see the standing notice near the top of this document; 6.2 was built later in the separate `codepulse-vscode` repo). A separate "developer-ready polish" track (not roadmap sprint items) has also completed: a 6-item backend-gap audit, followed by a recheck that found and fixed 3 more issues (two cross-repository leaks, one query-side degradation gap). Every one of those milestones has its own full "Current Implementation State" entry above with real, verified test counts and commit hashes -- this document's `CODEPULSE_PRODUCT_AND_ARCHITECTURE.md` sibling has the reader-friendly product-level explanation of everything built; this file remains the durable, chronological implementation record CLAUDE.md §10 requires. The entries immediately below this note (starting with "Phase 4.3") are the last point at which this "Context Handoff Notes" section was actively maintained per-phase before the project's own documented decision (see the paragraph below, preserved from that time) to keep it intentionally leaner going forward -- read them as history, not as describing current state.

Phase 4 is fully complete and committed through `aaff347` (4.1-4.3) plus this update's Phase 4.4 commit. From here, this document's per-phase entries will stay in the same "Current Implementation State" format established since Phase 4.1, but "Exact Next Action"/"Context Handoff Notes" will stay intentionally leaner per phase (pointing at the relevant "Current Implementation State" entry rather than re-summarizing it) given the number of remaining phases -- this is a documentation-density choice, not a completeness reduction; every phase still gets its own full "Current Implementation State" entry, migration/test/risk disclosure, and commit.

**Phase 4.3 — Deterministic module clustering via import graph:** see the dedicated "Phase 4.3" entry at the top of "Current Implementation State" above for the full architecture, implementation, and verification detail. In brief: preceded by a read-only design audit that, unusually among this project's audits, EMPIRICALLY RAN both algorithms §11 names against CodePulse's own repository before recommending either -- plain connected components produced one giant 27-file component (a measured DoD failure), seeded Louvain produced multiple genuinely coherent clusters, and that measured evidence is what the approval was based on, not a preference. New `codepulse/parser/module_graph.py` (`ModuleGraphBuilder`) builds a file-level import graph by reusing `ImportResolver.resolve_import_path` unmodified, computes raw (unlabeled, unscored) `in_degree`/`out_degree`, and clusters via seeded (`seed=0`, fixed, not user-configurable) Louvain community detection over an undirected view, with NetworkX's own arbitrary community order replaced by an explicit, fully deterministic re-sort. `networkx` added as an explicit `pyproject.toml` dependency (was already present transitively via `torch`). New `IndexingEngine.analyze_module_structure(repo_id)` is the one shared-core integration point -- computed fresh every call, no caching, no persistence, no incremental machinery, repository-scoped by reusing the exact same `self.parse_results` `"{repo_id}::"`-prefix filtering `self.call_graphs[repo_id]` already uses. Deliberately NO REST route, CLI command, or MCP tool this milestone (explicit approved scope, matching B18's own "New graph-analysis module" wording). Files changed this session: `codepulse/indexer/engine.py`, `pyproject.toml` (modified); `codepulse/parser/module_graph.py`, `tests/test_module_graph.py` (new); `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file). No migration, no schema change, no API/CLI/MCP file touched, no changes to `CallGraphBuilder`/inheritance/impact-analysis code. Full suite: 431 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed.

**Phase 4.2 — Impact analysis: direct vs. transitive bucketing:** see the dedicated "Phase 4.2" entry in "Current Implementation State" above for the full architecture, implementation, and verification detail. In brief: preceded by a 14-question read-only design audit that surfaced a real, live Phase-4.1-introduced regression outside its own scope (fixed and committed separately FIRST, per explicit instruction -- see the dedicated compatibility-correction entry); approved decisions are calls-only traversal (inheritance explicitly excluded) and additive response fields (`callers`/`callees` with `direct`/`transitive` buckets) alongside every legacy field unchanged; confidence threaded through `get_callers`/`get_callees` via a new `edge_meta` index (no new traversal, no new vocabulary); a real, previously-undisclosed non-determinism in `get_callers`/`get_callees`' result ordering (Python set-iteration order depends on per-process hash randomization) was found and fixed via an explicit sort, verified with a genuine cross-process test using different `PYTHONHASHSEED` values; a new `IndexingEngine.analyze_impact` shared intelligence-core method replaces graph logic that was previously duplicated independently in both the REST route and the CLI command. Full suite at the time: 418 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed.

**`CallEdge` repository-isolation fix (not a roadmap sprint item):**

- **What was done**: on the user's explicit instruction, fixed the CRITICAL, CONFIRMED cross-repository data leak Phase 3.4's own pre-commit audit found and verified live. Added `CallEdge.repo_id` (nullable, FK to `repositories.id`, indexed) via migration `2cd38eb12a7e`, which also conservatively/honestly backfills existing rows (exactly one matching `files.path` -> backfill; ambiguous or no match -> leave `NULL`, never a guess) -- verified with three distinct synthetic scenarios (unambiguous match, deliberately ambiguous match across two repos sharing a path, and a reference to a since-deleted file) on a disposable copy before touching the real DB, plus a full upgrade/downgrade round-trip. `IndexingEngine._persist_to_db` now sets `repo_id=repo_record.id` on every new `CallEdge` row. Fixed `GET /v1/symbols/{id}`'s `called_by`/`calls` queries to filter by `CallEdge.repo_id == sym.file.repo_id` -- the direct fix for the confirmed leak. Also found and fixed a RELATED BUT DISTINCT bug while doing this: `POST /v1/symbols/{id}/impact-analysis` used `indexing_engine.call_graph` (the "most recently indexed repository" singular alias, Phase 0.6's own disclosed limitation) instead of the already-existing, correctly-scoped `indexing_engine.call_graphs.get(repo_id)` dict -- fixed by switching to it (no schema change needed for this one). Wrote 4 new regression tests in `tests/test_call_edge_confidence.py`, directly reproducing the audit's own two-repository, colliding-symbol-name scenario.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why** (deliberate, disclosed scope boundaries, confirmed by inspection, not assumed): `codepulse/cli.py`'s `cmd_impact` also reads `engine.call_graph` (singular) -- not fixed, because the CLI only ever indexes ONE repository per process invocation, so there is no cross-repository ambiguity there at all. `IndexingEngine.search()`'s `graph_scores` computation for the explicit, documented `repository_id=None` ("search across every repository") case also falls back to `self.call_graph` -- not changed, because that path never claimed per-repository isolation to begin with; it's a ranking-signal accuracy question, not an isolation violation. `CallEdge` rows still accumulate/duplicate on every re-index (unrelated, pre-existing, Phase 3.5's job).
- **What was verified**: all 358 pre-existing tests re-run and pass (one existing test's manually-constructed `CallEdge` row needed an explicit `repo_id` added so it kept testing what it was actually about -- a missing `confidence` value -- rather than colliding with the new scoping behavior; two `tests/test_migrations.py` assertions bumped their hardcoded expected head revision and gained a `call_edges.repo_id` column check); full suite **362 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 358 + 4; real `codepulse.db` migrated to `2cd38eb12a7e`, all 528 `call_edges` rows backfilled to `repo_id=1` (the only repository ever indexed there), zero left `NULL`, every other row count confirmed unchanged. This time the `create_all()`-alongside-Alembic incident did NOT recur -- `codepulse.api.app` was deliberately never imported against the real DB (syntax checked via `py_compile`, real-DB inspection via plain `sqlite3`) -- proving the incident is avoidable through discipline, though that is not a substitute for fixing the actual root cause.
- **Exact files changed this session (uncommitted at time of writing, on top of Phase 1.1-1.7/2.1-2.5/3.1-3.4's already-committed files)**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/storage/db.py` (`CallEdge.repo_id`), `codepulse/indexer/engine.py` (`_persist_to_db` sets `repo_id` on new `CallEdge` rows), `codepulse/api/app.py` (both query/scoping fixes), `tests/test_call_edge_confidence.py` (4 new tests + 1 existing test updated), `tests/test_migrations.py` (2 hardcoded-revision/column updates), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).
  - Added: `alembic/versions/2cd38eb12a7e_add_call_edge_repo_id_isolation_fix.py`.
