**Phase 3.5 — Local BFS incremental graph update:**

- **What was done**: read §37 item 3.5's exact text and §9's "Call graph updates" section verbatim before writing any code. Established that this milestone operates at the DURABLE `CallEdge` persistence layer, not the in-memory `CallGraphBuilder` (which Phase 3.1 already rebuilds cheaply, fully, from cached parse data every run -- unrelated, unchanged). New `IndexingEngine._persist_call_graph_edges`, replacing the prior unconditional "append every edge every run" block: a full run deletes-then-replaces every `CallEdge` row for the repo (now possible at all thanks to the isolation fix's `repo_id` column, mirroring `BM25Index.remove_repo`'s existing pattern); an incremental run deletes and recomputes only rows whose source is in `changed_symbol_keys`, PLUS (the literal "local BFS one hop out") queries for existing rows whose TARGET is in that set, collects their source as `one_hop_keys`, deletes those too, and recomputes both sets fresh from the current in-memory graph. `changed_symbol_keys` is assembled by extending three existing methods (`_purge_stale_entries`, `_rekey_renamed_files`, `_diff_and_purge_file_symbols`) to return the `(file_path, symbol_name)` identities they already computed internally but previously discarded, plus every genuinely new-or-changed symbol from `all_symbols`. Wrote 8 new engine-level integration tests in `tests/test_call_graph_incremental_update.py`, including a direct reproduction of §9's own named scenario for BOTH "renamed" and "deleted."
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, and why** (deliberate, disclosed scope boundaries): the in-memory call graph cache's restart-survivability (Phase 3.1's own best-effort, single-process-lifetime stopgap) -- unrelated, this milestone fixed the durable table's update strategy, not the cache's durability; multi-hop confidence bucketing for `get_callers`/`get_callees` (likely Phase 4.2's territory); the `(file_id, name, start_line)` Symbol natural key itself (Phase 3.2/3.3's own disclosed limitation) -- this milestone's `changed_symbol_keys` correctly follows whatever that key produces, it doesn't change it. No database migration -- `CallEdge.repo_id` already existed.
- **What was verified**: all 362 pre-existing tests re-run unmodified and pass (no pre-existing test required updating -- confirmed via grep that the three methods whose return signature changed have no test callers outside `index_directory` itself); full suite **370 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 362 + 8; real `codepulse.db` untouched throughout (no migration to apply).
- **Exact files changed this session (uncommitted at time of writing, on top of Phase 1.1-1.7/2.1-2.5/3.1-3.4/the isolation fix's already-committed files)**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/indexer/engine.py` (`_persist_call_graph_edges`, the three methods' extended return values, `index_directory`'s `changed_symbol_keys` assembly, several docstring corrections for now-stale "3.5 not yet done" claims), `codepulse/storage/db.py` (`CallEdge`'s docstring updated to describe the resolved accumulation gap), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).
  - Added: `tests/test_call_graph_incremental_update.py` (8 new tests).
  - No other file touched -- confirmed via `git status --short`.
- **Exact tests run**: `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest -q` → 370 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed, 17 warnings, ~370-380s.

**Phase 3.4 — CallEdge.confidence scoring:**

- **What was done**: read §37 item 3.4's exact text, §11's "Call graph confidence" section, §29's Data Model row for `CallEdge`, and §42's risk-register mitigation verbatim before writing any code. Renamed `DependencyModel` -> `CallEdge` (table `dependencies` -> `call_edges`, indexes renamed to match) per §29's explicit target entity name, via migration `f6e481353553` (verified on a disposable copy first: upgrade AND downgrade both correct, all 528 pre-existing rows preserved exactly either way). Implemented the confidence computation in `CallGraphBuilder._build_graph` exactly matching §11's three tiers: `"low"` for a same-file bare-name match, `"high"` for an import-resolved callee CONFIRMED as a real symbol in the resolved target file (using `ImportResolver.symbol_index`, a lookup structure that has existed since this class's earliest version but was never read by anything until now), `"unresolved"` for a true external call or an import that resolved to a file but couldn't confirm the exact symbol in it. Threaded `confidence` through the full pipeline: `CallGraphEdge` (Pydantic model) gained the field, `IndexingEngine._persist_to_db` now persists it onto each `CallEdge` row, and `GET /v1/symbols/{id}` now includes it in every `called_by`/`calls` item, directly satisfying §42's "surfaced to callers, not hidden" mitigation. Wrote 7 new tests (4 in `tests/test_graph.py`, 3 in the new `tests/test_call_edge_confidence.py`).
- **A THIRD occurrence of the `create_all()`-alongside-Alembic incident, this time requiring more than a stamp**: applying the migration to the real `codepulse.db` hit `there is already another table or index with this name: call_edges` -- traced to this session's own `import codepulse.api.app` sanity-check command (whose module-level `storage_manager = StorageManager()` defaults to the real DB), which silently `create_all()`'d an empty `call_edges` table with the new schema. Unlike the two prior incidents (Phase 1.3, Phase 3.1), `stamp_database` alone could not fix this one -- the migration needed to MOVE 528 real rows from `dependencies` into `call_edges`, and the accidental empty table blocked that rename outright. **Remediated**: verified the accidental table was genuinely empty and schema-identical to the migration's own target, backed up the real DB, dropped the accidental table, then ran the real migration normally. Real `codepulse.db` confirmed unchanged in substance before and after.
- **What was explicitly NOT done** (deliberate scope decisions matching neighboring, later roadmap items, disclosed in full above and in Known Issues/Current Risks): extending confidence to the multi-hop `get_callers`/`get_callees` (Phase 4.2's "direct vs. transitive bucketing" territory, not 3.4's single-edge scope); recomputing only the edges touching changed symbols instead of accumulating every run (explicitly roadmap item 3.5's job); correcting a renamed file's stale `CallEdge` rows (Phase 3.2's own disclosed, still-open finding, same reason -- 3.5's job).
- **What was verified**: all 351 pre-existing tests re-run unmodified and pass (two pre-existing `tests/test_migrations.py` assertions required updating their hardcoded expected head revision and the `"dependencies"`->`"call_edges"` expected-table name -- a real, expected, roadmap-approved behavior change per CLAUDE.md §9's own carve-out, not a weakening); full suite **358 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 351 + 7, matching this milestone's own test plan; real `codepulse.db` migrated to `f6e481353553`, all 528 `call_edges` rows (formerly `dependencies`) preserved with `confidence=NULL` (honest, not backfilled), every other row count confirmed unchanged.
- **Exact files changed this session (uncommitted at time of writing, on top of Phase 1.1-1.7/2.1-2.5/3.1-3.3's already-committed files)**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/storage/db.py` (`CallEdge` rename + `confidence` column), `codepulse/models.py` (`CallGraphEdge.confidence`), `codepulse/parser/graph.py` (the confidence computation + `symbol_index` now actually used), `codepulse/indexer/engine.py` (import rename, persist-step update), `codepulse/api/app.py` (import rename, `confidence` in `called_by`/`calls`), `tests/test_graph.py` (4 new tests), `tests/test_migrations.py` (2 hardcoded-revision/table-name updates), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).
  - Added: `alembic/versions/f6e481353553_rename_dependency_to_call_edge_add_confidence.py`, `tests/test_call_edge_confidence.py` (3 new tests).

**Phase 3.3 — Symbol-level body_hash comparison to skip unchanged embeddings:**

- **What was done**: read §37 item 3.3's exact text, §9's "Symbol-level change detection" section, and §8's pipeline diagram lines ("Chunking (only for new/changed symbols, by body_hash comparison)" / "Embedding (only chunks whose body_hash changed)") verbatim before writing any code. Planning surfaced a real, necessary architectural consequence BEFORE implementation began: Phase 3.1's existing `_purge_stale_entries` purges a modified file's lexical/vector doc_ids at FILE granularity, unconditionally, before the parse loop -- if the skip-decision simply left an "unchanged" symbol's doc_id alone while that pre-pass had already wiped it moments earlier, the symbol's search entry would be gone forever with nothing to restore it (a real data-loss regression, caught during planning, never shipped). **Restructured accordingly**: removed MODIFIED files (and a rename's new path) from `_purge_stale_entries`'s pre-parse-loop input entirely (it now purges only genuinely `deleted_relpaths`); added a new `IndexingEngine._diff_and_purge_file_symbols(repo_id, rel_path, new_symbols)`, called per-file INSIDE the parse loop right after that file is parsed, which compares fresh symbols against existing durable rows (matched by the SAME `(file_id, name, start_line)` key `_persist_to_db` already uses, plus a `type` check) and classifies each as unchanged (left completely untouched -- no purge, no re-add, no re-chunk, no re-embed), changed (its stale doc_ids purged now, then re-chunked/re-embedded/re-added normally by the existing pipeline), disappeared (its `SymbolModel`/`Embedding` rows deleted directly and its doc_ids purged -- a real, previously-disclosed Phase 0.4 gap closed as a side effect), or new (untouched, handled as always). Wired the resulting `unchanged_symbol_ids: set[int]` (Python object identity, safe within one `index_directory()` call) into the parse loop (skips `self.chunker.chunk_symbol(sym)` entirely -- no chunks, no embedding call) and the BM25 population loop (skips `self.bm25_index.add_symbol(...)`). Added `"symbols_skipped_unchanged"` to the return dict. Wrote 5 new engine-level integration tests in `tests/test_incremental_indexing.py`.
- **What was explicitly NOT done** (deliberate, disclosed scope boundaries, two of them consciously mirroring the Phase 3.2 precedent rather than re-litigating the same fork): §9's fuller `(name, type)`-based, start_line-independent symbol matcher with a signature-similarity fallback (a symbol whose `start_line` shifts is not recognized as unchanged -- same conservative choice Phase 3.2 made, applied consistently here); the skip optimization for a renamed file (pure or edited) -- a rename's doc_ids always change (they embed the file path) regardless of body content, so this milestone's mechanism deliberately does not touch renamed files at all, leaving Phase 3.1/3.2's existing full-re-embed-on-rename behavior unaffected; the skip optimization during a FULL (non-incremental) re-index -- a full run's unconditional `bm25_index.remove_repo` wipe would make skipping any symbol's BM25 re-add a real correctness bug, so this is enforced by construction (the diff method is only ever called during an incremental run); correcting `DependencyModel` rows' stale references (unrelated to this milestone, §9 assigns it to 3.4/3.5); no database migration (none needed -- pure application logic).
- **What was verified**: all 346 pre-existing tests re-run unmodified and pass (no pre-existing test required updating -- no migration, no schema change); full suite **351 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 346 + 5, matching this milestone's own test plan; real `codepulse.db` confirmed untouched throughout (no migration exists for this milestone to apply).
- **Exact files changed this session (uncommitted at time of writing, on top of Phase 1.1-1.7/2.1-2.5/3.1-3.2's already-committed files)**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/indexer/engine.py` (`_diff_and_purge_file_symbols`, the `_purge_stale_entries` input narrowing, the parse-loop and BM25-loop wiring, `symbols_skipped_unchanged` in the return dict), `tests/test_incremental_indexing.py` (5 new tests), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).
  - No new file was added this milestone -- every change landed in an existing file.
  - No other file touched -- confirmed via `git status --short`.
- **Exact tests run**: `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest -q` → 351 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed, 13 warnings, ~330-340s.
- **What remains**: (historical -- now resolved) committing this work to git was completed as `c185e0b`. Phase 3.4 (CallEdge.confidence scoring) was the next roadmap step at that time; it is now also complete (see the Phase 3.4 section above).

**Phase 3.2 — Rename/move detection + symbol re-keying:**

- **What was done**: performed a full read-only audit BEFORE writing any code, per the user's own explicit instruction -- quoted §37 item 3.2, §9's rename-detection/re-keying text, the Phase 3 DoD's "renamed files retain their symbol/embedding history," and §42's risk-register mitigation verbatim; inspected `SymbolModel`'s `(file_id, name, start_line)` natural key, `FileModel`'s `(repo_id, path)` key, `DependencyModel`'s plain-text path columns (confirmed read live by `GET /v1/symbols/{id}`), and the existing BM25/Vector doc_id format. The audit surfaced a genuine scope fork (file-level re-keying alone vs. building a `(name,type)`-based, start_line-independent symbol matcher for a rename that also edits content) and presented it to the user via AskUserQuestion rather than silently picking a side -- **the user chose file-level re-keying only**. Implementation then: rewrote `codepulse/git_diff.py` to use `git diff -M` (replacing Phase 3.1's own deliberate `--no-renames`) and parse `R<similarity>` status lines into a new `ChangeSet.renamed: list[tuple[str,str]]`, relying entirely on git's own default 50% similarity threshold rather than a custom heuristic (4 new/inverted tests); added `IndexingEngine._rekey_renamed_files` (finds the existing `FileModel` row at the old path, purges its symbols' old-path-encoded search-index doc_ids via a new shared `_doc_ids_for_file` helper factored out of `_purge_stale_entries`, then updates `path` in place -- preserving `file_id` and hence every `Symbol.id`/`Embedding.id`); wired it into `index_directory` before the parse loop, with `IncrementalPlan` gaining a new `renamed` field (requiring every existing `IncrementalPlan(...)` call site to switch from positional to keyword arguments, to avoid silently misbinding the new field into the old positional `reason` slot). **Found and fixed one real ordering bug during wiring, before it shipped**: `_purge_stale_entries`'s unnarrowed input would have deleted the very row `_rekey_renamed_files` just re-keyed (since its `path` already equals the new path by the time purge ran) -- fixed by excluding renamed new-paths from `_purge_stale_entries`'s input. Wrote 6 new engine-level integration tests in `tests/test_incremental_indexing.py`.
- **What was explicitly NOT done** (the resolved scope fork, plus deliberate boundaries matching 3.3-3.5's own separate roadmap items, disclosed in full above and in Known Issues/Current Risks): `(name,type)`-based symbol matching for a rename that also shifts a symbol's `start_line` (explicit user decision); correcting `DependencyModel` rows' stale old-path references (§9 assigns this to 3.4/3.5's "Call graph updates," not 3.2); skip-re-embedding for a byte-identical pure rename (consistent with Phase 3.1's own established "any re-parsed file is fully re-embedded" behavior; true skip-on-no-change is 3.3's job); no database migration (none needed -- pure application logic).
- **What was verified**: all 337 pre-existing tests re-run unmodified and pass (no pre-existing test required updating -- no migration, no schema change); full suite **346 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 337 + 9, matching this milestone's own test plan; real `codepulse.db` confirmed untouched throughout (no migration exists for this milestone to apply).
- **Exact files changed this session (uncommitted at time of writing, on top of Phase 1.1-1.7/2.1-2.5/3.1's already-committed files)**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/git_diff.py` (rewritten for `-M` rename detection), `codepulse/indexer/engine.py` (`_rekey_renamed_files`, `_doc_ids_for_file`, `IncrementalPlan.renamed`, the ordering fix, keyword-arg conversion of all `IncrementalPlan(...)` call sites, `renamed_files` in the return dict), `tests/test_git_diff.py` (1 test inverted + 3 new), `tests/test_incremental_indexing.py` (6 new tests), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).
  - No new file was added this milestone (unlike Phase 3.1) -- every change landed in an existing file.
  - No other file touched -- confirmed via `git status --short`.
- **Exact tests run**: `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest -q` → 346 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed, 13 warnings, ~320-330s.

**Phase 3.1 — Git-diff based incremental indexing:**

- **What was done**: audited §37 item 3.1's exact text (*"Git-diff change detection (added/modified/deleted)"*) plus §9 (Incremental Indexing Design) and §29 (Data Model, `RepositorySnapshot` explicitly listed as required-now) in full, quoted verbatim, before writing any code; built a standalone `codepulse/git_diff.py` (git-diff-based change detection via the system `git` binary, `--no-renames` deliberately used so a rename reports as delete+add rather than silently doing roadmap item 3.2's job) with 14 new unit tests; added `RepositorySnapshotModel` + migration `c286d277ea21` (verified on a disposable DB copy first: upgrade/downgrade both correct, all existing data untouched); added fine-grained deletion to both search indexes (`BM25Index.remove_docs`, `VectorIndex.remove_docs` -- the latter requiring a monotonic-rowid-counter redesign since real deletion breaks the old positional-arithmetic shortcut) with 4 + 5 new unit tests; added two new config settings (`incremental_indexing_enabled`, `incremental_max_changed_file_fraction`); wired all of this into `IndexingEngine.index_directory()` via three new methods (`_plan_incremental_run`, `_purge_stale_entries`, `_record_snapshot`) and a new `IncrementalPlan` dataclass, implementing every one of §9's own fallback-to-full-reindex conditions in order (config-disabled, not-git, no-cached-call-graph-after-restart, no-prior-snapshot, model-changed, same-commit, git-diff-fails, >50%-changed). **During the wiring itself, self-identified and fixed THREE real correctness gaps before considering the milestone done** (not left as known limitations): (1) a modified file's stale doc_id entries would have silently duplicated alongside its freshly re-parsed ones -- fixed via `_purge_stale_entries` applied to modified files before re-parsing, not just deleted files; (2) `self.bm25_index.remove_repo(repo_id)`'s pre-existing unconditional whole-repo wipe (Phase 2.1) would have silently deleted every unchanged file's lexical documents on an incremental run -- fixed by gating it on `not incremental_plan.is_incremental`; (3) `RepositoryModel.file_count`/`symbol_count` would have silently reported only the changed-file-subset's count (a fabricated-metrics violation) -- fixed by computing both from a real `SELECT COUNT(*)` against the DB instead of `len(parsed_files)`/`len(all_symbols)`. Also added a best-effort, disclosed-as-single-process-lifetime call-graph-completeness mechanism (merging `self.parse_results`, now repo-qualified, with each run's freshly parsed files) plus a restart-safety fallback guard, so incremental indexing does not silently regress the in-memory call graph relative to every pre-Phase-3.1 (always-full) run. Wrote 11 new engine-level integration tests in `tests/test_incremental_indexing.py` exercising all of the above together against real, temporary git repositories.
- **What was explicitly NOT done** (deliberate scope decisions matching 3.2-3.5's own separate roadmap items, disclosed in full above and in Known Issues/Current Risks, not oversights): rename/move detection + re-keying (3.2); symbol-level `body_hash` comparison to skip re-embedding an unchanged symbol within a changed file (3.3) -- incremental cost saving is at file granularity only; `CallEdge.confidence` scoring (3.4); local BFS incremental graph update (3.5) -- this milestone's call-graph fix is an explicitly-disclosed stopgap, not 3.5's real, durable fix; §17's full crash-safe read semantics; Dependency-row deduplication (still a pre-existing, unrelated Phase 0.4 gap); `VectorIndex`'s full-reindex repository-replacement idempotency gap (Phase 2.2, unrelated, still open).
- **What was verified**: all 303 pre-existing tests re-run unmodified and pass (two pre-existing `tests/test_migrations.py` tests required updating their hardcoded expected head revision from `de22df2fc3ce` to `c286d277ea21` -- a real, expected, roadmap-approved behavior change per CLAUDE.md §9's own carve-out, not a weakening); full suite **337 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 303 + 34, matching this milestone's own test plan; real `codepulse.db` migrated (via `stamp_database`, after hitting the exact same `create_all()`-alongside-Alembic incident class as Phase 1.3 -- see Known Issues) to revision `c286d277ea21`, all pre-existing row counts confirmed unchanged before and after (`repositories=1, files=18, symbols=128, dependencies=528`, etc.), new `repository_snapshots` table present and empty.
- **Exact files changed this session (uncommitted at time of writing, on top of Phase 1.1-1.7/2.1-2.5's already-committed files)**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/storage/db.py` (`RepositorySnapshotModel`), `codepulse/search/bm25.py` (`remove_docs`), `codepulse/search/vector.py` (`remove_docs` + monotonic-rowid redesign), `codepulse/config.py` (two new settings), `codepulse/indexer/engine.py` (the full incremental-indexing wiring described above), `tests/test_tantivy_lexical_index.py` (4 new tests), `tests/test_sqlite_vec_semantic_index.py` (5 new tests), `tests/test_migrations.py` (2 hardcoded-revision updates), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).
  - Added: `codepulse/git_diff.py`, `alembic/versions/c286d277ea21_add_repository_snapshot_model.py`, `tests/test_git_diff.py` (14 new tests), `tests/test_incremental_indexing.py` (11 new tests).

**Phase 2.5 — Benchmark harness across 3 repo-size tiers; publish measured numbers:**

- **What was done**: read §37/§36/§19/§20's exact relevant text (quoted verbatim); built a reusable, pure benchmark harness (`tests/benchmark/harness.py`: fixture-repo generator with real lexical diversity, percentile computation, latency measurement) deliberately kept OUT of pytest's default collection (no `test_` prefix) since a real multi-tier run takes real minutes and has no business slowing the routine full-suite check, matching §20's own "not every PR -- too slow" framing; ran a small calibration (50/200 files) to measure real throughput (~48 symbols/sec) before committing to tier sizes; ran the full 3-tier benchmark (`tests/benchmark/run_benchmark.py`, standalone script) -- which **crashed partway through the medium tier** with `sqlite3.OperationalError: k value in knn query too large, provided 40000 and the limit is 4096`, a real, previously-undiscovered bug in Phase 2.2's `VectorIndex.search()` (repository-scoped search requests `top_k` = entire corpus size, which sqlite-vec's own `vec0` KNN query hard-caps at 4096); fixed it (`codepulse/search/vector.py`, clamp at `min(top_k, len(doc_ids), 4096)`), added a regression test reproducing the exact crash scenario, then re-ran the full 3-tier benchmark from scratch, which completed cleanly and produced the real, published numbers now in `tests/benchmark/results.json` and CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md's "Current Implementation State" section above.
- **What was explicitly NOT done** (deliberate scope decisions, disclosed in full above and in Known Issues/Current Risks, not oversights): the "large" tier used 25,000 files, not the roadmap's literal ~100K+ (throughput-calibrated, disclosed); no search-latency optimization was attempted despite every tier's p95 exceeding its §19 budget; no CI pipeline was built (none exists in this repo, consistent with Phase 2.4's identical finding); `IndexingEngine.search()`'s own candidate-pool-sizing heuristic was left unchanged -- the fix lives entirely inside `VectorIndex.search()`, the one place that owns the actual SQL call.
- **What was verified**: all 290 pre-existing tests re-run unmodified and pass, including the full pre-existing `tests/test_sqlite_vec_semantic_index.py` suite (proving the `k<=4096` clamp changes behavior only for previously-crashing `top_k` values); full suite **303 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 290 + 13, matching this milestone's own test plan; real `codepulse.db` confirmed unchanged (revision `de22df2fc3ce`, all row counts identical) both before and after (the benchmark uses temporary, disposable databases throughout).
- **Exact files changed this session (uncommitted at time of writing, on top of Phase 1.1-1.7/2.1-2.4's already-committed files)**:
  - Modified: `codepulse/search/vector.py` (the `k<=4096` fix), `tests/test_sqlite_vec_semantic_index.py` (1 new regression test), `CODEPULSE_PROGRESS.md` (this file).
  - Added: `tests/benchmark/__init__.py`, `tests/benchmark/harness.py`, `tests/benchmark/run_benchmark.py`, `tests/benchmark/results.json`, `tests/test_benchmark_harness.py` (12 new unit tests).
  - No other file touched -- confirmed via `git status --short`/`git diff --stat`.
- **Exact tests run**: `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest -q` → 303 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed, 13 warnings, ~250-290s. Plus the standalone `tests/benchmark/run_benchmark.py`, run twice manually (once pre-fix, crashing; once post-fix, completing cleanly) -- not part of the pytest suite.
- **What remains**: **committing this work to git** (the checkpoint commit for Phase 2.5 immediately follows this document update, per this project's established pattern). This closes out Phase 2 in full. Phase 3 (Incremental Intelligence) is the next roadmap step, and must **not** begin without an explicit instruction to do so.

**Phase 2.3 — Wire RRF fusion path to `search_type`:**

- **What was done**: read §36/§37/§45's exact relevant text (quoted verbatim; confirmed no dedicated backlog row exists for 2.3, matching the 1.6/1.7 pattern); grep-confirmed `HybridRanker.fuse_rrf` was completely unreferenced anywhere outside `ranker.py` before this milestone; inspected `fuse()`'s existing metadata-reconstruction logic and `fuse_rrf`'s pre-existing return shape, finding a genuine, necessary contract gap -- `fuse_rrf` returned raw `{"doc_id", "rrf_score", "metadata"}` dicts, incompatible with `IndexingEngine.search()`'s `list[SearchResultItem]` contract every other search_type already relies on; fixed by extracting a shared `HybridRanker._build_result_item` helper (used by both `fuse()`, behavior unchanged/verified byte-for-byte, and the rewritten `fuse_rrf`, now returning `list[SearchResultItem]`); added a new `elif search_type == "rrf":` branch to `IndexingEngine.search()`, pool-sizing/repo-scoping candidates identically to the existing `"hybrid"` branch; widened `SearchRequest.search_type`'s Pydantic pattern to accept `"rrf"`; added a new `rrf_k` config field (default 60, mirroring `bm25_k1`'s exact TOML/env-var pattern), justified directly by §37 item 2.3's own "search_type**/config**" wording.
- **What was explicitly NOT done** (deliberate scope decisions, disclosed in full above and in Known Issues/Current Risks, not oversights): `fuse_rrf`'s signature was NOT extended to accept `graph_scores` -- RRF still combines only lexical + semantic candidates, exactly as it already did; RRF's `final_score` was NOT rescaled/clamped to match `hybrid`'s `[0, 1]` range -- the real, computed RRF score is reported as-is (no fabricated normalization); `codepulse/cli.py`'s `--type` argument was NOT widened to accept `"rrf"` (§37 item 2.3 names the API specifically); `VectorIndex`'s Phase 2.2 idempotency gap was NOT fixed (unrelated to this milestone, still open); no relevance dataset (2.4) or benchmark harness (2.5) work was started; no database migration was created.
- **What was verified**: all pre-existing tests touching `HybridRanker`/`IndexingEngine.search`/`SearchRequest` (`tests/test_ranker_and_chunker.py`'s original `test_hybrid_ranker_fusion`, `tests/test_config.py`, `tests/test_repository_scoped_search.py`'s full hybrid/keyword/semantic isolation suite, `tests/test_api.py`'s original `test_api_search_endpoint`) pass completely unmodified in behavior (only extended with new assertions where noted, never weakened); full suite **276 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** -- exactly 261 + 15, matching this milestone's own test plan; real `codepulse.db` confirmed unchanged (revision `de22df2fc3ce`, all row counts identical) both before and after.

**Phase 2.2 — Embedded (sqlite-vec-backed) ANN vector index:**

- **What was done**: read §36/§37/§45/§7's exact relevant text (quoted verbatim, no separate architecture gate this time -- this milestone was authorized directly, mirroring Phase 1.3-1.5's own "Begin Phase X" -> direct audit-plan-implement-test-document cycle rather than Phase 2.1's dedicated preceding gate); inspected `VectorIndex`'s current implementation and EVERY external call site, including one not previously catalogued in this document -- `codepulse/api/app.py`'s `find_similar_code` endpoint calls `indexing_engine.vector_index.search(...)` directly, bypassing `IndexingEngine.search()` and its `_repo_filter` entirely (a pre-existing, unscoped-by-design characteristic, unchanged by this milestone); inspected `hydrate_repository()`/`hydrate_all()` (Phase 0.5) and confirmed semantic search's restart contract is the OPPOSITE of lexical search's (vector state IS restored automatically from durable SQL on a fresh engine; `BM25Index` deliberately is not) -- this milestone preserves that hydration path unchanged; attempted `pip install hnswlib` (§7's first-named candidate), which failed on this development machine (no prebuilt wheel, no C++ compiler) -- disclosed, not silently worked around; installed and directly introspected the real `sqlite-vec` 0.1.9 binding via round-trip experiments (schema creation, batch insert, KNN query semantics, distance-metric verification, `k`-larger-than-corpus/`k=0`/empty-table/all-zero-query edge cases, cross-thread connection safety) *before* writing any adapter code; rewrote `VectorIndex`'s internals (same class name, module path, and full public interface: constructor, `.dimension`, `.doc_ids`, `.vectors`, `add`, `add_batch`, `search`) to be sqlite-vec-backed.
- **What was explicitly NOT done** (a deliberate scope decision, disclosed in full above and in Known Issues/Current Risks, not an oversight): repository-replacement (delete-by-repo-then-add) idempotency was NOT added to `VectorIndex`, unlike Phase 2.1's `BM25Index.remove_repo` -- §37 item 2.2's literal text is "adapter... behind existing `VectorIndex` call sites," and the old `VectorIndex` never had replace logic either, so this was read as a pre-existing gap outside this milestone's literal scope (CLAUDE.md §12) rather than something to opportunistically fix. `HybridRanker` was not modified; `BM25Index`/lexical search was not touched; no on-disk sqlite-vec persistence was introduced (in-memory only, matching Phase 2.1's own precedent); no physical per-repository index segmentation; no RRF API wiring (2.3), relevance dataset (2.4), or benchmark harness (2.5) work was started; no database migration was created; no latency/performance numbers are claimed anywhere (Phase 2.5's job).

  - Added: `tests/test_sqlite_vec_semantic_index.py` (16 new tests).

**Phase 2.1 — Tantivy-backed embedded lexical index:**

- **What was done**: a dedicated, read-only architecture gate ran first (16-section design report: current-interface documentation, ranking-contract analysis, Tantivy lifecycle/source-of-truth/storage-location/isolation/schema/score-compatibility/idempotency/restart/failure design, dependency-verification plan, test plan), concluding READY FOR PHASE 2.1 IMPLEMENTATION. Implementation then: installed the already-declared `tantivy` dependency directly (the project's own `pip install -e .` is broken by an unrelated pre-existing issue -- disclosed, not fixed); directly introspected the real installed 0.22.2 binding's API via `dir()`/`help()` and small round-trip experiments (schema construction, in-RAM index creation, writer reuse across commits, searcher staleness after commit, JSON metadata round-trip, delete-by-term safety on empty/no-match indexes) *before* writing any adapter code, per explicit instruction not to assume the Python binding matches upstream Rust Tantivy or general prior knowledge; rewrote `BM25Index`'s internals (same class name, module path, and full public interface: constructor, `.k1`/`.b`, `.doc_ids`, `.num_docs`, `add_symbol`, `add_document`, `build`, `search`) to be Tantivy-backed; added one new `remove_repo(repo_id)` method implementing delete-by-repo-then-add-then-commit idempotent replacement; wired exactly one new call (`self.bm25_index.remove_repo(repo_id)`) into `IndexingEngine.index_directory()` immediately before its existing per-symbol lexical-population loop.
- **What was explicitly NOT done** (by design, matching Phase 2.1's exact, narrowly-scoped authorization): `HybridRanker` was not modified; `VectorIndex`/semantic search was not touched; no on-disk Tantivy persistence, index versioning, or corruption-recovery logic was introduced; no physical per-repository index segmentation was introduced; no RRF API wiring (2.3), ANN index (2.2), relevance dataset (2.4), or benchmark harness (2.5) work was started; no database migration was created; no latency/performance numbers are claimed anywhere (Phase 2.5's job).
